# 17 — Decision ledger (the owner's one-pass veto review)

Campaigner is built autonomously: across the pipeline the dispatcher made
default calls on the owner's behalf — everything reversible, everything
recorded here, everything reviewable in one pass. Every decision below is
**Status: active** in the shipped code — re-verified landing by landing against
`main` (the last full pass was the consolidation that landed this line, at
`main` @ `79abe82`: 236 files / 2727 tests passing under the bounded gate
`pnpm exec vitest run --maxWorkers=2`, lint 0 problems, typecheck clean). That
baseline is a DATE STAMP, not a claim about HEAD — it is not re-frozen on every
docs commit, so a stale-looking number means "re-run the gate", not "the ledger
is wrong"; nothing here is verified by a remembered count. The enumeration is
complete: no standing default lives outside this
list. The binding spec text stays in the referenced doc/section — where a
row and its reference disagree, the reference wins.

| 65 | **Fog is opaque and blocks; veil is transparent and clicks through** (owner report, verbatim: “There are no fogged rooms right now. Rooms just have transparent veils.” then “There seems to be another bug here, fog and veil in the battleground interface do the exact same thing (create a veil). So... click through veil, but not fog and repair fog (it should render a non transparent rectangle).”). **This SUPERSEDES the 8fa7abd rule (“veils render at a ~10% tint in BOTH views … not to blind the GM to their own map”, docs/09 M5-D amendment 2026-09-06)** — do not “fix” the opacity back without reading this row. Two GM-only toolbar buttons (`veil-tool` / `fog-tool`) call ONE creator `addVeil(kind)`; before this row the ONLY behavioral read of `kind` in the whole repo was the fill class, `'fog' ? 'bg-zinc-200/10' : 'bg-black/10'` — both 10% — which is why (a) the owner saw no fogged rooms at all (every SEEDED veil is already `kind: 'fog'` — `layout.veilsFromSpawnClusters`, `battleSeed`), and (b) the two tools were indistinguishable. Now `veil.kind` is the ONE switch for BOTH the fill and the behavior: **fog = OPAQUE and blocking** (`bg-zinc-300`, solid/alpha-free, never `opacity-*`, identical in GM and player view; a sub-threshold tap selects the fog and stops there) and **veil = transparent and pass-through** (keeps `bg-black/10`; a sub-threshold tap on its body whose point falls inside a room-key marker’s 44px pad opens that room’s key card instead of merely selecting the veil). Keying off `kind` (NOT a new field) means existing AND newly seeded rows light up with no migration and no Dexie bump; the marker hit pads are resolved by the PURE `domain/battle/veil.markerUnderPoint` over already-computed geometry, never `elementFromPoint` (jsdom does not hit-test, docs/08 §Testing — a DOM hit-test could not be pinned by a test). Markers stay BELOW veils with `z-index: auto` (the 469f058 fix: lifted pads swallowed token/veil pointerdowns) — reachability comes from the veil’s PASS-THROUGH, never from z-index or DOM reordering; the veil body keeps `data-gesture-grab` so drag, resize, select and `delete-veil` are unchanged, and the tap is only the sub-threshold release (`GESTURE_TAP_THRESHOLD_PX`). Coverage/pruning/initiative/rail stay kind-agnostic and byte-unchanged (today’s kind-blind coverage is already fog-shaped). WHY THE EARLIER DECISION WAS OVERTURNED: its reasoning (“not to blind the GM”) did not describe the actual risk — tokens still paint ABOVE the veils by DOM order, and the GM can drag a fog aside, delete it, or lift it with “Reveal next room” — while at ~10% the two kinds rendered identically, which was the reported bug. REVERSIBLE BY ONE TERNARY (the fill) plus the tap branch if the GM objection returns. | `src/domain/battle/veil.ts` (`MARKER_HIT_PAD_PX`, `MarkerGeometry`, `markerUnderPoint`); `src/features/play/battle/BattleSurface.tsx` (toolbar `veil-tool`/`fog-tool`, `VeilView` fill + docstring, `markerGeometry`/`markersRef`, `finishMoveGesture` pass-through); `tests/features/battle-surface.test.tsx` (fog/veil fill + both views, toolbar distinctness, pass-through open / fog blocks / tap-off-pad selects, seeded-fog end-to-end, marker DOM-order extension); `tests/domain/battle-engine.test.ts` (`markerUnderPoint` units: inside, each edge, half-open boundary, first-match, no markers, unmounted frame, non-positive pad); docs/09 M5-D amendment + §M5-D ported rules; docs/05; docs/11 D4 + §Acceptance; docs/18 battle seam rows | `kind` already encoded the seeder’s fog intent, so no new field, no migration, and existing rows light up immediately; a 10% fog made the two tools the same tool (owner-reported); an opaque fog the GM owns, can move and can reveal does not blind anyone, while a DOM hit-test would be an unverifiable claim in jsdom. | active |

| 77 | **A generated mob cover is a VEIL, not a fog — and fog is an animated cloud** (owner reports, verbatim: "The mobs should be covered by a veil, not fog." / "Side note: When clicking on a fog, the delete action is labeled delete veil, please correct." / "Fog: Right now its just a white opaque rectangle. I would like this to be grey-ish and animated, cloudy with some contrast, not just mushy."). `veilsFromSpawnClusters` and `veilsFromRooms` seeded every cover `kind: 'fog'`; they now seed `kind: 'veil'` — hiding is COVERAGE, which is kind-agnostic (`portraitCoveredByVeils`, player-view DOM removal, initiative prune, auto-roll on reveal), so the transparent kind does the job and additionally keeps the map readable, passes a sub-threshold tap through to the room-key marker beneath it, and repairs a real access bug (a keyed room's marker sits at its `mobsRect` centre — inside the seeded cover by construction — and markers paint BELOW veils, so the opaque fog made the room's OWN key untappable). Fog stays the opaque BLOCKING GM-drawn kind (row 65 stands); its fill is now `.battle-fog-cloud` in `index.css`: three layered alpha-free radial gradients combined with `background-blend-mode` only (no `mix-blend-mode`, no alpha channel, no `opacity-*`), drifting on ONE `background-position` animation — no per-frame JS, no timers — `prefers-reduced-motion: reduce` stops the drift and keeps the cloud, and the class sets no `position`/`z-index` (an unlayered rule would beat the board's `absolute` and the markers-below-veils paint order). The veil rail's labels are kind-aware (it resolves the selected RECORD, not just its id): "Delete fog"/"Resize fog n" for a fog, veil for a veil; test ids stay family ids. NO MIGRATION: `kind` is persisted board data, so an already-seeded battle keeps its fog covers (still blocking, now cloud-filled) until re-seeded from its encounter. REVERSIBLE by the seeder literals, the fill class and the label nouns. | `src/domain/encounterMap/layout.ts` (`veilsFromRooms`, `veilsFromSpawnClusters`); `src/index.css` (`.battle-fog-cloud`, `@keyframes battle-fog-drift`, the reduced-motion guard); `src/features/play/battle/BattleSurface.tsx` (`VeilView` fill, `selectedVeil`/`selectedVeilNoun`, delete label, handle aria-labels); `src/features/guide/guideContent.ts`; `tests/features/battle-surface.test.tsx` (cloud layers/animation/reduced-motion + no flat slab, kind-named controls, seeded cover tap-through, seeded fill in both views), `tests/domain/encounterMap.test.ts`, `tests/db/battleSeed.test.tsx`; docs/09 M5-D fog-cloud amendment; docs/11 D4 + §Acceptance | a generated cover over a mob area is plain cover (the fill was never the hiding mechanic); fog is the GM's opaque blank and must stay opaque, so the cloud is built from opaque blended greys rather than any transparency; the marker-pad pass-through is what keeps a covered key reachable (never z-index); one CSS animation per rect keeps a many-rect board cheap; the record type stays ONE kind-discriminated shape — no new field, no migration. | active |

| 39 | **Model escalation is UNCONDITIONAL — the chain is the bound** (owner-directed, verbatim: “ANY ERROR, ANY AT ALL should lead to the fallback”; amends the error-taxonomy gating of row 29): `walkModelChain` advances to the next chain entry on EVERY model failure — 'length' truncation (another model may fit), strict-schema rejections (another model may support strict mode), unknown 400s (the owner's bug report: Meta's “The response was filtered due to the prompt triggering our content management policy.” never matched `FILTER_PATTERN` and died loudly), watchdog stalls, refusals, unexpected throws. Only the model-independent failures stop the walk — `MissingApiKeyError` (fails identically for every model; escalating is pure noise) and user-initiated aborts (AbortError from the caller's signal; escalating would defy the stop; watchdog timeouts abort with `TimeoutError` and escalate). An exhausted chain throws the combined `chainError` (every model tried, in order; last error's kind/status survive). `failureKindOf`/`fallbackReasonFor`/`FILTER_PATTERN` keep their Details-view vocabulary untouched — annotation only, never a gate; single-entry chains (no fallback configured) keep the pre-feature original-error surface; the vision guard (text-only fallback for image-input requests) still truncates the chain. The image transport should follow this contract when its arc lands. | `src/llm/modelFallback.ts` (`walkModelChain`, `isModelIndependentFailure`); `src/llm/openrouterErrors.ts` (`FallbackReason` + 'other', classifier docs); `src/llm/failureKind.ts`; `src/llm/runEngine.ts` (`escalationNotice` 'failed' wording); docs/04 §OpenRouter client | The owner's image-model runs died on content filtering without ever trying the fallback he had configured — a taxonomy gate fought the actual failure mode; "there is really nothing hard about that." | active |
| 73 | **Every generated scene is a labeled SCENE BLOCK — the full per-scene field set, with the anti-formula rule in the SAME prompt** (owner decision). **The finding:** the best-evidenced GM-usability result in this arc's research (multi-source practitioner consensus, not one blog): a scene has to arrive at the table as a block the GM can run from the page — what it is for, where it is, what he reads aloud, who is present and what they want, the conflict already running, what changed, the likely reactions, movable secrets, leads, and a graded outcome — rather than prose the GM must re-read and re-organize mid-session. **Owner chose the full field set** over a prose-only instruction. The block is written INSIDE the part's markdown (heading + ENCOUNTER/EVENT tag, Where, First impression, Who is here and what they want right now, The situation, What changed, If the party acts, Secrets, Leads, Outcome) — therefore NO schema, NO Dexie version and NO migration, per the owner's testing-phase stance; the ONE parts-document format, the canvas split-save, the byte-exact durable version snapshots, the reader's markdown rendering and the encounter floor's counting all keep working on the same text (round-trip pinned end to end, `tests/features/scene-block-document.test.ts`). The scene's HEADING carries its `[[wiki-link]]` on purpose: the floor counts canonical `[[encounter]]` LINKS in the part text, so a scene named only in passing prose would be invisible to the gate — that serves the EXISTING counter and changes neither it nor the floor (schema, resolver, per-part shares, message and goldens untouched). The ENCOUNTER/EVENT tag is TEXT for now: maps, monsters and rosters are still decided by the entity's recorded `kind === 'encounter'` in the artifact pipeline, so an EVENT can never produce a map or monster targets and an ENCOUNTER still does. **TWO things the owner REJECTED, recorded so they are not re-proposed:** (a) a "generate 4 candidate directions and pick one" slate — owner, verbatim: *"The story is seeded by a human idea already and can be further refined, we already have the tools for that. + modules already have drafts with approval. Thats enough."*; (b) code-checked cardinality gates (>=3 routes per conclusion, >=2 factions, and the like) — owner, verbatim: *"I dont want this to become formulaic. I fear that if we prompt this creativity gets lost."* **ANTI-FORMULA RULE (binding requirement of the format, not a nicety):** the field set is a GM-facing SCAFFOLD, never a fill-in template, and the prompt says so in the SAME instruction block immediately next to the field list (test-pinned verbatim via `PART_SCENE_VARIATION_DEMANDS`): the block ORDERS information and is not a form; scenes must differ from each other in length, shape and voice; a field may be one short line when the scene is small and padding a field to look complete is named as the failure; no symmetric structure across scenes and no repeated beat pattern (arrive → talk → fight) across the part or the module; a scene with nothing at stake is rewritten or deleted, never filled with prose to satisfy its labels — and the fields never dictate what happens. Part-level rules ride the same block: no scene may require one specific party action to proceed (a passive party just means the relevant faction advances its own plan), the text addresses the GM and never the players (what a player character does, says, thinks or feels is never authored), at most one new entity per scene used in the scene that introduces it, and the part ends with at least two threads pointing into other parts. **BOUNDARY (unchanged and binding):** only the encounter FLOOR is machine-checked, at exactly the numbers it had before; the block adds NO new runtime gate, NO check over prose, and NO ratio demand of any kind (a research pass confirmed no source recommends a content ratio, and a per-part quota would be fabricated — the same class of error as the retired mix gate, docs/17 row 72). | 80 | **"Generate everything" in the entities sidebar: one sweep, two targets, and the intent is not a precondition** (owner request, verbatim: **"In the entities sidebar i would like to have a button 'generate everything' that just fills all generation gaps. All entity details, all images, encounters, maps in encounters... everything thats missing. Same way as its triggered in module generation."**). **The dead end this closes.** The only bulk fill was the canvas's "Resume automatic module creation" (row 74), and it is bound to the `automationIntent` RECORDED on the module row at creation: `resumeModuleAutomation` REFUSES outright when `module.automationIntent === null` — every module created before that field existed (row 71) — and otherwise fills only the kinds that creation was asked to automate. Its refusal message literally pointed the owner at the entity panel's per-kind batch buttons, i.e. one button at a time. The owner's modules therefore had gaps no control could ever fill. **The mechanism: a parameterized target, never a second sweep.** `runModulePostGeneration(moduleId, campaign, target?)` takes an OPTIONAL explicit target of exactly the row's automation shape (the same `ModuleAutomationIntent` / zod shape, `src/domain/module.ts`) that DEFAULTS to the row's own four fields, so the creation-time call site and the resume are byte-identical; the new control passes `FULL_AUTOMATION_TARGET` (every `ENTITY_KINDS` entry in BOTH lists, `autoGenerateBattlemaps` + `autoGenerateMobImages` true — derived from the enum, never a hand-kept list). `deriveAutomationDeviation(module, artifacts, target?)` is parameterized the same way, so the sidebar's confirmation and its "is there anything to do" decision describe EXACTLY the work the sweep would run; the existing detectors (`batchTargets`, `imageTargets`, `encountersNeedingMaps`, `encountersNeedingMobPortraits`, `unclassifiedModuleNames`, the closed-normalization gate) and their definitions of "missing" are untouched — ADDITIVE ONLY, nothing existing is ever re-generated, re-detailed or overwritten. `resumeModuleAutomation` becomes a thin caller of the shared internal pipeline (`runResume`), which runs the SAME units in the SAME order for both targets — the incremental name-classification pass (a name the text picked up with no recorded kind is invisible to every batch), then the name-normalization pass when `entityNamesNormalized` is false, then the sweep — with the same stop-epoch guard (a Stop all ends either control at its next unit; the sweep keeps its own entry capture). **Which preconditions apply depends on where the target came from, and each difference is stated where it is decided:** with no explicit target the legacy-row refusal and the `automationIntentDrift` refusal both apply exactly as before; with an explicit target NEITHER does — the legacy refusal is the dead end this control exists to close, and drift guards the OLD design (the confirmation described the recorded intent while the sweep read the row's fields), so once the sweep is handed the target itself there is nothing to drift from. The `status === 'ready'` gate is NOT parameterized: a module whose parts pass did not finish has nothing to automate, whichever target is asked for, and it refuses LOUDLY naming the text path. **The row is never mutated:** the automation fields and `automationIntent` stay the persisted record of what the owner asked creation to automate (row 71) — a temporary write would corrupt that meaning, so a test pins them unchanged after a full-target run. **The control is DERIVED, never stored and never permanently disabled:** it appears only while the deviation against the full target is non-empty and shows the work count; with nothing missing its place holds a passive "Nothing missing" statement (the permanently-disabled-control defect cured in the canvas Save arc is not repeated here); while a generation is in flight it is disabled with the honest REASON in `title` (the `saveBlockedReason`/`derivedActionBlockedReason` house convention); confirming lists `deviationLines`' own wording (the sidebar and the canvas describe the same work the same way) and a finished run is reported with a toast. **The boundary, and the one gap it cannot fill (finding, reported):** this fills ARTIFACT gaps derived from text that ALREADY EXISTS — it never rewrites the module text, adds a scene or fabricates a fight. A **fight the prose stages without an `[[encounter]]` wiki-link of its own, or with no `encounter` RECORD, is undetectable by every derived detector in this repo** — the encounter floor (`countModuleEncounters`) counts only recorded canonical `[[encounter]]` links, and the parts pass creates no encounter artifacts at all (encounters come from the encounter persona through the entity batch). That case is named in the confirmation and in docs/05 with its remedy (the canvas's "Fix module problems", a scoped text rewrite) rather than silently omitted; detecting it would need a prose classifier over the spine and scene tags, which the owner already rejected (row 75: the tag stays prose, the spine stays prose; no runtime quality gate may gate generation). **Do NOT touch the canvas:** the intent-bound control stays exactly where it is and keeps its meaning — the two controls answer two different questions. The seam is REVERSIBLE: delete the `FULL_AUTOMATION_TARGET` export and `resumeEverything`, and `runModulePostGeneration`/`deriveAutomationDeviation` fall back to their pre-row behavior with no row migration and no data loss. | `src/features/modules/post-generation.ts` (`FULL_AUTOMATION_TARGET`, the optional `target` parameter); `src/features/modules/automation-deviation.ts` (the optional `target` parameter + the doc comments on why drift is not consulted for an explicit target); `src/features/modules/resume-automation.ts` (`runResume`, `resumeEverything`, the thin `resumeModuleAutomation`); `src/features/modules/entity-panel.tsx` (the toolbar control, its passive state, `generateAllBlockedReason`, `useModuleQueuesBusy`, the confirmation dialog); `tests/features/generate-everything.test.tsx` (18); docs/05 §Screen: Module reader; docs/18 §2.2/§2.3 | The owner's modules had gaps and the only bulk fill refused to touch them: making the recorded intent a PRECONDITION of the sweep was the bug, and re-using the one sweep with a caller-supplied target closes it without a second pipeline, without redefining "missing" and without inventing intent for legacy rows (which row 71 forbids) — nothing is inferred, the target is stated. | active |

Renaming such a row is the same defect one step earlier: an invented NPC's name would become the linked creature's. | active |

| 82 | **A bestiary creature row is not a DETAILED entity — the module entity view asks for an authored entity of its own** (owner report, verbatim: *"In the module there is a named zombie, and somehow this got an entity with JUST the zombie picture, the global one, and nothing else. That is the owner's *"no 'generate x npcs' catches it"*, exactly. **The mechanism: ONE verdict, read by both seams.** The question becomes *"does this name have an authored, DETAILED entity of its own?"* — `features/modules/detailed-entity.ts` (`detailedEntityVerdict` / `hasDetailedEntity`), computed over the resolution's own winning-tier candidates (the module-context answer: module tier beats the campaign, globals last) with the creature-row half being `isMobArtifact` — **never a second interpretation of "creature row"** (row 81's rule). A name whose only row is a creature row is therefore NOT detailed: the panel offers it ("Generate N npcs"), and generating it lands a **module-owned `npc` of that exact link name** (module-owned from birth via `placementModuleId`, the model's invented name kept as an alias) which the module tier then prefers, so the panel row, the entity card and the reader chips show the module's own entity from then on. **The shared row is untouched** — byte-identical after a generation run for that name (name, aliases, summary, body, `data.monsterChunkId`, `data.statBlock`, cover, images, tags, links, scope and revision count all pinned) — and the batch's row-81 destination guard keeps refusing a run that somehow lands on one. **Said honestly in the UI (the chosen wording, recorded):** the not-detailed row carries a dashed **`bestiary only`** marker whose `title` and screen-reader sentence name the shared row («Zombie») and the remedy — *"…is the campaign's shared bestiary creature row — a rulebook stat block and a portrait, cited by every encounter that uses it, with no authored detail of its own. | 85 | **A bestiary creature row's authored text is read-only, and the editor's own write refuses it — with a constructive exit instead of a dead end** (owner-approved; asked whether hand-typing on such a row should be refused the way the AI action already is, with a constructive exit rather than a dead end, the owner answered verbatim: *"sounds like a good idea about that decision, make it so."*). **The fourth door of the same arc** (rows 81/84 shut the AI action, the entity paths and the refill WRITE): the artifact editor's OWN autosave and form writes still persisted whatever was typed onto the shared row — ONE campaign row per cited rulebook chunk, read by every citing encounter. **What is locked:** the authored inputs — name, aliases, summary, body, `data.appearance`, `data.personality` — render READ-ONLY on such a row, each carrying the honest reason in its `title` with the full reason IN PLACE beside them (ONE copy source: `creature-row-guard`'s shared identity + remedy clauses, the same sentences `creatureRowAiRefusal` is composed from), and that notice says what IS possible instead of only what is not — the portrait, the gallery, tags, relations and scope stay editable, because a shared bestiary portrait is what the row is FOR. **The write seam (chosen, and why):** the editor's single save funnel writes through `creature-row-guard.updateArtifactRefusingCreatureRowAuthored`, which classifies with `isMobArtifact` and throws a `CreatureRowAuthoredWriteError` (carrying the untouched row) BEFORE `artifactRepo.updateArtifact` — so autosave, the form inputs, the blur flush and the unmount flush are all covered by construction, the row is byte-identical with NO revision, the toast is loud, and the draft is put back on the row's real values. It is deliberately NOT inside `db/artifactRepo`: that layer may host neither the copy nor the predicate (an upward `db/**` → `features/**` import AND a second reading of the `monsterChunkId` marker — both forbidden, §5 / row 81). **The repair is not collateral:** the refusal distinguishes reduction from authoring INTRINSICALLY, never by a caller flag — `creatureRowAuthoredWriteFields` treats a change as authorized only when the next value is BLANK (the row's birth state `getOrCreateMobArtifact` creates), so `clearCreatureRowAuthoredContent` writes through the SAME boundary and still clears exactly summary/body/appearance/personality (its revision keeps the cleared text restorable) while any content write is refused. **The constructive exit:** where the row has module context (`artifact.moduleId !== null`) an explicit action creates THIS MODULE'S OWN npc of that name through the EXISTING per-entity chain (`features/modules/entity-detail.generateSingleEntity`, the 1-target batch — no new creation seam) and navigates to the created row; where it has none (a campaign-scoped row — the common case, and the only one the workspace editor can see, since its URL carries no module) the surface says so and names the route (Modules → the module → its entity panel) with a plain, honestly-labelled navigation, and no action pretends to have generated anything. Refusing AT the boundary (not at the input) is what makes the row byte-identical by construction, and a refusal with no way forward is its own dead end, so the exit reuses the generation chain the entity panel already runs rather than inventing a second creation seam. | active |

| 86 | **Module prompts become STYLES: an editable layer, a fixed contract, and one byte-identical default** (owner request, verbatim: **"I do not really like the new module structure. It has its merrits, but its way too formalistic now. The module is just a list of things with the same structure. There is no room to spin a story. What is happening in the module now should be handled differently: The module should tell the story with events and encounters in the mix. Encounters are still important enough to gate them (just because LLMs naturally dont like doing them it seems). But i would like to get rid of the strict structure this has right now."** — and the mechanism the owner proposed in the same message: **"How about we make these prompts (with placeholders) visible and changeable in settings? We could even have several 'styles' and the user could just select one... or author his own, based on another."**). **The diagnosis of row 73, not a reversal of it.** The scene block arrived as a MANDATED form: one field list, in one order, for every scene of every module, with no in-app way to write differently. The owner's own anti-formula rule was the tell — a rule that demands "not a form to fill in" while the form is fixed in code is a rule fighting its own prompt, and a fixed form cannot leave room for a story. The intent behind the request is therefore *"the module prompt is a thing I can shape"*, and row 73 survives inside it: the block is now what the **Classic** style says, with its anti-formula rule riding the same instruction block, and a second built-in (**Story**) writes beats instead. **TWO LAYERS, and the split is the whole design.** A style owns the USER prompt: everything the author writes, with `{{placeholders}}` for the run's values. The CONTRACT layer is the part the app parses, counts or depends on — the spine's JSON reply format, the parts' markdown/no-H1 format, the encounter floor clause, the wiki-link rules, the reply's entity/scene kinds, the GM-address rule, the mechanics clause, the encounter-casting clause, the length target — and it is injected on EVERY composition from values the seam computes (`spineContractValues` / `partsContractValues`). A style says anything around those clauses and cannot remove them: the template validator names each missing one, the composer refuses a template that fails validation (loud, before any model call), and a test asserts each built-in carries every required placeholder. **The template is SECTIONED, and that is a judgement call to report:** one flat template cannot compose two structurally different prompts, so `templateText` is split by the markers `--- SPINE ---` / `--- PARTS ---` (the planner call and the per-part writer call). Text outside the sections is refused by name. **Placeholders are named and loud:** `{{campaign}}`, `{{moduleConcept}}`, `{{levelMin}}`… `{{contract.floor}}`; an unknown token is an ERROR naming it (never rendered literally, never silently emptied), a token used on the wrong surface is an error naming the surface, and a placeholder ALONE on a paragraph disappears when the run has no value for it — exactly how the pre-style builders dropped a null entry from the message array (a placeholder alone on a LINE inside a block leaves an empty line, which is what the pre-style disabled-floor part prompt looks like). Values are substituted WITHOUT re-parsing, so a value that is itself multi-paragraph (the prior-parts context) renders exactly as before. **Built-ins are immutable and shipped in code:** Classic (today's text, verbatim) and Story (narrative beats, no field list, heading+link requirement kept because the encounter floor counts canonical `[[encounter]]` links). The owner's three binding decisions: the style layer is editable, the contract layer is not; the app has a DEFAULT style plus a per-module choice; both halves of the feature land together. **A module RECORDS the style it was written in** — id, name, version and the full template TEXT — on the module row (`domain/module.promptStyle`, additive and optional). That recorded copy is what every later generation of that module composes from: a resume, a repair, a per-part regeneration and the floor-repair rewrite all read the ROW, never the current styles list, so editing a style cannot rewrite a module that already exists and deleting one cannot break it. A module written BEFORE styles has no recorded style and resolves to the immutable Classic as PROVENANCE (`promptStyleForModule` → `source: 'legacy-classic'`), not as a fallback that hides a preference: it was written with that text, and it keeps composing it — pinned by tests over a row put into Dexie with the key ABSENT. **Moving an existing module forward is an explicit, visible act:** when a module's recorded text differs from its style's current text, the canvas shows a bar stating the versions and offering "Adopt vN" behind a confirmation that says what it does and does not do (parts already written keep their text; later parts, retries and repairs use the new text; the module re-records). With no difference the bar renders nothing, including for every legacy module. **Byte identity is the acceptance criterion** (`tests/llm/promptStyles-classic-identity.test.ts`, 16 tests): eleven fixtures captured by RENDERING the pre-refactor builders (the 89e5d71 method, never transcribed) — the default floor, a disabled floor, prior modules with the shared cast, an extra retry instruction, tone bans with a campaign description, part 0, continuity from the previous part, the finale wording, the bare case — must match the composed Classic prompt character for character, plus the legacy row and the recorded-Classic row. **Storage decision (justified, and reversible):** styles live on the SETTINGS ROW (`promptStyles` + `defaultPromptStyleId`), not in a new Dexie table — no version bump and no migration golden to re-derive, the app default is a preference that belongs there anyway (so a style is never a second, differently-failing read), the row already carries a comparable per-feature object (`newModuleDraft`) with its own read carve-out, sizes are a few KB like the rows already there, and backup/export already carries the settings row so authored styles survive an export/import with no `OPTIONAL_TABLES` change. Its one hazard — a settings write clobbering a field it does not own — is closed in `settingsRepo.updateSettings`, which carries a field it does not own forward VERBATIM and fails the write loudly if that blob cannot be read. A styles blob that cannot be read at all is reported on both surfaces (New Module dialog, Settings) with ONE explicit way out ("Discard unreadable styles"); it is never shown as "no styles". **Owner-visible behaviour beyond the editor:** the New Module dialog gains a Writing style select (defaulting to the app default, which is resolved AT CREATION when the user made no choice, so changing the default is not frozen out by an old draft), an id that resolves to nothing throws before a module row exists, and the settings card supports duplicate → edit → save (version bumps only when the TEMPLATE changes), make default, reset to source, delete and preview/validate with the contract lines marked in the composed preview. | `src/domain/promptStyle.ts` (new — vocabulary, schema, validator, composer); `src/llm/promptStyles.ts` (new — contract texts, Classic/Story templates, contract value builders, module recording/resolution); `src/llm/moduleGen.ts` (both prompt builders compose from the template; the inline instruction arrays deleted); `src/domain/module.ts` (additive optional `promptStyle`); `src/domain/settings.ts` + `src/db/settingsRepo.ts` (the styles field and its read carve-out + verbatim carry-forward); `src/db/promptStyleRepo.ts` (new — CRUD with save-time validation and name uniqueness); `src/features/settings/prompt-style-preview.ts` + `prompt-styles-section.tsx` (new — the authoring editor); `src/features/modules/canvas/module-style-bar.tsx` (new — the adopt action); `src/features/modules/new-module-dialog.tsx` (the select); `tests/llm/promptStyles-classic-identity.test.ts` (16), `tests/llm/promptStyles-composition.test.ts` (19), `tests/features/prompt-styles-section.test.tsx` (6), `tests/features/module-style-bar.test.tsx` (5); docs/08 §M4-B-2 + §Editable prompt styles; docs/05 §Settings + §New Module + §Module reader; docs/18 §2.2 | A mandated form with a rule against formula is a form that wins, and the owner could not change one word of the prompt that produced it: the fix is not a different fixed form but a layer the owner owns, with the parts the app PARSES or COUNTS held fixed so a style can never talk the parser or the floor out of its contract. Recording the text on the module is what makes the layer safe to edit — provenance that is also the input, so no later edit can reach backwards into a module that exists. Classic's byte identity is the guarantee that the owner's live campaigns and every pre-existing module keep composing exactly the prompt they were written with. | active |
| 87 | **A THIRD built-in writing style, `Freestyle`: the setting and the technology, a goal, and no structure at all** (owner request, verbatim: **"i see classic and story as options. I would like you to add a 'Freestyle' option where just the setting and the technology is explained and the goal to make this a noteworthy and fun module to play, no actual structure given on top of that. I want to experiment with that. All artefact types and the encounter floor still need to be explained."**). **This is an EXPERIMENT the owner wants to run, not a claim that unstructured prompts are better.** Row 86 built the layer; this row uses it to answer a question only the owner can settle by reading generated parts — what the model writes when nothing is prescribed. **What Freestyle CARRIES.** (1) **The setting**: every context placeholder the other built-ins use (`{{campaign}}`, `{{modulePremise}}`, `{{themes}}`, `{{allParts}}`, `{{partHeading}}`, `{{partSynopsis}}`, `{{partEndCondition}}`, `{{previousPart}}`, `{{ruleExcerpts}}`, `{{glossary}}`, `{{campaignIndex}}`, `{{priorModules}}`, `{{additionalInstruction}}`) — a prompt missing them writes a different module than the planner approved. (2) **The technology**, which is not creative preference and which the owner explicitly asked to keep: the text is what a GM runs a table from; every proper noun written as a `[[wiki-link]]` becomes a real artifact the app builds out, with its own generated details and its own generated images, while an unlinked name stays prose the app can do nothing with and two spellings of one name fork into two artifacts; **all six artifact kinds are explained** in the contract's own vocabulary (`npc` a person or creature the party meets, `location` a place, `event` a non-combat scene, `faction` an organization, `note` anything else, `encounter` a FIGHT) **together with what the app builds per kind** — an encounter gets a battle map, a monster roster and mob-portrait images, an event gets an illustration and nothing else, and every artifact can carry generated images and its own generated details; player characters are not the module's to write and `plotarc` is not an entity kind the module declares; and **the encounter floor**, whose numbers and wording arrive through `{{contract.floor}}` and are never restated, rounded or softened by the template (a style that repeated the numbers could disagree with the gate that counts them `countModuleEncounters`), with the encounter-versus-event line (a negotiation, hazard, puzzle, investigation or chase is an event) stated as the technology the floor depends on. (3) **The goal**: make this a noteworthy and fun module to play. **What it deliberately OMITS, and that IS the style:** no field list, no beat-heading template, no "write the part as …" instruction, and none of the craft-discipline bullets Classic and Story carry — no "two visible approaches", no "end the part with at least two threads", no "every conflict ends with someone worse off", no one-new-entity-per-scene rule, no finale-aware closing demand (the style does not use `{{partEnding}}`), no anti-formula block (there is no form for it to be anti-). **WHERE THE FORM IS STATED, and what that does NOT change:** Classic and Story carry the heading-with-`[[link]]` requirement as a FORMAT rule; Freestyle states the same underlying APP BEHAVIOUR as technology instead ("the app builds an artifact from every linked name and counts your encounters from them, so a fight staged only in passing prose is invisible to the app") and then lets the model choose the form. The counted thing is a wiki-linked name whose RECORDED entity kind is `encounter` (`encounterNamesIn`) — a fight becomes countable by being named, linked and declared with that kind, which is why the spine declares entity kinds and the contract's own link rules ask for `[[Encounter Name]]`. That boundary is PRE-EXISTING for every style, not something Freestyle introduces (docs/05 §Screen: Module reader, docs/18 §4). **The encounter floor itself is IDENTICAL for all three styles** — the same `{{contract.floor}}` clauses, verbatim, carrying the module's own numbers — so Freestyle weakens nothing here, and no claim is recorded that a freestyle part complies with the floor differently. **THE OWNER'S STATED EXPECTATION, in his own words** (his expectation, NOT a measurement by us): *"i do think that freestyle really is where front line models will shine and small models will struggle. Front line models profit from being unconstrained."* Two mechanism points attach to it, and only these: (1) Freestyle is the style where model capability is expected to dominate, because the scaffolding Classic and Story supply — which partly carries a weaker model — is exactly what Freestyle removes; (2) that is why the technology block is LOAD-BEARING rather than cosmetic: a model set loose still has to know the app's artifact model (a fight is an `encounter` with its own battle map, roster and images; anything else is an `event` with an illustration; every artifact is reached through a wiki-link), or the freedom produces prose the app cannot build from. The design principle worth writing down here: **freedom is safe where the technology is explained — the CONTRACT carries what the app must be able to read, the STYLE carries only what the model may choose.** **JUDGEMENT CALL, reported and vetoable:** Freestyle's SPINE section is Classic's **verbatim** — the planner's reply is a JSON contract (`partPlan`), its instruction is already conflict-first rather than formulaic, and the part plan is scaffolding the owner does not read, while the part text is what he is experimenting with; freestyling the planner too is a follow-up he can ask for. **The contract layer is UNTOUCHED for all three styles** — reply format, GM address, wiki-link and canonical-spelling rules, mechanics, encounter casting, length target and the floor clauses are injected exactly as before, no contract VALUE changed, and `src/domain/promptStyle.ts`'s schema gained no field (a new built-in is DATA). Classic's byte identity (`tests/llm/promptStyles-classic-identity.test.ts`) still guards every module the owner already has, and a third built-in changes nothing for a module that recorded Classic or Story, or for a legacy module with no record at all (`promptStyleForModule` → `legacy-classic`). **Reversible:** delete the `freestyle` entry from `BUILTIN_PROMPT_STYLES` (and its section function); modules that already recorded it keep their own copy of the text and keep generating in it. | `src/llm/promptStyles.ts` (`freestylePartsSection()`, the third `BUILTIN_PROMPT_STYLES` entry); `tests/llm/promptStyles-freestyle.test.ts` (13 tests); `tests/features/prompt-style-freestyle-ui.test.tsx` (2 tests — the settings list and the New Module select); `tests/llm/promptStyles-composition.test.ts` (the built-in-roster pin updated to three, the one existing test this arc had to touch, named loudly); docs/08 §Freestyle + §Editable prompt styles; docs/05 §Settings + §New Module dialog; docs/18 §2.2 | The owner cannot learn what a prompt without structure produces by reading a specification of it, and row 86 had already made styles data — so the honest way to run the experiment is a third immutable built-in beside the other two, carrying exactly the three things he named and NONE of the creative prescriptions he wants to see the model do without, while the parts the app parses and counts (the contract layer, the floor and its numbers) stay exactly as they were. **AMENDED the same day, with the owner's own test of it** — his words, verbatim: **"I have new information. I am using a very cheap but new model and its doing fine. Thats why. I like this style better."** So Freestyle was generated with, on a cheap but NEW model, and it performed well. This is the owner's own observation of his own run — NOT a measurement by us: we still have no generation evidence of our own for any style, and nothing here is re-stated as a finding about prompt design. **His earlier expectation above is therefore NOT CONFIRMED**: the expectation was that small models would struggle with Freestyle, and what he actually saw points the other way (a cheap model doing fine). The variable that showed up in his run is how NEW the model is, not what it costs. No document, comment or test from this landing may state or imply that cheap/small models struggle with Freestyle or that Freestyle requires a front-line model. | active |

| 88 | **Freestyle becomes the PRODUCT default module writing style for a fresh app** (owner request, verbatim: **"I tried it out and would like to have freestyle as default for a fresh app now."**). **Why, in the owner's own account (row 87's amendment): he generated with Freestyle, on a cheap but NEW model, it did fine, and he likes the output better.** That is the whole reason, and it is his own observation of his own run, not our measurement. **This is a PRODUCT default, NOT a migration of anyone's data.** Three things move, and only these: (1) the settings FACTORY (`defaultSettings().defaultPromptStyleId`), so a brand-new app's row is born with `'freestyle'`; (2) the zod DEFAULT on the settings field, so a row (or a backup) that does not STORE the field — the pre-styles-arc shape — reads back as `'freestyle'`; (3) the dialog's fallback for the window before the catalog read lands. One constant, `PROMPT_STYLE_FREESTYLE_ID` (`src/domain/promptStyle.ts`), is the ONE spelling of the id: the two defaults and the built-in's own entry use it, so no bare `'freestyle'` literal decides behaviour. **An explicitly STORED `defaultPromptStyleId` is honored VERBATIM — a stored `'classic'` is data, and there is NO migration, NO Dexie version bump and NO upgrade normalization that rewrites it (AGENTS rule 1: a silent rewrite is exactly the bug class this repo forbids).** The owner can flip his own row in Settings in one click. **THE INVARIANTS, unchanged and load-bearing.** (a) A module's own RECORDED style always wins — resume, repair, per-part regeneration and the floor-repair rewrite compose from the row's copy. (b) A module with NO recorded style keeps resolving to Classic for PROVENANCE (`promptStyleForModule` → `source: 'legacy-classic'`) — every module the owner already has was written under Classic, and the new default must not reach backwards into it. (c) The app default is reachable ONLY where no style has been recorded for the module being CREATED (`resolveCreationPromptStyle`, `src/llm/moduleGen.ts`) — the RESOLUTION ORDER is the module's recorded style → and, with nothing recorded, Classic by provenance; the app default is not a rung of that ladder, and consulting it there would silently re-voice every legacy module on its next resume. **How it is pinned, and the revert-proof.** `tests/llm/promptStyles-default-style.test.ts` (10) pins the factory, the field-absent read, an explicitly stored default surviving an unrelated write, a module created with no explicit choice recording Freestyle on its own row, and the provenance block; `tests/features/prompt-style-default-ui.test.tsx` (3) pins the dialog's select on both a fresh app and an explicitly stored non-Freestyle default (plus the loud missing-id surface); `tests/db/settingsRepo.test.ts` gains one line on the fresh-row default. Revert-proof: the shared `promptStyleForModule` was CHANGED to consult the app default and the three provenance suites re-run — 25 tests failed, the whole LEGACY block of Classic's byte-identity suite among them, which is the field failure this pin exists to prevent. Classic's byte-identity suite stays green untouched, and the real gate is 245 files / 2811 tests under `CAMPAIGNER_TEST_WORKERS=2 pnpm exec vitest run`. **The honest consequence, recorded rather than argued:** a fresh app's first module is now written in the unconstrained style, which is exactly what he asked for and what he has already read output from; his existing campaigns are untouched (recorded style wins, no-recorded-style stays Classic), and the setting is one click in Settings to change. **Reversible in one line each:** restore `PROMPT_STYLE_CLASSIC_ID` in the two defaults (`src/domain/settings.ts` — the zod default and `defaultSettings()`), and the dialog fallback with them; a fresh app is Classic again and every stored row is exactly as it was. | `src/domain/promptStyle.ts` (`PROMPT_STYLE_FREESTYLE_ID`); `src/domain/settings.ts` (the zod default + `defaultSettings()`); `src/llm/promptStyles.ts` (the built-in entry spells the constant, not a literal; `promptStyleForModule`'s doc comment states the order and that the app default is NOT reachable from it); `src/llm/moduleGen.ts` (`resolveCreationPromptStyle` documents itself as the ONE place the app default is consulted); `src/features/modules/new-module-dialog.tsx` (the select's fallback); `tests/llm/promptStyles-default-style.test.ts` (10), `tests/features/prompt-style-default-ui.test.tsx` (3), `tests/db/settingsRepo.test.ts` (+1 assertion in an existing test); docs/08 §Editable prompt styles + §Freestyle; docs/05 §Settings + §New Module dialog; docs/18 §2.2 + §4 | The felt problem was not "the default is wrong for everyone" but "the style I like best is not what a new app starts with" — a preference, on the surface where preferences belong. Changing where a preference DEFAULTS is small and reversible; nothing about it should touch the record of what existing modules were written in, which is why the provenance rule and the no-rewrite rule are the substance of this row and the default value is the footnote. | active |

| 89 | **The scene text is the TRUTH about the encounter it stages — everything it ASSERTS is binding, everything it leaves OPEN is free** (owner report, verbatim: **"The encounter prose generator actually did a good job here, and the mob generator was not too bad either. The problem is the disconnect. The prose actually holds truth, but it might not always be sufficient. If the prose is vague then the mob generator can improvise, if its specific like here, it must follow that lead."** — his German module staged two of the missing lumberjacks risen as undead, axes still in their hands, motionless on a narrow boggy footbridge over a knee-deep icy stream, and the roster came back **a sea hag, two ghoul soldiers and two skeletal guards**: five creatures, one swamp hag, nothing of the lumberjacks, their axes or their stillness). **The diagnosis, dispatcher-verified, and it is a MISSING RULE rather than a broken generator.** The scene text *did* reach the encounter prompt: `features/modules/entity-batch.ts:232-241` builds the brief from `surroundingParagraphs(moduleText, target.name)` (cap ~1200 chars) + module premise + party level + fixed cast via `buildEntityBrief` (`features/modules/persona-request.ts:71-91`), and that brief is the `Task:` line of the encounter pipeline (`llm/runEngine.ts:2323`), so the map and stylize steps receive it too. But it arrived framed as *"Where it is mentioned:"* — CONTEXT, not SPECIFICATION — while the encounter designer was told its job is to design one encounter: appropriate difficulty for the party level hint, a concrete monster list with counts, terrain, tactics and treasure, with every monster resolving to a real stat source (a citable bestiary roster name or an inline stat block; name-only monsters are rejected). The roster therefore optimised for level-appropriate, citable, environment-plausible creatures — the swamp gave it the hag, the undead gave it ghouls and skeletons — and **nothing obliged it to agree with the scene text**. The only bridge that existed was the FIXED CAST (`llm/roomBudget.ts:492-542`: every `[[Name]]` in the scene resolving to a drafted npc-kind artifact is pinned "MUST appear, exact names"), and the scene named no participants, so the cast was EMPTY — because the module writer's own contract clause (`PARTS_ENCOUNTER_CASTING`, `llm/promptStyles.ts`) told it that rank-and-file fighters *"stay anonymous and undescribed by name (no names, no counts), so the encounter pipeline casts them."* The contract caused the vague opposition, and the vague opposition unbound the pipeline. **The rule, directional and threshold-free (the owner's approved solution).** Everything the scene ASSERTS is binding on the encounter; everything it leaves OPEN is the generator's to invent. It is deliberately NOT a "is the prose specific?" judgement — a threshold is exactly what an LLM answers inconsistently and then rationalises after the fact — so there is no threshold anywhere in the mechanism. Four pieces carry it, each encounter-only and each with a revert-proof test: (a) the rewritten contract clause tells the writer to **state what the fight IS and where it happens** (the opposition's nature, roughly how many, what they carry, what they are doing; the place, its terrain and the conditions of the fight), that **a stated count is BINDING**, that this is FICTION and not mechanics (no stat lines, no tactics rules, no map — the mechanics slot's boundary restated), that a rank-and-file fighter gets **no personal name**, and that the pipeline owns the CASTING and **must not contradict the fiction** — while **silence is not a constraint**; the "no names, no counts" prohibition that produced the vague opposition is deleted, and the clause stays ONE contract slot rendered once per part in all three styles; (b) `SCENE_AUTHORITY_SECTION` (`src/llm/sceneAuthority.ts`) renders in the encounter pipeline's prompt assembly — the Smith draft prompt next to the `Task:` line for `kind === 'encounter'` ONLY, and the Cartographer brief — because it must reach the owner's EXISTING app and personas are user-editable stored rows (a persona edit never would); (c) `buildEntityBrief` gains an explicit `encounterScene` parameter (set true only by the encounter path in `entity-batch.ts`) so the scene text stops reading as background and becomes "The scene this encounter must stage — whatever it states about the opposition and the place is FIXED, and the roster and the map must match it"; (d) **the loud collision path, mandatory**: both roster contracts (`encounterDraftSchema`, `encounterGeneratorBriefSchema`) gain an ADDITIVE OPTIONAL `substitutions: [{ asserted, used, reason }]` defaulting to empty (absent AND null read as "none declared", so a brief stored before this field parses and renders nothing), the prompt says a stated assertion it cannot honour must be built as a complete inline `statBlock` for exactly the creature described or be recorded there — **never silently swapped** — and the app surfaces it through the EXISTING advisory seam the fixed-cast checks use (`roomBudget.substitutionAdvisories` into `data.budgetAdvisory` + the step notice, at all four encounter finalize seams), so the GM reads "your text says two risen lumberjacks; the roster uses ghouls — here is why"; a present-but-unreadable declaration is a LOUD error, never a dropped one. **The MAP is bound by the same text** (the rule names the roster AND the map): D17's natural-site mode already rebuilds an outdoor map from the brief's own prose and the Cartographer's `terrain`/room fields carry it indoors, so no new map-side mechanism was added. **The byte-identity consequence, stated honestly rather than smuggled:** the contract layer is CODE, not recorded data, so changing a contract VALUE re-renders the parts prompt for EVERY style — including a module that already exists and is resumed (its recorded `templateText` is only the style layer; contract values are injected fresh at composition). That is deliberate and owner-approved: coherence between his prose and the encounter his prose produced beats frozen contract bytes. The shared `statBlockSchema` is deliberately NOT tightened: it is the READ boundary for EVERY stat block in the app, including the editor's blank form (`domain/create.blankStatBlock` parses `level: ''`) and PDF-ingested chunks, whose detection is best-effort by design and whose unparseable levels are a documented sort-last state (`tests/features/spawn-picker.test.tsx` seeds a chunk at level `'high'`) — tightening it would reject two legitimate states to catch one illegitimate one (docs/18 §4). **What is locked (B):** the portrait batch covers EVERY roster participant that can own a portrait, routed by what the row's creature IS rather than by the shape of its `source`: a `rulebook` citation and an `npc-ref` to a CHUNK-BACKED artifact (a mob artifact, `data.monsterChunkId !== undefined`) are the SAME creature kind and share the one bestiary portrait, deduped by artifact — never a second job, never a second cover; an `npc-ref` to an artifact WITHOUT the marker (a materialized inline-statblock monster, or a named NPC standing in the roster) belongs to the invented lane, whose jobs carry NO `chunkId` and can therefore never read or write the global `mobPortraits` cache — the firewall is structural, not a lane label, so *"a special look for a special zombie"* never enters the shared bestiary art and a bestiary creature is never re-illustrated locally because a roster row points at its artifact. a row that already has a cover is left alone). **Consequences carried:** the encounter's post-create `mobPortraits` extra runs BOTH lanes (`runPostCreateExtras`), so a freshly created encounter's materialized monsters are illustrated without a second manual press; | 92 | **An offer the deleter will refuse is a bug — the orphan panel and the orphan sweep share ONE guard predicate, and a refusal is never offered again** (owner report, verbatim: *"I got a button after module generation 'Delete 2 orphans'. when i press it, it gives me 2 lumberjacks to remove. Those 2 lumberjacks are in an encounter, just checked."*). **The root cause:** the panel's "Orphaned (unmentioned)" list tagged rows with its OWN read-time predicate (`deriveModuleOrphans`) and applied exactly ONE guard — the ambiguity shadow. The deleter (`sweepOrphanedArtifacts`) re-derived candidates inside its transaction and applied FIVE, in order: campaign-wide mentions, the ambiguity shadow, a portrait token on any campaign battle board, a frozen seed fighter, deliverable outline artifact nodes, and an encounter roster citation (`npc-ref` `artifactId` / rulebook `mobArtifactId`) on any SURVIVING encounter. So the panel could count and list rows the sweep always refuses. The guard that refused HIS two creatures was correct and stays exactly as strong — deleting roster-cited creatures is data loss — and the panel's count drove the toolbar label, the group list and the confirm copy, so the refusal left the derivation unmoved and the same two rows came back forever. **The fix (presentation, not guard strength):** ONE exported pure predicate `orphanSweep.evaluateOrphanGuards(candidates, input)` decides every candidate's verdict (refusal reason + guard kind, or deletable) plus the module-mention and shadow sets; the sweep calls it with the full bundle it re-lists inside its tx (its decisions and `OrphanSweepOutcome` shape are unchanged), and the panel calls the SAME function with the subset its existing props can see (`panelOrphanGuardInput`: `campaignModules: [module]`, no battles, no deliverables — docs/08 §M4-C binds the hook to `module` + `artifacts`, so no live query and no new props were added). The two guards that ARE derivable — the ambiguity shadow and the encounter roster (the pool holds every campaign encounter) — are therefore applied at read time: **his two lumberjacks are refused before any sweep runs**. The three the props cannot judge (the cross-module half of the mention gate, battle tokens/seed fighters, outline nodes) are closed by RECORDING the refusals a sweep returned in the panel's view state (`orphanOfferView`), so after any sweep the offer is exactly what the deleter decided and a refusal never leaves the same rows offered again. Copy and affordances follow: a kept row stays in the group as **in use — <the sweep's own reason>** with NO trash button; the toolbar count, the confirm title and the confirm list name only rows a sweep will delete, and at zero the destructive control is absent (the rows say why); the confirm states that the sweep re-checks every reference and names whatever is still in use. **Named limitation (not a silent widening):** the first offer after a page load can still name a row one of the three underivable guards will refuse — it is refused once, named with its reason, and then never offered again in that view; the recorded refusals hold for the mounted module and are deliberately NOT cleared when the artifact pool re-fires (a sweep that deletes rows re-fires it, and clearing there would re-open the loop), while the two derivable guards stay live so fixing a roster citation or a duplicate clears immediately. | 93 | **Provenance: the app shows WHICH MODEL wrote each text and each image** (owner request, verbatim: *"I would like you to put a very small id below generated texts (in entity cards and the module), indicating which model wrote this. And a small id below images indicating the image model."*). **The intent, as read:** the owner is experimenting with models and wants to look at a passage or a picture and see which model produced it — nothing more (no per-row audit log, no filter, no report). **THREE OWNER DECISIONS, and they are the whole design:** (1) *text written before this change has no recorded model — show NOTHING, and never invent, guess or backfill an id from the settings of the day*; (2) *a hand-edited text KEEPS the writing model's id — the id answers "which model wrote this", so the owner's edits must not erase that provenance*; (3) *app only — NEVER in exported PDFs or deliverables; player-facing handouts stay clean*. **The finding that shaped the mechanism:** every chat call is resolved TWICE — the model the app ASKS (`resolveChatModel` + the task tier) and the model the server reports it actually SERVED (`chat()`'s `modelUsed`, after `modelFallback.walkModelChain` escalates on ANY error, or after a contract-repair retry runs on the escalation tier). Those differ exactly when the answer matters, so an id derived from settings (or from `opts.model`) is a LIE about the text on screen — the id is therefore recorded at the WRITE SITE from the `modelUsed` the call returned, never looked up later. **What was built:** `writerModel: z.string().default('')` additive on the artifact row (`domain/artifact.artifactBaseShape`, plus the optional `ArtifactPatch.writerModel`), on the module's `spine` and on EACH part (`domain/module.moduleSpineSchema`/`modulePartSchema`) — parse-on-read, no Dexie version bump (the `coverImageId`/`promptStyle` precedent) — and the image half needed NO schema work at all: `storedImageSchema.model` (+`source`) has recorded the image model since M3-A and only lacked a display. The ONE display rule is `domain/provenance.recordedWritingModel` (trim; `''`/null ⇒ NOT RECORDED) and the ONE component is `components/writer-model-id.WriterModelId` (smallest muted mono type, selectable, `aria-label` only, never a placeholder). **Every recording seam** (the complete list, so a future writer can audit coverage): `runEngine.runDraft`'s draft step output records `modelUsed` and `runFinalize` stamps it on the generic create, the encounter create (the brief step's id), the continuity report (the check step's id), the prose-only redesign, and every in-place refill; `materializeMonsterNpc` takes the run's id (filling only an EMPTY id — the run analogue of the hand-edit rule); `db/mobArtifacts.materializeInventedCreatureArtifact` takes the ENCOUNTER row's id (no model call runs there — the stored roster text is copied); `moduleGen.runSpine` records the model of the reply that PARSED (first call, JSON repair, or floor repair); `moduleGen.generatePart`/`partCall` records each part's own id including the too-short repair retry (a `generating`/`failed`/`pending` slot carries the previous id forward so a placeholder never blanks it); `db/moduleRepo.patchModulePartText` treats an OMITTED id as "carry" (the hand-edit rule, enforced at the repo); the canvas writers pass theirs through (`chatController`, `snapshotChat`, and an accepted refine/rewrite proposal via `CanvasPage`). **Every display surface:** the entity card (text id + image id — `peek-writer-model`/`peek-image-model`), the module reader (premise id + per-part id), and the image surfaces (lightbox caption, module cover hero, campaign cover art); `NpcCard`/`EncounterCard` take an explicit `showWriterModel` opt-in that the peek modal sets and the battle table's GM "Open card" dialog does not, so no id leaks onto the table. Deliberately LEFT out (reported, not silently skipped): the workspace artifact editor (an authoring surface, where the owner is the writer), the tiny list-row thumbnail and the 48px in-card portrait (an id would truncate there, and the same image renders full-size elsewhere with it), and battle tokens. (The module canvas was on this list too, and the owner REVERSED that — see the amendment below.) **Two design changes made on the way, both reported:** (a) `canvasRefine.refineModuleText` now returns `{ replacement, modelUsed }` instead of a bare string — the seam genuinely cannot know its serving model, and the alternative (a settings lookup) is the lie this arc exists to prevent, so its consumers and its suites were updated; (b) **a real defect found by the suite:** adding a `.default('')` field to `moduleSpineSchema` silently injected `writerModel` into the SPINE JSON SCHEMA SENT TO THE MODEL (that contract is composed from `moduleSpineSchema.shape`), and because the strict-subset converter marks defaulted fields REQUIRED, the decoder was being forced to emit an id it could only invent — fixed by `.omit({ writerModel: true })` on the emitted contract and pinned in both directions (the spine contract excludes it; the NPC draft contract excludes it), with the rule stated in docs/18 §2.2/§4: provenance is never model INPUT. **Export exclusion (shape chosen: no route, not a flag):** the deliverable/PDF builders (`lib/modulePdf`, `lib/pdfExport`) read explicit fields off rows and pre-rendered strings and never touch `writerModel`; `tests/lib/provenance-export.test.ts` builds the REAL definitions from content that carries provenance, serializes them, and asserts no id (nor the field name) appears — while first asserting the ids ARE on those rows and that `moduleDocumentText` (model context + deliverable source) excludes them, so the negative cannot pass vacuously. No opt-out flag exists and none is needed: a flag would be a second thing to get wrong. **Tests:** `tests/llm/provenance-recording.test.ts` (9 — the FALLBACK-served case is the load-bearing one, plus legacy rows, hand edits, artifact patches, the display rule), `tests/features/provenance-display.test.tsx` (8 — module reader premise/part, entity card text+image, upload shows nothing, the `showWriterModel` opt-in, lightbox + cover hero), `tests/lib/provenance-export.test.ts` (5), plus new pins inside `tests/llm/moduleGen.test.ts` (spine repair-turn id, per-part ids, the contract omission) and `tests/llm/runEngine.test.ts` (the draft contract carries no `writerModel`); suites updated for the deliberate shape changes (`canvasRefine`, `canvasChat`, `module-canvas`). | `src/domain/provenance.ts` (new — the ONE display/recording rule); `src/domain/artifact.ts` (+`writerModel` on the base shape and `ArtifactPatch`), `src/domain/create.ts`, `src/domain/module.ts` (spine + part); `src/llm/runEngine.ts`, `src/llm/moduleGen.ts` (recording + the emitted-contract omission), `src/llm/canvasRefine.ts` (return shape); `src/db/moduleRepo.ts` (carry-on-omit), `src/db/mobArtifacts.ts`; `src/features/modules/partText.ts`, `src/features/modules/canvas/{saveDoc,chatController,snapshotChat,CanvasPage}.tsx`, `src/features/campaign/mob-portrait-queue.ts`; `src/components/writer-model-id.tsx` (new), `src/features/images/use-image-model.ts` (new), `src/features/images/lightbox-image.tsx`, `src/features/modules/peek-modal.tsx`, `src/features/modules/ModuleReaderPage.tsx`, `src/features/play/artifact-cards.tsx`, `src/features/covers/cover-art.tsx`; Everything else follows from that — additive parse-on-read fields (no migration ceremony), one display rule that renders nothing when nothing is recorded, one component so every surface looks the same, an explicit opt-in where a shared card is used on a GM surface, and an exclusion that is structural (no route into a document) rather than a flag. **AMENDMENT — the canvas shows it too (OWNER DECISION, same arc, after he used the app):** his words, verbatim: *"i do want to see who wrote the module text and i dont think i can see that elsewhere. So… please put it below the module text."* He had been told the canvas was deliberately left uncaptioned and he REVERSED that call, so this is an owner decision, not the agent's preference. Built as: a caption under each part's rendered text in the canvas PREVIEW (`canvas-preview-part-model-<planIndex>`), and a footer strip outside the editable area (`canvas-writer-model`, visible in Edit and Preview) naming who wrote the module's text — ONE id when the premise and every part agree (`domain/provenance.moduleWritingSummary`), otherwise the per-scope list including `not recorded` entries, never a bare "various" and never a majority id standing in for a part another model wrote. The premise's own id lives in that strip because the canvas document excludes the premise by design (owner: "without premise"), so the preview has no premise text to caption. **The constraint that decided the placement:** the canvas doc string IS the module text — persisted to the parts and re-sent to models — so an id rendered "below the text" INSIDE it would become model INPUT (the same rule as the emitted-contract defect above). Both captions therefore read the SAVED ROWS (`module.parts[].writerModel` by `planIndex`, plus `module.spine.writerModel`) and render as SIBLINGS of the text; the doc, each part's `markdown` and the assembled document stay byte-identical to what the owner edits. `tests/features/canvas-provenance.test.tsx` pins both halves (ids render; no id in the live doc, the persisted parts or the assembled text). **Also in this follow-up, and the reason it exists:** the first landing shipped behind a VACUOUS gate — the reported `tsc --noEmit` type-checks zero files because the root `tsconfig.json` is `files: []` + references, and the real gate (`pnpm typecheck` = `tsc -b`) then found **36 type errors**, one of them PRODUCTION code: the reader's spine-checkpoint `IntroBlock` call site had dropped the now-required prop, so that branch rendered a premise card with no id. Fixed properly (the call site passes `module.spine.writerModel`; the fixtures state the field; NO schema was weakened), pinned by a new both-branches reader pin, and recorded as a gotcha in docs/18 §4 | active |
| 94 | **The resource bound is the CONFIG DEFAULT, not an instruction to remember** (owner-directed, verbatim: **"Second time something like that happened. Please put a rule up to not use up all resources."**). Real incident, twice: a writer ran the BARE `vitest run` — the flag the agent rules used to prescribe (`--maxWorkers`) does not bind here, because it lands on the root config while both `test.projects` resolve their own inherited value — and the second time the harness restarted mid-turn leaving that unbounded run alive: 8 processes, 7 workers, still burning the box that is shared with the owner's own desktop, until the dispatcher reaped it (the dispatcher's own first kill attempt SIGTERMed its own shell, because the payload matched its own command line). **The fix is structural, because a rule that depends on every future writer remembering an env var is what failed twice**: `vite.config.ts` now defaults `maxWorkers` to `DEFAULT_TEST_WORKERS = 2`, so the bare form — the exact form behind both incidents — cannot exceed two workers whatever anyone forgets; raising it is an explicit, deliberate `CAMPAIGNER_TEST_WORKERS=N` for a run that owns the machine, and an invalid value still fails loudly rather than falling back. `AGENTS.md` §Host hygiene is rewritten to match (rule 3: bounded by default, the env var raises it; new rule 4: an interrupted turn's processes are the DISPATCHER's to reap — audit `pgrep` after any restart or resume, kill by self-excluding pattern and PID, and re-run a killed writer's gate from clean instead of assuming it), and the §Parallel writers gate-budget note no longer teaches the non-binding flag. **AMENDS row 25** (`maxWorkers: 6`). | `vite.config.ts` (`DEFAULT_TEST_WORKERS`, exported `testMaxWorkers`); `AGENTS.md` (§Parallel writers 2, §Host hygiene — now 6 rules); `docs/08-TESTING.md` (the flake-proofing note taught `--maxWorkers 2`); `tests/lib/test-workers.test.ts` (default 2, blank = unset, explicit raise, loud invalid value) | The felt problem was never "six is wrong" but "the box became unusable twice and nothing stopped it" — so the remedy is a bound that holds without cooperation. Six was a defensible measurement taken while the machine was exclusively ours (84.8s → 58.1s wall); on a shared box, two workers cost wall-clock and buy the owner's desktop back. Reversible in one line each: `DEFAULT_TEST_WORKERS` back to 6, or any single run raised with `CAMPAIGNER_TEST_WORKERS`. | active |

The two artifact-identity lookups it needs got the same treatment instead of a second scan: `db/mobArtifacts.mobArtifactIn` / `inventedCreatureArtifactIn` are now the pure cores that `findMobArtifactByChunk` / `findInventedCreatureArtifact` wrap. **The sweep runs both lanes**, additively, in the editor's own order (rulebook, then invented) inside ONE try per encounter — so the existing "one encounter's failure never kills the sweep" aggregation still counts ENCOUNTERS, a dangling `npc-ref` surfaces as that encounter's loud reason, and the completion toast counts what BOTH lanes actually enqueued. The stop-epoch discipline is unchanged (asked before each encounter). **The count is EXACT for every row whose artifacts are in the snapshot** — which is every row the sweep reads (the module-creation pool over `listArtifactsByCampaign`, and a creature row is never a `pc`), and the art state comes from the one `portraitArtOf`, never a second reading.
