| 95 | **Pathfinder 2e ability values are d20 SCORES, stored once and printed per system** (owner report, verbatim: **"there is a small problem with pathfinder attributes in generated mobs. The engine seems to interprete something like +2 for str as a literal 2 (like it would be in dnd i guess). Pathfinder 2 mostly gets rid of the traditional system and just cares for the boni."**). **The diagnosis, verified at `01b85de`:** the app's ratified convention is ONE internal representation — d20-scale ability SCORES (docs/12 §5: PF2e's printed modifiers map as `score = 10 + 2·mod`, "the exact inverse of `abilityModifier`", with the raw mods kept in `extras['Ability modifiers']`) — and the defect sat at the GENERATION boundary, not in the maths. `statBlockSchemaHint` asked for `"abilities": { "str": number, … }` and never stated the convention, so a PF2e-aware model emitted its printed `+2`; the shared read schema's `numericStat` coerced `"+2"` into the number `2` (JS `Number` accepts a sign) and every consumer then read `2` as a SCORE — the stat block printed owner-visible **"2 (−4)"**, and the battle initiative used `abilityModifier(2) = -4` (`db/fighterStats`, `db/battleSeed`). **TWO OWNER DECISIONS, both binding:** (1) *for Pathfinder 2e the stat block shows the BONUS ONLY ("Str +2"); every other system keeps `score (bonus)`*; (2) *mobs already generated keep their stored values — no converter, no backfill, no migration; the fix governs what happens from now on.* **What was built:** the shape hint STATES the convention — scores in EVERY system, PF2e included, the worked example (a PF2e "Str +2" is written 14, `score = 10 + 2 × the printed modifier`) and "an ability value NEVER carries a sign"; `runEngine.statBlockSignedAbilityIssues` refuses a SIGNED ability value as a NAMED issue that TEACHES the conversion ("a +2 modifier is 14", derived through the shared `abilityScoreFromModifier` so the example cannot drift from the maths), riding the EXISTING one-repair-then-loud path at all three places a model authors a stat block (the Smith draft and the Cartographer brief through `encounterSourceIssues`, and the statblock step's own reply), reading the reply's RAW pre-coercion object — the parameter is REQUIRED, never defaulted, so no future caller can silently drop the check — because zod's own coercion is exactly what destroys the sign; `domain/statblock` gains the ONE convention (`abilityScoreFromModifier`, `printsAbilityModifiers`, `formatAbilityValue`) so the pack importer, the shared card, the editor and BOTH PDF stat boxes stop composing it themselves (display: PF2e bonus only, every other system unchanged); the editor's PF2e field edits the printed bonus and states the conversion on screen instead of doing it silently; and the battle initiative stays `abilityModifier(score)` — the score, never the printed modifier it was derived from. **THE HONEST LIMITATION, recorded so nobody "fixes" it with a heuristic:** a model that writes the bare number `2` while MEANING the PF2e modifier is indistinguishable from a creature that genuinely has a score of 2, and guessing would corrupt correct data — no threshold, range or plausibility rule may separate them; the defence for that case is the contract wording plus the worked example. The SIGNED case (what the owner actually hit) IS caught mechanically, because no stat block in any system prints a score with a sign. `domain/statblock.ts`'s `numericStat` and `statBlockSchema` were deliberately NOT tightened: that schema is the READ boundary for stored rows, PDF-ingested chunks and the blank editor form (`level: ''`), which is why the level validation (row 90) sits at the model boundary too. **AMENDS nothing** (docs/12 §5's ability row is clarified, not contradicted: the promise it makes — imported and generated PF2e rows print identically — still holds, now as "both print the bonus only"). | `src/llm/runEngine.ts` (`statBlockSchemaHint`, `statBlockSignedAbilityIssues`, `statblockSignedAbilityIssues`, `rawReplyMonsters`, `encounterSourceIssues`, `runStatblock`); `src/domain/statblock.ts` (`abilityScoreFromModifier`, `printsAbilityModifiers`, `formatAbilityValue`); `src/features/campaign/components/stat-block.tsx` (card + editor); `src/lib/pdfExport.ts`, `src/lib/modulePdf.ts`; tests `tests/llm/runEngine.test.ts`, `tests/llm/encounterRun.test.ts`, `tests/llm/encounterCartographer.test.ts`, `tests/features/stat-block-abilities.test.tsx`, `tests/db/battleRepo.test.ts`, `tests/db/battleSeed.test.tsx`, `tests/lib/pdfExport.test.ts`, `tests/lib/modulePdf.test.ts`; docs/05 §Artifact editor, docs/11 §D5, docs/12 §5, docs/18 §2.2/§4 | The felt problem was never the arithmetic — it was that one number meant two different things depending on who wrote the row, so the same creature printed "2 (−4)" when a model wrote it and "+2" when the pack importer wrote it. Stating the convention where the model is asked, catching the one case that is mechanically provable (a sign no score carries), and making the display say what each system actually prints fixes the owner's report without touching a single stored row, without weakening a read boundary, and without guessing at data that cannot be distinguished. | active |

| 97 | **A test that starts real orchestration SETTLES it before teardown — the post-run-extras gate flake was a pending continuation, not a data bug** (dispatcher report, measured: a full-suite run at `01b85de` printed **253 files / 2891 tests passed** with every individual test green and still exited **1**, because vitest also reported `Errors 1 error` — `NotFoundError: PersonaRun not found: e4deb265-…` from inside the transaction at `src/db/runRepo.ts:48`, attributed to `tests/features/post-run-extras.test.ts`, "latest test that might've caused the error" = `a completed npc run without the statblock extra attaches no notice`; isolated repeats of that one file: **1 of 6** printed the `Errors 1 error` line, the other 5 were clean, all 12 tests passed every time — a timing signature, and it appeared while the box was loaded 17–23 by another session). **Root cause, proved by delaying the cause instead of loading the machine.** The `mobPortraits` test creates a FRESH encounter, so the `post-run-extras` completion listener hands that encounter to the unattended **encounter-map queue**, whose job calls `runEngine.startRun` — a pipeline that is FIRE-AND-FORGET (`startRun` resolves once the row is written) and a queue that waits only for a terminal STATUS. The test asserted the monster's portrait cover and RETURNED with the map job still in flight and its Cartographer run still `running`; MEASURED with a temporary probe at the end of that test: `runs: <mapRunId>:running:other | <smithRunId>:completed:smith`, `map queue: queued=0 active=1`. The next test's `beforeEach` then ran `clearDatabase()` (deleting `db.runs`) and `useEncounterMapQueue.getState().reset()` (aborting the job): the abort reaction's `runEngine.cancel` write hit the deleted row — caught by the queue factory, which classifies the job cancelled, but it left `cancelRequested` set — the still-live pipeline's own catch then wrote `cancelled` to the same vanished row, that NotFoundError escaped `executeFrom`, and the engine's own failure chain `void this.executeFrom(…).catch((error) => void this.fail(runId, error))` called `fail`, whose `updateRun(runId, { status: 'failed' })` rejected **with nothing awaiting it** — vitest's `Errors 1 error`, exit 1, every test green. The chain was pinned by a second probe: `toastError` was called with the bare `PersonaRun not found: <the map run's id>`, the message `fail` toasts before its own write fails, so the unhandled write is `fail`'s and no other. **The fix is at the cause, test-side.** `tests/features/post-run-extras.test.ts` gains ONE module-scope `settleStartedQueues()` — the queues' own `queued`/`active` state, the same seam the app's unattended callers wait on (`waitForRunStatus`) — called from the file's `afterEach` BEFORE the next `clearDatabase()`, plus the pin that `db.runs` holds no `running` row; the file's map-only local `queueSettled()` is folded into it so the mid-test settle points in the automatic-battlemap tests assert the same contract, and no assertion was weakened. **Why not the other route, argued not assumed:** making `runEngine.fail`/`cancel` skip a vanished row, or weakening `runRepo`'s row-must-exist guard, would be a SILENT FALLBACK over a run that vanished mid-pipeline (AGENTS rule 1) — the guard is right and stays byte-identical, and it is exactly what made the pending write loud; the product-side cure for the analogous REAL-APP case (the Runs list lets the owner delete a RUNNING run, so `deleteRun` removes the row the pipeline is writing and the same chain would surface as the global "Unhandled error in a background task" toast, `lib/globalErrors` — deduced from the chain, NOT measured in the app) belongs in `src/llm/runEngine.ts`, which a concurrent arc owns — reported as a finding, deliberately not touched. **Deterministic reproduction by DELAY (the `89e5d71` method), single file, `CAMPAIGNER_TEST_WORKERS=2`, one process at a time, no synthetic load:** HEAD as-is 1/1 green (it does not fire unloaded — which is why repetition alone never found it); with 250ms added to that test's chat replies and NO cure, **RED 2/2** — `Errors 1 error`, exit 1, 12/12 tests green, the same `runRepo.ts:48` NotFoundError; with the SAME delay and the cure, green (exit 0); with the cure and the delay removed, green **5/5** (4.37–4.66s against a 4.08s baseline). Revert-proofs: the `afterEach` settle call removed under the delivered delay reproduces the red gate on demand, and with only the DRAIN removed while the `running`-census pin stays, the pin fails LOUDLY — 3 assertion failures naming the still-running run id — instead of degrading into the file-level unhandled error. **No guard was widened:** nothing added to `ALLOWED_NOISE`, no `process.on('unhandledRejection')` swallow, no blanket `catch` around a DB write, no `--retry`, no weakened `runRepo` guard. | `tests/features/post-run-extras.test.ts` (`settleStartedQueues` + the async `afterEach` + the two mid-test settle points, `db` imported for the census); `docs/08-TESTING.md` §the pending-continuation flake; `docs/18-ARCHITECTURE.md` §4 | The class earns a row because of how it fails, not how often: EVERY test passes and the gate still goes red, so a real regression landing in the same run reads as "just the flake" — it cost the owner an hour of separating noise from defect, and the honest cure has to make the state impossible rather than quiet. The direction follows from the evidence: the continuation belonged to a run the TEST started through a real seam (the completion listener → the map queue) and never settled, and teardown is precisely where the row it is writing disappears — so the test now waits for the state the product actually guarantees, through the same door the app's unattended callers use, instead of racing a clock. Deliberately NOT done: an `ALLOWED_NOISE` entry (widening the strict guard), any rejection swallowing (AGENTS rule 1), and any change to the engine's failure chain, which is a separate decision about a vanished run row and belongs with that file's owner. | active |
| 98 | **A blocked control states its reason PERCEIVABLY — ONE device, because "disabled with its reason in `title`" was a promise the browser never kept** (owner, verbatim: *"there is time to care for that now. Please do. I want this as clean as possible."*, on a dispatcher-flagged item: three canvas header controls that appeared to do nothing). **The corrected diagnosis, verified at the SHA before building anything.** `canvas-preview-toggle`, `canvas-refine-selection` and `canvas-rewrite-part` were never dead: `CanvasPage.togglePreview`, the shared instruction dialog and the whole-doc refine/rewrite turns are implemented, and `tests/features/module-canvas.test.tsx` drives BOTH AI actions end to end through `runInstruction`, asserts their disabled state while the module generates and toggles the preview repeatedly. What the owner actually hit is smaller and sharper, and it IS a real defect: a control that cannot act gave him no perceivable reason. Both AI buttons are `disabled={aiBlocked || previewOpen}` and the canvas OPENS in Preview (session-only default, `openByModule[moduleId] ?? true`), so on first open pressing either does nothing — and **measured with a brace-aware scan of that commit, those three controls stated NO reason anywhere at all**: neither has a `title`, an `aria-describedby` or a hint beside it (`canvas-preview-toggle` likewise). The brief's framing — that the reason lived in a non-rendering `title` — is wrong for the incident itself; the non-rendering `title` is the other half of the problem, and the repo's device for stating why was a `title` ON the disabled control, which Chrome never renders (a natively disabled form control fires no pointer events; measured at HEAD, a disabled shadcn Button renders `<button disabled … class="… disabled:pointer-events-none …">`, so it is not even hit-tested) and which is unreachable by keyboard (a disabled control cannot take focus) and unannounced by AT. Scale measured at that commit (brace-aware scan of every JSX element carrying both `disabled` and `title`): **131** `disabled={...}` sites in `src/**/*.tsx`, of which **12** state a why-not reason — **11** on natively `disabled` controls, whose `title` cannot render in Chrome, and 1 that is the Base UI Checkbox below. **The device chosen, and why.** `src/components/blocked-control.tsx` (`BlockedControl`) — ONE home, applied by every surface: it WRAPS the control so the wrapper is the Tooltip trigger and therefore the hover target (the only box that can receive it), it is focusable (`tabIndex=0`) exactly while blocked so the reason opens on focus for keyboard users, and it renders the same sentence into a visually hidden node the wrapper points at with `aria-describedby` so assistive tech gets it with no popup open. It reuses the EXISTING `components/ui/tooltip` primitive and its working `render={<…/>}` precedent (`campaign-tree.tsx`) rather than inventing a second idiom, and it passes `reason: null` for a live control (inert `inline-flex` span, same DOM shape, so a state flip never remounts or re-focuses the control). **What was deliberately NOT changed:** no blocking condition, and no `aria-disabled` conversion — `module-canvas.test.tsx:744-745` and its siblings pin `toBeDisabled()`, the gates are spec'd, and the job was to make the existing reason perceivable rather than to re-litigate the gating. Every reason names the way out where one exists, using the copy the repo already had (Preview blocking the AI actions says to switch to Edit, exactly like `saveBlockedReason`). **The honest limits of what a jsdom test can prove.** jsdom has no layout and no hit-testing, so the pins prove three things and no more: the control is STILL natively disabled, the reason text is present and associated via `aria-describedby`, and the popup OPENS when the wrapper receives hover/focus (asserted ABSENT before the interaction, so a wrapper that never receives it fails the pin instead of passing on the always-rendered hidden text). That Chrome's hit test actually lands on the wrapper is NOT measured in this repo — it rests on the documented behaviour plus the measured `pointer-events-none`, which together leave the wrapper as the only possible target; the external evidence is equinor/design-system issue 2724, microsoft/fluentui issue 17606, and KendoReact's tooltip-on-a-disabled-button recipe, all cited in docs/18 §4. **8 of the 11 non-rendering reason sites are converted, and every exception is a measured decision rather than an omission.** First, the correction to the brief's premise: a disabled Base UI **Checkbox** (the entity panel's image checkbox) renders `<span role="checkbox" aria-disabled="true" tabindex="-1">` — no native `disabled` attribute — so its `title` DOES render in Chrome and its `aria-label` already carries the same reason for AT; wrapping it would have added a duplicate description plus a redundant tab stop, so it stays as it is with the measurement recorded in `entity-panel.tsx`. Two further non-rendering sites keep a VISIBLE in-place paragraph as the statement instead (`artifact-editor`'s creature-row AI refusal, the persona panel's refused refill) — where the copy is long, text beside the control beats a tooltip, and the `title` stays a mirror; the third exception is `creature-row-create-own-npc`, whose own label reads "Creating…" in exactly the state its `title` describes. The brief's "10 sites" was approximate: the honest count is 12 reason-stating sites, and the classification of each is in the landing report. **Reported, not chased:** the other ~121 sites were audited and grouped in the landing report (self-evident by rule vs. blocked with no reason at all). The silent-block group — Writers'-room's eight `busy` controls, the Rules import/embed menu items, `spine-checkpoint`'s busy buttons, `stub-popover`/`mob-portraits`/export/image-upload/board-rewrite controls — is named there as its own follow-up slice. | `src/components/blocked-control.tsx` (the device); applied in `src/features/modules/canvas/CanvasPage.tsx` + `ChatSidebar.tsx`, `src/features/modules/entity-panel.tsx`, `src/features/campaign/components/artifact-editor.tsx`; pins `tests/features/blocked-control.test.tsx`, `tests/features/blocked-reasons.test.tsx`, plus the converted-site pins in `tests/features/generate-everything.test.tsx`, `tests/features/entity-classify-new.test.tsx`, `tests/images-ui.test.tsx`; docs/05 §Why a control cannot act + the four amended `title` claims; docs/18 §2.3 seam row + §4 gotcha | The felt problem is the owner's, and it is not cosmetic: a greyed control with no statement reads as broken, and the same `title` recipe was written into docs/05 in five places across four sections, so the repo was promising a reason it could not deliver. The direction follows the evidence — the reason already existed in every one of these states (as a `title`, or in the gate's own flags), so the fix is a perceivable carrier, not new state, new gating or new copy for its own sake; and the device is ONE component rather than a per-screen workaround because the check is mechanical ("can this control be disabled?") and the answer must be uniform. Rejected: `aria-disabled` instead of `disabled` (changes interaction semantics and breaks the pinned gates), a bespoke per-surface tooltip, and a device that only adds `aria-describedby` (invisible to a mouse user, which is the half the owner asked for). | active |
