Each of these five is a reason for a run that is genuinely holding the control, so none of the sentences is false; the gates themselves are exactly as they were. **One gate whose state cannot occur from its only production caller, measured and reported rather than "fixed":** `spine-checkpoint`'s `busy` is passed `true` by nobody — its single caller renders that branch only when the part list is empty AND not busy — so its four reasons are correct and currently unreachable; a reason was written for the state the gate encodes (the canvas's own sentence for the same state, so one state is never explained two ways) and the reachability is recorded in the component's comment, in docs/05 and here, instead of inventing a caller. **Self-evident states get NO wrapper, and every one of those judgements is a PINNED decision rather than an omission:** the Writers'-room's first-step Move up, last-step Move down and empty-plan Run chain; the spine checkpoint's at-an-end move buttons; the module board's Apply/Discard while the apply is in flight (Apply's own label reads "Applying…" in exactly that state, beside the control it explains); the bestiary full-list switch while the listing runs (its own checked state plus the line directly below: "Listing every pack in the repo…"); the mob-portrait section's empty roster (the copy beside the button names it: "No creatures to illustrate — add roster entries first."); the export dialog's zero selection (the label itself reads "Export 0 artifact(s)"); the embedding panel's embeddings-off rung (the "inactive" badge plus the notice paragraph) and its nothing-to-embed rung (the stats line's own "0 of 0 chunks embedded"); the dice roller's empty tray (nothing to roll); the stub popover's empty name (nothing to create) and its "Generating…" label. **The honest limit of what a jsdom pin proves — the row-98 limit, restated for this sweep.** A jsdom pin proves the DOM contract (the control is held exactly as its gate says, the sentence is present and `sr-only`, `aria-describedby` points at it, the wrapper is focusable, and the popup is ABSENT before the interaction and present after hover) and it does NOT prove that Chrome paints the tooltip or that hit-testing lands on the wrapper. One further limit specific to this sweep, measured and encoded in the helpers: "held" is not always the native attribute — a Base UI **menu item** renders as `div[role="menuitem"][aria-disabled="true"][data-disabled]` and a Base UI **Switch** as `span[role="switch"][aria-disabled="true"]`, so `toBeDisabled()` (which reads the native attribute off form tags only) is the wrong question there and a pin using it would be vacuous; `tests/helpers/blocked-reason.ts` therefore carries one helper per held form (`expectBlockedReason`, `expectBlockedReasonMenuItem`) and one per self-evident form (`expectSelfEvidentBlock` with its native/aria-disabled variant, `expectSelfEvidentElement` for a control that is out of scope). **The proofs, MEASURED, one control at a time (never en masse, so a failure names its own control):** 31 revert-proofs — the reason expression at that one site replaced by `null`, the NAMED pin required to FAIL (each one did, every failure naming `[data-testid="<control>-reason"]`), then the file restored and verified byte-identical by `md5`; 31/31 load-bearing, 0 surviving pins. 19 injections: 4 wrong-sentence injections (the pin fails on the EXACT text, so a pin cannot pass on "some reason exists"), 11 forbidden-wrapper injections at a control an self-evident pin calls bare (the pin fails), and 4 gate relaxations for the three controls that carry no wrapper at all in the source (the pin's held half fails). Both scripts were scratch under the worktree and are gone; the runs are the record. Counts: 30 reason-bearing controls wrapped, 3 new test files (a writers-room file, an entity-sweep file, the shared helper), 9 existing test files extended, 23 new pins plus 2 existing pins extended in place. **Docs amended in the same commit:** docs/05 §per-surface (the Writers' room, the Rules library + the embedding panel, Bestiary packs, the artifacts' Images/Battlemap uploads, encounter mob portraits, the export dialog, the module board, the dice roller, the spine checkpoint, the stub popover); docs/08 coverage rows (the sweep, the revert-proofs/injections, the self-evident inventory, the helper); docs/18 §4 (the new gotcha: a blocked control raises three questions — is the block intended, can the state occur, and what does the held state LOOK like in the DOM); this row. Rows 95–98 are untouched. | 11 source files (writers-room, spine-checkpoint, RulesPage, stub-popover, bestiary-fetch-section, mob-portraits-section, images-section, export-dialog, embedding-panel, boardNodes, DiceRoller); `tests/helpers/blocked-reason.ts` (new — the pin helpers and their documented jsdom limit); `tests/features/blocked-reasons-writers-room.test.tsx` (new), `tests/features/blocked-reasons-entity-sweep.test.tsx` (new), and the extended pins in `spine-checkpoint.test.tsx`, `module-board-rewrite.test.tsx`, `rules-page.test.tsx`, `rules/embedding-panel.test.tsx`, `bestiary-fetch-section.test.tsx`, `mob-portraits-section.test.tsx`, `dice-roller.test.tsx`; docs/05, docs/08, docs/18, docs/17 (this row) | Row 98 built the device; what it could not do in one arc was audit the other ~121 disabled sites, and this is the half the owner's "as clean as possible" actually bites on: a control that is dead with no statement reads as broken, and 30 of them were exactly that. The direction follows the evidence rather than taste — the reason already existed in every one of these states (it is the gate's own flag), so the work is a perceivable carrier plus copy that is TRUE for the state, not new state or new gating; and "do not change WHEN a control is blocked" is honoured literally (measured three ways) precisely because the brief that asked for reasons must not become a brief that quietly re-gates the app. The over-block findings are the interesting residue and they are stated, not resolved: a shared `busy` flag blocks controls that are not its subject, which is a real UX question for the owner, and answering it here would have been this arc deciding something it was told to report. Rejected: wrapping the self-evident states (the documented rule says the label, an empty input, nothing to act on, or an end position IS the statement — and each of those is now a pin, so a future change cannot silently add a tooltip there), a second per-surface reason idiom, and fixing the unreachable spine gate by inventing a caller. | active |
| 101 | **A change is requested through ONE seam that forwards to the engines: `changeArtifact` routes by the artifact's own kind, and an instruction only ever ADDS a prompt paragraph** (owner's design instruction, verbatim: *"Good feedback. There are already engines to design each artefact and those engines already get context and can get additional instructions, you are right that we should reuse those, makes the intellectual load on the chat much lighter. I suggest a centralized changeArtefact function that can change any artefact based on instructions and then forwards that to the specialists. I am a fan of centralized functions."*). **The felt problem.** The owner refines a module through the canvas chat, but the chat sees only the parts document (raw markdown) plus reference grounding — it can neither see nor touch an artifact's details (an encounter's level/budget/rooms/roster, an NPC's stat block, a location's fields). The agreed route is that the chat may READ details on request (a later arc) and that CHANGES are requested through the design engines rather than written directly; this row lands the SEAM ONLY — no chat wiring, no chat protocol, no new UI beyond what exists. **Why routing beats a chat-side writer.** A chat that wrote artifact rows itself would be a second writer with no `writerModel`/revision provenance, no per-kind brief, no validation boundary, no creature-row guard and no busy gate — and it would have to relearn that an encounter has exactly two regeneration operations, that a location is detailed by the Worldbuilder while a note is detailed by the Plot Architect, and that a rulebook-cited creature row is not authored at all. One writer path, one provenance story, one failure story; the chat's whole intellectual load becomes one call. **The seam** is `src/features/modules/change-artifact.ts` — `changeArtifact({ artifactId, instruction?, encounter? })`. It resolves the row, chooses the route from `artifact.kind` (never from a claim the caller makes) and forwards. The routing table, verified per kind: `encounter` → `features/campaign/encounterRegen`, the requested one of its two EXISTING operations (`encounter.operation: 'repopulate'` / `'everything'`, REQUIRED — they are genuinely different and the second replaces the layout and map, so the destructive one is never a default), with the existing `redesignProse` and `dungeonMapPath` options forwarded unchanged; `npc` / `location` / `event` / `faction` / `note` → `features/modules/entity-batch.runEntityBatch` with a per-target `artifactId`, which rides `runEngine`'s in-place refill (the already-documented "use the NPC smith to correct this" flow: identity, links and images preserved, the model's invented name kept as an alias, `writerModel` and the `persona` revision source recorded) instead of creating a second row of the same name. **How the instruction reaches a prompt — the reality at `e72bc82`, measured.** The engine's `Additional instruction: …` paragraph was ALREADY rendered at four sites (`runEngine`'s draft, statblock, review check and encounter brief) but was reachable ONLY from `retryStep`/`resumeRun`: `startRun` could not carry one, and nothing persisted one on the run row, so a run-input field would have been dropped silently on the next resume. The seam therefore invents no run input — the instruction rides the BRIEF the specialist already builds, in ONE implementation (`llm/additionalInstruction`, which `runEngine`'s four sites render through as well). Two consequences are stated because they are load-bearing: the brief persists as the run row's `userBrief`, so a resumed run keeps the instruction; and the brief is also the retrieval query and the grounding-detection text, so an instruction participates in grounding — accepted (it is the same free-text field, and grounding on the requested change is the better error). Per kind: `buildEntityBrief` gained a trailing optional `instruction` (its final paragraph, carried by the entity lane's `RunEntityBatchInput.instruction`), and `EncounterRegenOptions.instruction` reaches EVERY brief the operation sends — the content leg, the single path's map leg and the prose leg; the prose leg keeps its own prose-only + roster-verbatim contract, so a reply that lets the instruction override it fails loud with nothing half-applied. **Empty means absent, byte-for-byte:** `additionalInstructionSection('')` is `null` and `withAdditionalInstruction(brief, '')` returns the brief UNCHANGED — no separator, no empty paragraph. That is why the artifact editor's two long-standing buttons (which pass no instruction) keep sending exactly the prompts they always sent, and why every existing full-literal brief/contract pin still guards the default. **The refusals — returned as values, never reported as success.** A bestiary creature row is REFUSED with the shared `creature-row-guard.creatureRowAiRefusal` copy, nothing written and no engine called. A module-less entity row is refused (the entity engines ground every brief in the owning module's text, so there is nothing to ground in — the reason names the way out). `pc` and `plotarc` are UNSUPPORTED: the Party is authored (row 69) and a plot arc is a real artifact kind with a real engine but is not a module entity kind (`ENTITY_KINDS` has no plotarc; this arc extends no vocabulary). A kind-option mismatch (encounter options on an npc, no operation for an encounter) THROWS: that is a programming error, not a user-facing refusal. **THE CREATURE-ROW REFUSAL IS A RATIFIED BOUNDARY** (owner's standing decision for this arc, verbatim: **"the bestiary creature-row refusal STANDS. It is the ONE documented exception to 'change any artifact from an instruction'."**): a rulebook-cited, chunk-backed creature row is ONE shared row per campaign per cited chunk, pointed at by every citing encounter, with battle seeding resolving its stats through it and its portrait cached globally — it is an identity, not an authoring slot, and it is never rewritable by instruction. The refusal carries its named reason and the remedy, writes nothing at all, and can never be reported as success, so a caller (later, the chat) can see WHY and relay it to the owner instead of appearing to fail silently. This is a deliberate exception, not debt and not a TODO: do not "fix" it later. **Uniform semantics, not new ones.** The one-generation-per-module gate is the EXISTING `llm/canvasBusy` registry — claimed for the change, released in `finally`, second claimant gets the existing loud `ModuleBusyError` (never a queue, never a silent no-op). Failures PROPAGATE (AGENTS 1/2): a specialist that throws, a run that did not complete carrying the specialist's own reason, a vanished campaign or module. The only non-throwing outcomes are the seam's own decisions, returned as a discriminated result — `changed` (with the `operation` that ran), `refused` (with its `reason`) and `unsupported` (with its `reason`), every arm carrying `artifactId` and `kind` — so a caller branches without matching a string. Provenance and persistence stay the specialist's write. **NO UNDO STORY IS INVENTED — the honest position.** Artifact ROWS have no versions and no undo: the module DOCUMENT has durable versions (`moduleVersionRepo`), an artifact row has only its own revisions (which `updateArtifact` writes before a content write) plus the documented regeneration semantics — a regenerated artifact legitimately loses what regeneration replaces. The seam adds no undo, no snapshot and no partial-apply protection beyond what each specialist already gives; a caller that needs the previous text reads the row's revisions, and this row does not pretend the seam bought anything more. **The proofs, MEASURED — nine, one at a time, each watched to fail its NAMED pin and then restored byte-identically (md5 compared per file, `git status` clean):** the entity brief ignoring the instruction (`buildEntityBrief`), `legBrief` dropping it (the encounter prompt reaches the model without the paragraph), the creature-row check disabled (the refusal pin), the busy claim removed (`ModuleBusyError` pin), `'repopulate'` routed to the other operation (routing pin), the slot release removed (release pin), and the paragraph made unconditional (the no-instruction byte-identity pin, `tests/features/persona-request.test.ts`'s full-literal brief and the prompt pin). Two of the nine are NON-VACUITY INJECTIONS that keep the code compiling: the seam swallowing a specialist failure into a `changed` result (the failure-propagation pin) and the artifact editor calling the specialist directly past the seam (the UI seam pin, `tests/features/change-artifact-ui.test.tsx`). Every reverted-source run failed EXACTLY ONE named pin with the rest skipped, so no proof passed for a missing-test reason. **What it deliberately is not:** no chat wiring, no read-on-request, no UI instruction box, no new prompt form, no second busy registry, no queue and no retry, no catch-and-continue, no scope change (a change never re-scopes a row — the entity lane skips the ownership stamp for an in-place target, since the seam resolved the module FROM that row), and no second `changeX` per surface. **One deviation from the brief, REPORTED rather than smuggled.** The brief asked for the entity panel's per-kind batches to be re-routed through the seam. They are NOT, and the reason is structural: those batches are not changes — their targets are NAMES that have no artifact yet, they carry no instruction, and their semantics are a bounded `maxParallelRequests` pool under ONE dock job (`module-entities-<moduleId>-<kind>`). A single-artifact seam would serialize them and give every target its own progress job. `runEntityBatch` therefore stays the entity engine's ONE runner — the seam CALLS it — so no surface gains a second path to a specialist: creation has one way, change has one way. **Re-routed call sites.** `src/features/campaign/components/artifact-editor.tsx`'s two encounter actions now call the seam and keep only their own `running` flag and their toasts; a `refused` result is toasted with the seam's own reason (never a silent no-op). The persona panel's "run a chosen persona on this artifact" surface is NOT routed here: it is the engines' own manual UI, not an instruction-driven change. | NEW `src/features/modules/change-artifact.ts` (`changeArtifact` + `ChangeArtifactRequest`/`ChangeArtifactResult`); NEW `src/llm/additionalInstruction.ts` (`additionalInstructionSection`, `withAdditionalInstruction` — the ONE form); `src/llm/runEngine.ts` (the four render sites now call it, byte-identical); `src/features/modules/persona-request.ts` (`buildEntityBrief`'s trailing optional `instruction`); `src/features/modules/entity-batch.ts` (`RunEntityBatchInput.instruction`, `EntityBatchTarget.artifactId` → `targetArtifactId` instead of `placementModuleId`, no ownership stamp for a change target); `src/features/campaign/encounterRegen.ts` (`EncounterRegenOptions.instruction` through `legBrief`); `src/features/campaign/components/artifact-editor.tsx`; NEW `tests/features/change-artifact.test.ts` (17) and `tests/features/change-artifact-instruction.test.ts` (3) + 3 new pins in `tests/llm/encounterRepopulate.test.ts`; docs/18 §2 + §4, docs/08 §Batch generation, docs/11 D18, this row | The owner's reasoning IS the rationale and the evidence agrees with it: the engines already exist, already receive context and already accept an instruction, so the only missing piece was one door — and a door is cheaper and safer than a second writer (the alternative buys a chat that must re-learn every kind's contract and would drift from the engines the moment a prompt moves). Key sub-decisions, each with its reason: the route is read from the ROW because a caller-declared kind is a lie waiting to happen; the instruction is optional-with-`''` because the existing surfaces genuinely have none and a canned one would change prompts the artefact editor has sent since row 54 (the empty case is the honest "none", not a masked failure); the encounter operation is REQUIRED because the two operations differ in what they destroy; refusals are VALUES while failures THROW because a refusal is a decision the caller must relay and a failure is one it must not be able to ignore; and the creature-row refusal is RATIFIED as the single documented exception to "change any artifact". Rejected: a chat-side writer; a `changeX` per kind; a required non-empty instruction; a default encounter operation; a new run-input field for the instruction (unpersisted, silently dropped on resume); a `failed` status arm beside the throws; re-deriving a brief or a run wait inside the seam. | active |

| 100 | **Every wiki-link chip's hover tooltip LEADS with the byte-exact token it was written from — because the plugin that builds the chip had thrown the source bytes away** (owner request, verbatim: *"i would like to have a hover tooltip over all Wikilinks where the raw text of the link is displayed. Some things are just not visible in the rendered version, like encounter parameters."*). **The finding, verified at `e72bc82` before anything was built.** `remarkWikiLinks` matches `[[Name]]` / `[[Name\|display]]` in a text run and REPLACES the match with a synthetic mdast `link` node whose `url` is `#wiki:<encoded name>` and whose only child is the DISPLAY text — `match[0]` is byte-exact and available at the match site, and `wikiLinkNode(name, display)` was handed only the trimmed name and display, so **the raw token was discarded there, silently and unrecoverably**: downstream, `[[Encounter:Ash Gate\|the gate]]` and `[[Ash Gate]]` are the same two strings, and the thing the owner cannot see on screen (encounter parameters, the target itself) exists nowhere in the render. `WikiMarkdown` then built the chip's `title` from what survived — `${kind} ${artifact.name}`, the ambiguity list, or `${name} — not detailed yet` — so a chip reading "the gate" said nothing about what it points at. **What was built.** (1) The token is CARRIED, never reconstructed: `splitWikiText`'s wiki segment gained `raw: match[0]`, `wikiLinkNode(name, display, raw)` puts it on the node's `data` — **`data.hProperties[WIKI_RAW_ATTRIBUTE]`, `'data-wiki-raw'`, the one supported mdast→hast route for custom properties** (`mdast-util-to-hast`'s `applyData` merges it into the produced element; `hast-util-to-jsx-runtime` hands it to the React component) — and `WikiMdNode` grew a deliberate minimal `data` view rather than a cast. **VERIFIED EMPIRICALLY rather than assumed**, because a documented carrier that silently does not survive would leave the tooltip empty while every pin passed on the fallback: a scratch probe against react-markdown 10.1.0 / mdast-util-to-hast 13.2.1 rendered `<a href="#wiki:Ash%20Gate" data-wiki-raw="[[ Encounter:Ash Gate \|the gate]]">the gate</a>` and the `a` component received `href, data-wiki-raw, node, children` — so `hProperties` IS the mechanism and no fallback was needed. (2) The chip's tooltip is `wikiChipTitle(raw, existing) = \`${raw} — ${existing}\`` — the token FIRST, and **every existing title survives byte-intact as the tail**: resolved `[[ Ash Gate \|the gate]] — NPC Ash Gate`, unresolved `[[Kael]] — Kael — not detailed yet`, ambiguous `[[Ash Gate]] — ⚠ 2 artifacts match “Ash Gate”: …`. The token is also set on the chip element as `data-wiki-raw`, so the carrier is inspectable independently of the tooltip string. (3) **The mechanism is the native `title`, chosen deliberately**: the chip is a real, non-`disabled` `<button>`, so its tooltip renders (docs/18 §4's invisible-`title` defect is measured for natively disabled controls only — a disabled shadcn Button is not even hit-tested), and it adds NO wrapper element inside flowing prose — chips are `inline-flex` in module prose and a reader renders many of them, so a tooltip trigger per chip risks layout drift at reader scale; `BlockedControl` is NOT used (nothing here is blocked). **Surface coverage is automatic, and the audit is the reason to believe it**: `#wiki:` hrefs have exactly ONE producer (`wikiHref`) and exactly ONE consumer (`wiki-markdown`), and every wiki-aware surface renders through that one `WikiMarkdown` — module reader, canvas preview, peek modal, artifact-body preview (`markdown-body`), part-text-editor preview, all three module-board card kinds, and the guide. The CodeMirror **editor** is a DIFFERENT path (`canvas/wikiDecorations`, its own `title` over the raw text) and is deliberately untouched: it already shows the raw text, so it needs nothing. **The export path is untouched, and that is structural, not a promise:** `lib/modulePdf` / `lib/pdfExport` / `lib/mdToPdfmake` never import `WikiMarkdown` or `remark-wikilinks` (pinned by a source scan), and the carrier is minted at React render time and written to no row — so no export can reach it. Documented and reported: the module/deliverable pipeline IS wiki-aware (`mdToPdfmake` renders the display text), while **the single-artifact GM-notes/handout export prints `[[…]]` VERBATIM** — `lib/markdown.markdownToText` is not wiki-aware, `lib/wikilinks.stripWikiLinks` has NO production consumer, and this is **PRE-EXISTING at `origin/main`** (`git show origin/main:src/lib/pdfExport.ts` lines 278/300) and deliberately NOT changed here, because this arc's brief requires export output to stay as it is. That corrects the brief's own premise — "raw `[[…]]` syntax must never appear in an exported document" is FALSE at HEAD for that one path — and the state is pinned in `tests/lib/wiki-raw-export.test.ts` as a recorded finding rather than swallowed. **Two further brief corrections, both measured:** the two pins the brief named as the ones that must keep passing unchanged (`tests/features/creature-row-resolution.test.tsx:259`, `tests/features/module-reader.test.tsx:216`) are not chip-title pins at all — the first asserts the entity panel's `entity-creature-only` marker (`stringContaining`, unaffected) and the second is a fixture guard — and it missed the three pins the title change DOES necessarily break, each updated to assert the NEW exact string (which is strictly stronger: both halves pinned), not weakened: `creature-row-resolution.test.tsx:363` (`'NPC Zombie'` → `'[[Zombie]] — NPC Zombie'`), `entity-batch.integration.test.tsx:155/161` (`findByTitle` is an EXACT match, so `'Kael — not detailed yet'` → `'[[Kael]] — Kael — not detailed yet'`), and the `splitWikiText` unit pin (`toEqual` on the segment, now including `raw`). Nothing about resolution, clicking, the ⚠ marker, chip styling, `onOpenArtifact`/`onStub` or the editor changed. **Tests and proofs:** `tests/features/wiki-chip-tooltip.test.tsx` (new — resolved with a PADDED token and a `\|display`, unresolved, ambiguous, the carrier on the element, an ordinal non-vacuity block, and four negatives: a plain `[text](url)` link, a token in a code span, a token in a fenced block, and a token inside markdown link TEXT), `tests/lib/wiki-raw-export.test.ts` (new — the module definition is bracket-free with the display text present and the tokens proven present on the rows first, plus the structural no-import scan and the recorded single-artifact finding), the reader surface pin (`module-reader.test.tsx`), the canvas-preview surface pin (`canvas-preview-default.test.tsx`), and the plugin-carrier pins (`remark-wikilinks.test.tsx`). **Every pin is MEASURED revert-proof and the non-vacuity injection is real:** (A) the carrier removed from `wikiLinkNode` → **9 named pins fail across 4 files** (reader, canvas preview, resolved, unresolved, ambiguous, plugin, `'' / undefined to be '[[ Ash Gate \|the gate]]'`); (B) the byte-exactness pins replaced by a RECONSTRUCTION `\`[[${name}\|${display}]]\`` → **7 pins fail**, the assertion naming both strings (`expected '[[Ash Gate\|the gate]]' to be '[[ Ash Gate \|the gate]]'`), which is exactly the non-vacuity injection the brief asked for; (C) the tooltip with the token APPENDED instead of leading → **6 pins fail** (the ordinal pin included); (D) `wikiChipTitle` returning only the token, dropping the existing information → **8 pins fail** across four files, so "never lose what the chip already said" is load-bearing too. Every file was restored byte-identically between proofs and verified with `md5sum -c` (0 mismatches); all scratch lived under the worktree and is gone. **Limit I could not prove:** jsdom has no tooltip rendering and no hit-testing, so the pins prove the DOM contract (title content, its byte-exactness, its order, and the carrier attribute) and NOT that Chrome paints the tooltip on hover — that rests on the documented behaviour for a non-disabled button, which is the same limit row 98 recorded for its device. | `src/lib/remark-wikilinks.ts` (`WIKI_RAW_ATTRIBUTE`, `WikiMdNode.data`, `WikiSegment.raw`, `splitWikiText`, `wikiLinkNode`); `src/features/campaign/components/wiki-markdown.tsx` (`WikiAnchorProps`, `wikiChipTitle`, `WikiChip.raw`, `data-wiki-raw`); `tests/features/wiki-chip-tooltip.test.tsx` (new, 10); `tests/lib/wiki-raw-export.test.ts` (new, 3); `tests/lib/remark-wikilinks.test.tsx` (+2, one updated); `tests/features/module-reader.test.tsx` (surface pin); `tests/features/canvas-preview-default.test.tsx` (surface pin); `tests/features/creature-row-resolution.test.tsx` + `tests/features/entity-batch.integration.test.tsx` (the two exact-match title pins updated); docs/05 §The chip's hover tooltip; docs/08 §M4-A; docs/18 §2.3 + §4; docs/08-TESTING.md coverage matrix | The owner's complaint is precise: the rendered view shows the link's DISPLAY text, so whatever he wrote the link FROM is invisible — and for an encounter the token is where the parameters live. The direction follows the data: the source bytes exist at exactly one moment (the plugin's match) and are destroyed there, so the cure is to CARRY them, not to guess them back — a `[[${name}\|${display}]]` rebuild is indistinguishable to a reader but wrong as written (measured failing on inner padding), which is why the fixture is padded and the pin is ordinal. Native `title` over a Tooltip primitive because the chip is already a real button in flowing prose: the tooltip renders, the reason-for-a-disabled-control problem does not apply, and no wrapper is added to a reader that renders hundreds of chips. Rejected: reconstructing the token from name+display; a second chip renderer or per-surface wiring; changing resolution, the ⚠ marker, click behaviour, chip styling or the CodeMirror editor (which shows the raw text already); `BlockedControl` (nothing is blocked); and fixing the pre-existing single-artifact export pass-through inside this arc (the brief forbids changing export output — recorded and pinned for the export path's own arc instead). | active |

| 103 | **The chat may REQUEST an artifact's stored details — and the request is answered from the STORED ROWS in exactly ONE further call, with the write half deliberately absent** (owner's design intent, verbatim: *"Just so you understand the normal workflow: Modules get autocreated and then refined with the chat. Thats a workflow that i already use very nicely with story outlines. So, the module chat is absolutely central as the main engine for refinement. I am considering right now if we should make the details available for the chat (maybe not unconditionally but for the LLM to be able to request). That would also need an ability for the LLM to actually change those details."*). **The felt problem, measured.** The canvas chat's context is the module's TEXT — the whole parts document plus continuity grounding — so an artifact's STORED details are invisible to it: the model refining a module written *around* "Salt Gate Ambush" cannot see that encounter's difficulty, level hint, roster (with four goblins, two per ledge), per-room keys, budget advisory, fill grade or legendary-resistance layout; nor an NPC's stat block, a faction's ranks, an event's hooks. Row 101 landed the seam that can CHANGE a row (one `changeArtifact` call, routing by kind, forwarding to the existing engines) but nothing can yet READ one. **Why a REQUEST beats pre-supplying, on the owner's own hesitation ("maybe not unconditionally").** The module document is already the dominant context and it is sent fresh every turn; the pool behind the chat is the WHOLE campaign plus the shared library — every encounter, NPC, location, event, faction, note, plotarc, PC row, each with a layout, a roster and prose — so an unconditional details block is unbounded input growth paid on every turn of every conversation, including the ones editing a single sentence. A request costs one named block, sized by what the model actually needs, and it is also the honest shape for the future write half: the model has to NAME the row it wants to change. The cost is stated: a request-based read makes a turn up to TWO model calls, which is why the round trip is capped at one. **The read half that landed.** The assistant may emit `<request><name>EXACT NAME</name></request>` (exactly one `<name>`, no attributes, the name VERBATIM — no quoting, no escaping, structural parity with `<edit>`), at most `MAX_REQUESTS_PER_REPLY` = **5 per reply**, parsed by the SAME strict extractor (`parseCanvasChatReply`, `src/llm/canvasChat.ts`): a malformed, unterminated, attribute-carrying, empty-named, stray-closing or over-cap request throws `CanvasChatParseError` and fails the WHOLE reply — nothing partial, exactly as `<edit>` behaves, and the two tags are found in ONE left-to-right walk so a `<request>` sitting before an `<edit>` is never swallowed into the prose. The app then makes **exactly ONE further model call** (`buildCanvasChatDetailsPayload`: the ordinary payload + the model's asking reply + an app turn carrying the answer — the roles stay alternating, which is what `DETAILS_ANSWER_TURN` is for) and returns; a request appearing in THAT reply is never served: it comes back as `ignoredRequests`, the caller toasts it by name and the capability re-arms on the next user message. No loop, no retry-until-answered, no third call under any input. **The answers come from STORED ROWS.** `loadChatDetailsPool(campaignId)` = the campaign's own artifacts + the shared library — exactly the pool the module's wiki chips resolve against — and every name goes through the EXISTING `lib/wikilinks.resolveWikiLink(name, pool, { moduleId })`, so chat resolution and chip resolution CANNOT disagree (row 100's parity, and the ambiguity candidate list is literally the resolver's). Each row is rendered per kind by stored fields only (`renderArtifactDetails` / `artifactDetailLines`): a shared head (`### <name> — <Kind label> · <scope>`, `also known as:`, `tags:`, `summary:`, `links:` with targets named from the same pool, and `prose body (the row's stored markdown):`), then — encounter: difficulty / level hint / shape / preset / location kind / map mode, fill grade, budget advisory, map image, terrain / tactics / treasure, the layout's rooms (description, roster indexes, challenge level, room key, key treasure, spawn) with corridors, grid, theme, map path and play order, the roster per entry (name, ×count, notes, per-entry treasure, and stats resolved through `monsterResolve.resolveMonsterEntryWithRepos` — an unresolved citation renders a loud `stats: MISSING — …`, and a name-only entry says so); npc: appearance / personality, the rulebook creature marker, the stat block on the row or the cited chunk's; pc: player / stat block / current HP / initiative override / notes; location+event: type, inhabitants, points of interest, hooks; faction: goals / methods / resources / ranks; plotarc: arc type, premise, stakes, beats, hooks, climax; note: the shared lines only (a note has no structured fields BY DESIGN, so an entirely empty note row is the honest "nothing stored" case). Every list is emitted only when non-empty, so a genuinely bare row yields the refusal rather than a block of blanks. The assembled block is `MAX_DETAILS_BLOCK_CHARS` = **12000 characters**, and the cap is LOUD, never a silent trim: ONE record that overflows alone is included TRUNCATED with `[TRUNCATED — …]` (reserved marker room inside the cap, so the marker can never itself be cut), and records that no longer fit are named in `[BLOCK FULL — … these requested records were NOT included: «A», «B»]`; sections that fit carry no marker at all. It rides the follow-up user turn as a `<requested-details>` block under `REQUESTED_DETAILS_HEADER` + `CANVAS_CHAT_DETAILS_INSTRUCTION` — REFERENCE-ONLY, never to be edited, never folded into the module text, with commands still applying only to the parts document. **The failure vocabulary is named and model-readable**, in fixed sentences computed by named functions: `NO SUCH ARTIFACT` (no row of that name in the campaign or the library, plus the same resolution hint the chips give), `AMBIGUOUS NAME` (the resolver's OWN candidates listed — the app REFUSES to guess, because the future write half must never edit the wrong row; a kind-discriminated request is deliberately OUT of this arc and the model is told to name the row it means), `NOTHING STORED ON THE ROW` (the row exists and is empty — the model is told to ask the owner instead of inventing), plus the cap verdicts above. What the model is NEVER handed is a substitute: not the row's summary standing in for its fields, not the module text re-quoted as if it were the row, not a fabricated default. **The write half does not exist, and this row does not pretend otherwise**: nothing on this path writes an artifact, a revision row, a module byte or a ledger entry (pinned by an equality check on the whole artifact list, the revision count and the module row across a full round trip), no request can mutate anything, and `features/modules/change-artifact.ts` is not called, not imported and not wired — details are changed through row 101's seam in its own arc, and the READ half is what the model needs first anyway (it has to name the row before it can change it). **Honest limits.** (1) The brief asked that NOTHING change when the capability is unused — "same prompt bytes, same single call" — and that cannot hold literally: a model cannot emit `<request>` unless the system prompt tells it the block exists, so `canvasChatSystemPrompt()` DID gain the request clause. It stays ONE constant, byte-identical for a turn that uses the capability and one that does not, and what IS pinned byte-for-byte is everything a turn decides: the turn content, exactly one `chat` call, no extra DB reads, the same apply path and the same streaming prose (see below). The claim is therefore stated as "unused replies are unchanged", which is what the pins can actually hold (docs/18 §4). (2) A request makes a turn TWO calls, so it is slower than giving the model everything — accepted, because the alternative is unbounded per-turn context. (3) The 12000-char cap can hand the model a truncated record; that is why the marker names the truncation, the record and the fact that the remainder is missing. (4) A kind-discriminated request (`<request kind="npc">`) is NOT in this arc: ambiguity is refused with candidates instead. **Nothing changes when nothing is requested, pinned.** A reply with no request takes ONE call, returns `details: null`, sends a byte-identical `canvasChatTurnContent` (a golden literal, no `<requested-details>` anywhere in the payload), reads no artifact rows beyond what the turn already read, applies through the SAME `applyChatCommandsFor` path and streams through the SAME `chatProseSoFar` (which returns the raw reply byte-identically when it contains no command opener). The controller side adds exactly one thing that only a request can produce: the follow-up reply lands as its OWN assistant bubble whose commands apply in reply order and persist through the same split-save with its OWN `writerModel`; a follow-up that fails is a loud `failed` card on that bubble while the first reply's work stands, and a stop settles BOTH bubbles as aborted. Where it lives: `src/llm/canvasChat.ts` (schema, cap, parser, renderer, resolver, payload builders, both call sites), `src/features/modules/canvas/chatController.ts` (editor follow-up bubble + apply + toast), `src/features/modules/canvas/snapshotChat.ts` (preview string-splice parity); tests `tests/llm/canvasChatDetails.test.ts` (31 pins) and `tests/features/canvas-chat-details.test.ts` (7 pins); docs/08 §Module canvas chat, docs/18 §2.2 + §4. REVERSIBLE by deleting the request clause, the resolver/renderer and the two controller branches — no persisted shape, no migration, no Dexie version (the round trip adds NO field: it rides the existing `ModuleChatMessage` history as two ordinary assistant messages, and `chatStore`/`chatPersist` are untouched). | `src/llm/canvasChat.ts`; `src/features/modules/canvas/chatController.ts`; `src/features/modules/canvas/snapshotChat.ts`; `tests/llm/canvasChatDetails.test.ts`; `tests/features/canvas-chat-details.test.ts`; docs/08 §Module canvas chat; docs/18 §2.2 (seam row) + §4 (gotcha) | a request the model controls is the only shape that stays honest about cost — the whole campaign + library cannot ride every turn, and only the model knows which row it is talking about; resolution MUST be the chips' own resolver over the chips' own pool, or the chat and the reading surface can name different rows; ambiguity must be REFUSED rather than guessed, because a wrong pick is harmless for a read and catastrophic for the write half that follows; the answer must be REFERENCE-ONLY and rendered from stored fields, never from a summary, the module text or a fabricated default (AGENTS 1/3); the cap must be loud, because a silently shortened record reads exactly like a row that stores less; one follow-up call is the whole budget — a loop would make a chat turn unbounded in cost without making the answer better; and the write half is deliberately NOT here: a read path that can mutate is how one seam becomes two writers. | active |

| 104 | **The chat may CHANGE an artifact: a `<change>` block names a row, an instruction and (for an encounter) an operation — and the change runs through row 101's ONE seam, sequentially, visibly and recoverably** (owner's intent, verbatim: *"the module chat is absolutely central as the main engine for refinement. I am considering right now if we should make the details available for the chat (maybe not unconditionally but for the LLM to be able to request). That would also need an ability for the LLM to actually change those details."* — the read half landed as row 103; this row lands the WRITE half). **The felt problem.** A module is autocreated and then refined through the canvas chat, but the chat's context is the module TEXT plus reference grounding, so the model can describe, quote and edit the prose while being unable to touch the ROWS the prose is built around: it cannot lower an encounter's level band, restock a roster, rewrite an NPC it decided is the smith's sister, or fix a location's fields — the very details row 103 taught it to READ. **What landed.** The assistant may emit `<change operation="repopulate\|everything"><name>EXACT NAME</name><instruction>WHAT TO CHANGE</instruction></change>`, at most `MAX_CHANGES_PER_REPLY` = **3 per reply**, parsed by the SAME strict extractor as `<edit>`/`<request>` (`parseCanvasChatReply`: one balanced left-to-right walk over all three tags, so a `<change>` before an `<edit>` is never swallowed into prose). Everything strict about the read half holds here: a `<change>` with an attribute other than `operation`, a duplicated or invented `operation` value, the children in the wrong order, a missing or EMPTY `<name>`/`<instruction>`, an unterminated block, a stray `</change>` or more than three changes in one reply throws `CanvasChatParseError` and fails the WHOLE reply — **the cap is checked before the first change runs, so a rejected reply executes NOTHING** (pinned). The instruction must be non-empty by construction, and the refusal to default it is deliberate: an empty instruction would run a canned engine operation behind the owner's back. **`operation` is REQUIRED for an encounter and is refused by name for every other kind — there is no default.** The two operations are genuinely different (`repopulate` restocks the encounter, leaving its prose alone; `everything` regenerates it INCLUDING its prose and replaces the layout and map), and only the model knows which the owner asked for, so an encounter change without one is answered with a named refusal that spells out both operations and their exact scope, and nothing is called. A non-encounter carrying `operation` is refused the same way (the seam itself calls that mismatch a programming error — it must never reach it). `everything` deliberately does NOT set the editor's own `redesignProse` checkbox: that is a bigger change than the model asked for, and the `changed` detail says so in as many words. **Names resolve like the chips, because the write half makes a wrong pick dangerous.** `resolveChatArtifactName({ name, moduleId, pool })` is the read half's resolution extracted into ONE function (both halves now call it, so chat resolution can never disagree with itself) and it is the EXISTING `lib/wikilinks.resolveWikiLink` over the SAME one-per-turn `loadChatDetailsPool` snapshot: an unresolved name is `NO SUCH ARTIFACT`, and a name two rows share is `AMBIGUOUS NAME` **carrying the resolver's own candidate list** — the app refuses to guess which row to overwrite. (Row 103 predicted exactly this: "a silent newest-wins pick … because the future write half must not edit the wrong row".) **Every change goes through the ONE seam, and the chat path writes no row.** `features/modules/canvas/chatChanges.executeChatChange` resolves the name, enforces the operation rules and calls `features/modules/change-artifact.changeArtifact` with the RESOLVED row's id, the instruction and the operation — it never touches `artifactRepo`, never routes on a kind it decided, never re-derives a brief. The seam gained exactly one additive field, `signal?: AbortSignal`, checked after its row/campaign reads and BEFORE its claim, so a stopped change never starts work; nothing else in the seam changed. **Sequential, capped, abortable — and honest about what a stop can promise.** The phase runs one change at a time, in reply order (pinned by an event-order and a max-concurrency measurement, not by a comment). **The module's generation slot is HANDED OVER for the phase**: the chat turn holds the `llm/canvasBusy` claim (chat/refine serialize) and each change claims the same slot through the seam, and since that registry is a `Set` and `claimModuleGeneration` is not re-entrant, a nested claim would throw `ModuleBusyError` immediately and no change would ever run. The turn therefore releases the slot for the phase and re-claims it in a `finally` for the follow-up call; if a real generation takes it in that window the turn does NOT wait — the outcomes stand and the follow-up is skipped with a LOUD named reason (the changes were made; the model was not told), because waiting would hold the owner's turn behind somebody else's run. A slot another generation holds reads as a named `MODULE BUSY` outcome, never a silent skip and never a queue. Stops are stated as the engines actually behave: the loop checks the signal at each change boundary (no new change starts after a stop), the seam refuses to start a change whose signal is already aborted, and a run that Stop all cancels ends the turn as a STOP (the caller settles the reply `aborted`) rather than as a failed change — with what the engines already guarantee and nothing more: a landed change is a full `updateArtifact` write (new revision, previous intact) and content not yet finalized was not written. **The outcomes ride the SAME one follow-up call.** `changed` / `refused` / `unsupported` (the seam's own arms, passed through untouched) plus this half's `unresolved` / `ambiguous` / `busy` / `failed` are relayed to the model in the turn's one follow-up as a `<change-results>` block (`CHANGE_RESULTS_HEADER` + `CANVAS_CHAT_CHANGES_INSTRUCTION`, built by `canvasChatFollowUpTurnContent`, which the details half now delegates to as well so the details-only form stays byte-identical): one `### Change «NAME» — VERDICT` line per change, every non-applied verdict reading `NOT APPLIED: <VERDICT>` so it cannot be mistaken for success, the collapsed `asked:` instruction echoed (two changes to the same row never blur), and a `failed` verdict stating that the failure did not report success and must be checked against the row. The document and the model's asking reply ride that call exactly once; a `<change>` in the FOLLOW-UP reply is `ignoredChanges` — a named no-op, never a third call (the read half's `ignoredRequests` shape, reused rather than reinvented). Both halves in ONE reply ride the ONE follow-up with both blocks, and the details are resolved from the pre-change snapshot the read half's header already declares. **The owner SEES it (AGENTS 2).** `chatChanges.reportChatChangeOutcome` fires per change the moment it settles, from BOTH flows (`chatController` and `snapshotChat`): a `changed` outcome is a loud success toast naming the artifact, its kind, the operation's exact scope and the instruction; every other status is a loud `toastError` saying it did NOT change (busy: "was NOT started") with the named reason and the instruction; and the `failed` copy deliberately does NOT claim nothing changed — it says the change did not report success and sends the owner to the row and its revision history, because the app cannot know how far a crashed specialist got. During the run the progress dock carries `Changing «NAME»` (the artifact's detail and kind, linked to the artifact page). The turn's own `changes.status === 'failed'` (the follow-up could not be sent) and a second change in the follow-up reply are toasts too. **Recovery is MEASURED, not promised.** A landed change goes through `updateArtifact`, so it is a NEW revision with the PREVIOUS one byte-intact (pinned by comparing the stored revision snapshot to the pre-change row), and the artifact editor's existing revision list + `restoreRevision` restores it (pinned end-to-end: restore returns the old bytes). No new undo mechanism was invented, and the honest gap is stated: the seam's own regeneration semantics lose whatever regeneration replaces, and a `failed` change may have partially run — the revision history is the only surface, and the app says so instead of reassuring. **One trade-off the owner should know about (AGENTS §critique-the-instruction).** The brief specified a chat that CARRIES OUT the change, so that is what shipped: the change happens when the reply settles, without a confirm step, exactly as `<edit>` commands have always applied. The mitigation is not a promise but a property: it is capped (3), sequenced, visible the moment it settles, named on refusal, abortable at the boundaries, written as a revision and restorable from the editor. A future arc could put a PROPOSAL step in front of it (the same shape the canvas's selection refine uses), and that is a real option the owner can order — it is recorded here as an open alternative rather than silently decided. **Honest limits.** (1) Change outcomes are NOT persisted as chat outcome cards: the stored `moduleChatOutcomeSchema.command` carries an `<edit>` command shape, so a change's record is the settle-time toast plus the model's own follow-up bubble (and the row's revisions) — a reload shows the conversation but not a re-playable change card, and that is a limitation of this arc, not a silent omission. (2) A reply that both requests details and asks for a change sends ONE follow-up with both blocks — the model gets its details and its change results at once, and the details are the pre-change bytes. (3) A <change> reply cannot be served without a wired executor: the engine throws loudly rather than skipping the block, so a caller that forgets the wiring fails in tests instead of in the owner's session. | `src/llm/canvasChat.ts` (`canvasChatChangeSchema`, `CanvasChatChangeCommand`, `MAX_CHANGES_PER_REPLY`, `scanTagAttributes` + `parseChangeAt`, `resolveChatArtifactName`, the change phase in `sendCanvasChatMessage`, `renderChangeResults` + `CHANGE_RESULTS_HEADER` + `CANVAS_CHAT_CHANGES_INSTRUCTION`, `canvasChatFollowUpTurnContent` + `buildCanvasChatFollowUpPayload`, `CanvasChatChangeOutcome`/`CanvasChatChangeExecutor`); NEW `src/features/modules/canvas/chatChanges.ts` (`executeChatChange`, `reportChatChangeOutcome`); `src/features/modules/change-artifact.ts` (additive `signal?: AbortSignal` + its pre-claim boundary check); `src/features/modules/canvas/chatController.ts` + `src/features/modules/canvas/snapshotChat.ts` (both flows: executor wiring, changes-aware follow-up copy, the relay-failure and `ignoredChanges` toasts, honest abort copy); NEW `tests/llm/canvasChatChanges.test.ts` (30) + `tests/features/canvas-chat-changes.test.ts` (9); docs/08 §Module canvas chat; docs/18 §2.2 (seam row) + §4 (two gotchas) | The chat is the owner's refinement engine, and refinement means touching the rows the prose only gestures at — but the moment a chat can WRITE, the interesting question stops being "can it" and becomes "which row, how much, and how does the owner find out and undo it". Every design choice above answers one of those: the row is RESOLVED through the chips' own resolver and an ambiguous name is refused because overwriting the wrong artifact is not a read; the SCOPE is named by the model in words (`repopulate` vs `everything`) and never defaulted, because the difference is destruction; the work goes through row 101's seam so there is ONE writer with provenance, briefs, guards and a busy gate — a chat-side writer would have to relearn every kind's contract and would drift from the engines the first time a prompt moves; the slot is handed over because the registry is a `Set` and a nested claim is an immediate `ModuleBusyError`; the COST is bounded (3 per reply, one at a time, and the results ride the turn's existing one follow-up call rather than buying a second one); and the ACCOUNTABILITY is a loud per-outcome notice plus a revision-backed restore, because "the AI changed something" must never be something the owner discovers later. Rejected: a chat-side or per-surface row writer; a second tag vocabulary or a fuzzy block extractor; a default encounter operation; a silent newest-wins pick on ambiguity; parallel changes (each is a real generation and the slot is one); a second follow-up call; treating a stop as a change or as an error; inventing a rollback/undo mechanism beside the revision list; marking `operation` optional because "the model usually means repopulate". | active |

| 105 | **An export renders the DISPLAY text of a wiki token, never the token — one implementation, and the single-artifact PDFs were the leak** (the owner's standing rule, verbatim: *"The display should only ever be an UI rendering, internally this needs to always be the wikilinks format."*). **The felt problem.** Row 100's arc found that the `[[…]]` token is the app's INTERNAL representation (that is why the chip's tooltip has to carry it back from the source bytes) — so a PDF that prints it has leaked the internal to the reader, and a handout reading `He guards [[Encounter:Ash Gate\|the gate]] at night.` is visibly broken. **The defect, MEASURED at `b06775d` from the real builders, not from a hand-computed string.** Both single-artifact body sites (`lib/pdfExport.ts`, GM notes and player handout) rendered the body through `lib/markdown.markdownToText`, a markdown-syntax stripper that knows nothing about wiki tokens: for the body `He guards [[Encounter:Ash Gate\|the gate]] and [[ Ash Gate \|the gate]], and [[Kael]] answers to [[Kael\|  the smith  ]].` both definitions carried that string BYTE-IDENTICALLY, tokens intact — while `lib/wikilinks.stripWikiLinks`, written for exactly this job and fully tested, had **NO production consumer at all** (grep at HEAD: only `tests/lib/wikilinks.test.ts`). **The two-pipeline disagreement.** `lib/mdToPdfmake` (module and deliverable PDFs, via `parseInline`/`pushWithWiki`) has rendered the DISPLAY as a bold run since it existed — one app, two export pipelines, two behaviours for the same syntax. Row 100's arc recorded the leak and pinned it AS the defect in `tests/lib/wiki-raw-export.test.ts`; those pins are REWRITTEN here to assert the fixed behaviour (never deleted — the record of the defect is row 100, and this row supersedes it). **What landed.** ONE new seam and two call sites: `lib/markdown.markdownToDisplayText(markdown) = markdownToText(stripWikiLinks(markdown))` — the token stage reuses `stripWikiLinks` (no second `\[\[…\]\]` regex anywhere) and the markdown stage reuses `markdownToText` — wired into the GM-notes and handout body sites; `markdownToText` itself is UNCHANGED and `lib/mdToPdfmake` is UNTOUCHED (its output shape is rich runs, and building those a third time was explicitly not the fix). **The stage ORDER is measured, not taste:** both orders agree on every ordinary input, and the one input where they differ — a token inside a markdown link's text, `[see [[Ash Gate]](url)` — leaves raw `[see Ash Gate](url)` markdown under the order that runs the markdown stage first, i.e. the same class of leak this arc closes, so the TOKEN stage runs first (now pinned). **THE CONSUMER INVENTORY (the complete set at `b06775d` — every importer of `@/lib/markdown`, each decided knowingly).** (1) `lib/pdfExport.buildGmNotesDefinition` — EXPORT, a rendering → CHANGED. (2) `lib/pdfExport.buildPlayerHandoutDefinition` — EXPORT, a rendering → CHANGED. (3) `llm/imagePromptDraft.buildImagePrompt` — a MODEL PROMPT, neither a rendering nor a round-trip → **UNCHANGED, deliberately**: nothing there is read by the owner, and the token is the only place the referenced artifact's real NAME survives (`[[Encounter:Ash Gate\|the gate]]` grounds the illustration on "Ash Gate" as well as "the gate"), while the default-on text-render guard is what keeps syntax out of the picture; pinned AS-IS by `tests/llm/imagePromptDraft.test.ts` so a future swap of the function fails a named pin. **No consumer round-trips plain text back into stored markdown** (checked at the seam, not assumed), so no behaviour had to be preserved behind a flag and `markdownToText` needed no option — the split is TWO named functions because the caller's INTENT is what differs. **No second implementation, structurally pinned:** `lib/pdfExport` has no `@/lib/wikilinks` import, no `WIKI_LINK_PATTERN` and no `\[\[` regex, and neither `lib/mdToPdfmake` nor `lib/modulePdf` mentions the new function. **Invalid tokens keep their rule** (pinned in both directions): `[[ not even this one` and an unclosed `[[` stay literal byte-exact in BOTH templates, in the same test that proves the token body is bracket-free — so "strip the token" can never quietly become "strip brackets". **Documented divergences, reported rather than silently harmonised:** the plain-text pipeline has no code style (`markdownToText` already unwraps `` `…` `` and strips syntax inside a fence), so a token inside a code span or fence IS rendered — while `mdToPdfmake`'s code branch keeps it literal (measured both ways and pinned); and a display carrying markdown emphasis is reduced to prose (`[[X\|the *old* gate]]` → "the old gate"), because the body is markdown and the markdown stage owns that syntax. **Tests (9 in `tests/lib/wiki-raw-export.test.ts` — the row-100 pins rewritten plus new ones — +1 in `tests/lib/pdfExport.test.ts`, +1 in `tests/llm/imagePromptDraft.test.ts`):** the two bodies assert the RENDERED body BYTE-EXACT (`He guards the gate and the gate, and Kael answers to the smith.`) with no `[[`, no `]]`, no target and no `\|`; the literal body asserts byte-exact literalness in both templates; one test pins the two pipelines TOGETHER on one body (display in both, brackets in neither); one source scan pins the single composition site and the still-absent carrier; two unit pins cover the order and the code-span divergence. **Every pin is REVERT-PROVEN, one revert at a time, each file restored byte-identically and checked with `md5sum -c` (0 mismatches):** (A) the leak restored in `markdownToDisplayText` — **8 named pins fail**, the GM-notes one printing `expected 'He guards the gate and the gate…' / received 'He guards [[Encounter:Ash Gate\|the gate]] …'`; (B) **NON-VACUITY INJECTION**: a naive `\[\[\|\]\]` bracket strip that keeps the whole inner text (i.e. drops the display half) — **7 pins fail**, including the LITERAL pin, because that injection is also what breaks non-tokens; (C) **NON-VACUITY INJECTION**: `display ?? name` WITHOUT the trim — **5 pins fail** (`expected '  the smith  ' to be 'the smith'`); (D) the two body sites reverted ONE AT A TIME — the GM site alone fails exactly the GM pin + the parity pin while the handout pin PASSES, and the handout site alone is the mirror image, so each site carries its own pin. **What I could NOT prove.** (1) The RENDERED page: the pins are on the definition's body node, byte-exact, plus the pre-existing test that a real `%PDF-` blob is produced — nothing here extracts text from the rasterized PDF, so "pdfmake lays this string onto the page" rests on a mechanism this repo already exercises, not on a new measurement. (2) Token-in-code-span parity with `mdToPdfmake` — the divergence is measured and pinned, but it is a divergence, not agreement. (3) The order choice is measured on the cases above; I did not prove the two orders equivalent for ALL inputs (the differing case is the reason the order is pinned at all). | `src/lib/markdown.ts` (`markdownToDisplayText`, the amended `markdownToText` doc comment); `src/lib/pdfExport.ts` (both body sites + the module doc comment); `src/llm/imagePromptDraft.ts` (the exclusion recorded where the call is); `tests/lib/wiki-raw-export.test.ts` (7, the row-100 pins rewritten to the fixed behaviour); `tests/lib/pdfExport.test.ts` (+1 — the split pinned from both sides); `tests/llm/imagePromptDraft.test.ts` (+1 — the exclusion pinned verbatim); docs/07 §Wiki-links in an exported document; docs/05 §The chip's hover tooltip; docs/08-TESTING.md coverage matrix (2 rows); docs/18 §2.3 (two seam rows in place of one) + §4; this row | The direction follows the owner's rule and the DATA: the token is the internal, a PDF is a rendering, and the module pipeline already had the right behaviour while the single-artifact path silently disagreed — so the fix is to make the two agree through ONE implementation rather than to invent a third. `stripWikiLinks` was written for this job, was tested, and needed no production consumer invented for it; the markdown rich-run builder stayed exactly where it was because a second bold-run builder is what the seam index forbids. Sub-decisions each with a reason: the token stage runs first because the measured failing case is a residual-markdown leak of the same class; the faithful-source stripper stays intact because a MODEL prompt is not a rendering and would lose the target's name; the literal rule is pinned in the same test as the token rule so the two can never drift apart; and the code-span/emphasis divergences are recorded rather than harmonised, because harmonising them means teaching the plain-text pipeline about code semantics it deliberately does not have. Rejected: making `markdownToText` wiki-aware (silently changes the image-prompt consumer and destroys the round-trip-safe half); a boolean/option flag on one function (the call sites differ by intent, and a flag reads at the call site as noise); a second regex in `pdfExport`; copying `mdToPdfmake`'s bold-run building into the export path; touching any `mdToPdfmake` pin; deleting the row-100 pins instead of rewriting them. | active |
| 106 | **There is ONE creature tier: a LIBRARY creature is cited and never materialized, casting one is ONE idempotent function the module side alone holds, and portraits are presentation keyed by identity** (owner report, verbatim: *"there is no need to preserve my current work, its all just for testing up to now. So, if we can make this system more robust from the ground up, i am all for it. A system without exceptions in the code where responsibilities are clear and easy to understand. A refactor that takes the new reality (core mobs were an addition) and works with it. Layered, so that top code does not need to understand the underlying fabric completely but can just use robust functions."*, with the cast path called out as *"Thats actually an important path. Often modules want lets say a zombie, but its old aunt agatha. So, she will have zombie stats but with prose."*). **The felt problem.** Two mobs the owner had spawned showed up as ORPHANS; adopting them and deleting them left two encounter roster entries stuck on a permanent `missing ref`, and the row that broke was not a monster at all — it was an `npc` artifact carrying a hidden `data.monsterChunkId` marker, i.e. a creature wearing an authoring artifact as a disguise. **The defect, MEASURED before the arc:** 67 usages across 17 files asked "is this NPC actually a mob?" (`isMobArtifact` and its callers, `features/campaign/creature-row-guard.ts`, the refill/editor refusals, the entity verdict, auto-promote, the delete cascades), each a place to get the question wrong, and the roster's dangling-ref reason had two spellings so a surface could compare the wrong one and go mute without failing. **Why a guard could not fix it.** Every one of those 67 sites was a correct answer to a bad model; adding a 68th check would have added a 68th way to be wrong. The model, not the checks, was the bug. **What landed (the model).** (1) *Library tier, read-only and addressable*: a creature is a statblock chunk (or a prose chunk the reader derives one from), read through `db/creatureRepo.listLibraryCreatures` / `wikiLinkCreatures` / `publishLibraryCreaturePool`; nothing writes it, and `features/campaign/creature-row-guard.ts` is DELETED rather than emptied, because with no writer there is nothing to guard. A `[[Zombie]]` mention resolves to a DERIVED node (`lib/wikilinks.WikiLinkCreature` + the creature node kind in `domain/wikiGraph.ts`), so the reader is unchanged while the module entity view correctly answers "not detailed" — resolution and DETAIL are different questions (D10). (2) *Authored tier*: an `npc` either has its own stat block, has none, or CITES a library creature through `npcDataSchema.creatureRef` — and the schema REFUSES a citation next to an authored stat block, loudly and by name, so two sources of truth cannot coexist. (3) *Presentation tier*: a portrait belongs to an IDENTITY, not a row — one canonical blob per creature in the global `mobPortraits` table plus one per-campaign row in `creatureImages`, read through the ONE seam `creatureCoverImageId` (presentation row → the cast npc's own cover) and written through `setCreatureCover`; `artWithoutCover` is therefore structurally impossible, because the board and the batch ask the same question. (4) *The cast seam*: `db/creatureRepo.castCreatureAsNpc` is ONE idempotent function — per (campaign, module, name, IDENTITY) it creates once and REUSES afterwards without a write, refuses a same-named rival drawing from a different creature, refuses to cast over an authored npc, refuses a creature the library cannot supply, and stamps the module tag so a cast row is an ordinary module-owned npc to every module-scoped reader; its stats are DERIVED at read time, never stored. (5) *The asymmetry is ABSENCE OF A FUNCTION*: the encounter and sweep sides have no cast seam and the roster schema cannot express the field, so there is nothing to check (pinned in `tests/db/creatureRepo.test.ts`); the module generator and the bestiary spawn dialog are the only callers. (6) *Dexie v20* migrates the old rows — portrait slots re-keyed to `chunk:<id>`, citations rewritten onto the library, marked creature rows retired, the presentation table created — through ONE loud idempotent repair seam in the `version()` chain (`db/creatureRepair.repairCreatureCitations`), reporting its outcome in settings rather than migrating silently. **What the arc found that the brief had wrong.** The refusal the old model had in `changeArtifact`/`runEngine` survives for a CAST row on a reason the old code could not state: `castCreatureAsNpc` is idempotent per NAME, so a writer that renamed the row would let the generator cast a second one; a refill (which preserves the name and rewrites prose) is LEGAL and is exactly the owner's path. Two further real defects were found by chasing the failing pins, not by reading: `enumerateBatchKinds` read a cast npc's own cover as MISSING (the batch would have overwritten the owner's art), and `castCreatureAsNpc` never stamped the module tag (a spawned creature was invisible to every module-scoped predicate). **Not yet done, deliberately:** the module generator does not yet EXPOSE the cast field (`moduleEntityKindSchema` has no optional creature slot, so no module text can ask for Aunt Agatha) — the seam it needs, with its idempotency and refusals, is in place and pinned; the wiring is the next slice. |
| 107 | **The module generator can ASK for a cast: an entity record carries an optional bestiary slot, the spine clause offers it, and finalize casts through the ONE function** (owner intent, verbatim: *"Thats actually an important path. This path should be easily available for mob generation (inside the module generator mainly, i think the encounter generated mobs wont need it, they should not introduce important NPCs on their own)."*). **The felt problem.** Row 106 gave casting ONE idempotent implementation and two possible holders — the bestiary spawn dialog (a user action) and "the module generator" — but the generator could not actually ask for one: nothing in the module-generation contract could express "this NPC borrows a library creature's stats", so the owner's Aunt Agatha case was reachable only by hand, from a different screen, after the module was written. **What landed (the model, three hops with one owner each).** (1) *The contract* — `domain/module.entityBestiarySlotSchema` (`{ creature, book? }`: the creature's name as the library spells it, `canonicalCreatureName`'s innermost heading, plus the book's title only when two books share a name) as an OPTIONAL field on the entity record (`moduleEntityKindSchema.bestiary`, additive as strictly as a field can be: a row written before it parses with NO key, `undefined` means "no cast", and the model's own `"bestiary": null` — how the strict JSON contract spells "absent" — reads the same). The spine's emitted contract (`moduleGen.spineReplySchema`, now exported) carries the key REQUIRED-and-nullable, because the strict subset has no "optional": the decoder must answer it and `null` is its "none". The request SURVIVES the name-normalization substitution by `withEntityBestiarySlots` (matched by the record's own name and every name it absorbed, refusing loudly rather than picking when two source records answer one canonical with different creatures) — without it, the normalized records that REPLACE `module.entityKinds` would have dropped the planner's own request. (2) *The prompt* — `llm/promptStyles.spineEntityKindsClause` appends the clause to the entity-kind bullet (the place the prompt describes what an NPC entity is), and renders it ONLY when `db/creatureRepo.listLibraryCreatures()` is non-empty, so a workspace with no bestiary composes the PRE-CHANGE prompt byte for byte — MEASURED, not remembered: the new test runs the real `runSpine` over the same module row the pre-style golden was captured from and compares character for character against `tests/fixtures/promptStyles/spine-classic-default.txt`, then proves the delta with a library is exactly `spineEntityKindsClause(true) − spineEntityKindsClause(false)`. (3) *Finalize* — `features/modules/entity-batch` reads the slot off the module row (`domain/module.bestiarySlotForEntity`), resolves the NAME to a library citation (`libraryCitationForEntity`, over the ONE stat-block pool the wiki-link resolver and bestiary browser already read), and casts through `db/creatureRepo.castCreatureAsNpc` — never by writing `creatureRef` at a call site. A cast entity starts NO persona run: the creature's numbers are the library's and the prose is the module's own paragraphs about her (`surroundingParagraphs` over `moduleDocumentText`), so the result is ONE module-owned `npc` carrying her name, that prose and a `creatureRef`, with `statBlock: null` (the pair `npcDataSchema` refuses by name) and an empty `modelUsed` — a person wrote that text, not a model. **What the cast does when it succeeds, and each refusal (the owner's terms).** SUCCESS: she exists as a real NPC with her own name and the module's prose, her numbers derived from the library creature, reusable by the table exactly like any authored npc; a SECOND run over the same module REUSES that row (`castCreatureAsNpc`'s idempotency, untouched) rather than minting a twin. REFUSALS, all loud, all named, all landing in the batch's existing `failed[]` (the convention the panel and the module automation toast — never a silent skip, never console-only): the library holds no creature of that name; two creatures share the name and the slot named no book (both candidates listed with their books, and the field to disambiguate named); the named book holds no creature of that name; one canonical entity asked for two different creatures. In every case NOTHING is written — no statless twin, no half-cast row — and the prose is never silently dropped. **The asymmetry stays structural.** The request is the GENERATOR's alone: the encounter-generation contracts (the Smith draft, the Cartographer brief) carry no `bestiary` and no `cast` property anywhere, `runEngine`/`encounterRoster` name neither `castCreatureAsNpc` nor `bestiarySlotForEntity`, and the batch's cast branch is guarded to `kind === 'npc'` — so the encounter side is not told not to cast, it CANNOT SPEAK IT. `tests/llm/moduleGen-cast.test.ts` (16) extends the roster-side data-schema pin in `tests/db/creatureRepo.test.ts` rather than restating it. **UNPROVEN (explicit).** (a) The PROSE QUALITY of a cast row is unmeasured: this arc takes the module's own paragraphs around the mention (`surroundingParagraphs`) rather than spending a model call on prose, so an entity the finished text never actually wrote a scene for gets a body that is only her name; the alternative — running the entity persona for the prose and then casting that text in — is deliberately NOT built (two artifacts for one entity), and it is the first thing to revisit if the owner finds the prose thin.
