| 108 | **The MODULE is the document and deliverables are gone: one PDF renderer reads the module (premise, part plan, parts) plus its artifacts, GM and Player are ONE code path with the audience as an explicit option, and a map plate is printed only where a map IMAGE exists** (owner's intent, verbatim: *"I think this can be completely simplified, no need to have 'deliverables' at all, just export decisions if needed."* and *"Encounter maps should obviously be part of the PDF. They need to be included at the right places."*). **The felt problem.** "Deliverables" were a SECOND document the owner had to author, seed, store, outline and keep in sync with a module that already contained the same text — a stored artifact whose only job was to be a copy of something else, and the copy could drift (the module's prose changed, the deliverable's outline did not, and nothing said so). Print was the second half of the same problem: the PDF was generated from the deliverable rather than from the module. **What landed (the simplification).** (1) *The concept is DELETED end to end*: the `Deliverable` domain shape, `deliverableRepo`, the page, its nav entry, its route, the outline-seeding path, and every `deliverables` reference in the export/import, backup, maintenance, census, orphan-sweep and auto-promote seams. (2) *Data compatibility is NOT required, but nothing breaks silently* (AGENTS rules 1–2): `RETIRED_EXPORT_TABLES` names the retired table, `retiredTableRows` counts its rows in the RAW file BEFORE tolerant parsing, and the count is REPORTED to the owner on import (backup section and campaign picker) as *"Skipped N rows from the retired \"deliverables\" table — that table is gone (a module PDF is generated from the module itself now), so those rows had nowhere to land. Everything else imported."* — an old file imports everything it can and says exactly what it dropped. The Dexie side is the loud twin: v21 drops the table and records its row count in `settings.deliverablesRemoved`, which `AppShell` toasts once and resets (the `retiredSessionNotesRemoved` precedent — an upgrade body runs before React exists and cannot toast). (3) *PDF generation MOVES to the module* (`src/lib/modulePdf.ts`): the input is the module row plus the artifact pool, the body is assembled from `spine.premise`, the part plan and `parts` through the SAME `assembleModulePartsDocument`/`splitPartsDocument` seam the canvas edits, so the `==========`/`[Part n of total]` scaffolding a stored document carries can never reach a page; GM and Player come out of ONE builder with `audience` as an explicit option (what differs is the audience rule, never a second renderer); the export is offered where the module lives — the canvas header and the campaign tree's module-group header — through ONE component (`ModulePdfButton`), so the two entry points cannot print different books; the Deliverables page, route and nav entry are gone while the single-artifact GM/handout exports in the tree stay as they are (row 105's subject). (4) *The audit's defects are pinned, not narrated*: a map plate is printed from `encounter.data.mapImageId` and `board.mapImageId` AT ITS ANCHOR (inside the encounter, not in an appendix) and a battle with NO stored map image prints NO plate — no schematic, no geometry, no placeholder drawn in its place (owner decision; the `layout` a row may carry is never rendered); maps decode at `PDF_MAP_MAX_LONG_EDGE = 4096` (the map pipeline's own ceiling) while covers stay at 1024; an image that cannot be read or decoded prints a NAMED placeholder AND appears in the returned `problems` list, which the export reports; location/event data (`locationType`, `inhabitants`, `pointsOfInterest`, `hooks`) renders; roster origins render for `npc-ref` (a cross-reference to the row) and name-only entries (the named missing-ref reason via `isMissingRefOrigin`), and a `rulebook` citation keeps its `(see Bestiary)` form. **Where it lives.** `src/lib/modulePdf.ts` (the renderer), `src/lib/pdfImages.ts` (the image seam: `loadPdfImages` never throws, records a `PdfImageFailure` per id, and `assertPdfmakeImageDataUrl` fails LOUDLY on a format pdfmake cannot embed), `src/features/modules/module-pdf-button.tsx`, `src/db/db.ts` (v21), `src/lib/exportImport.ts` + `src/lib/backup.ts` (the retired-table report), spec in `docs/07-MILESTONE-3.md §M3-D`, seams in `docs/18-ARCHITECTURE.md §2.3`. **Rationale.** A document that is DERIVED cannot drift from its source, and a derived document needs no storage, no outline, no seeding and no census; where the old shape had a second authored copy of the module, the new one has a render function over the row that already exists. Deleting a stored concept is exactly where silence is most dangerous, so both deletion boundaries (an old export file, an old database) report what they dropped instead of swallowing it. **Status.** Landed. Reversible at the cost of the schema/format work above (an export written by this build carries no `deliverables` key; a v21 database has no table to write). |
| 109 | **The module PDF's STRUCTURE is authored by a model as DATA — the model writes the PLAN, the deterministic renderer writes the PAGES** (owner's intent, verbatim: *"The more i think about this the more i think we should have an actual LLM author for this, to make this good. Just for structural decisions. If we do this purely procedural, i think this will look bad often."*; and *"there is no need to have 'deliverables' at all, just export decisions if needed."*; and *"A real module has the module text and things like explanations and such in a sidebar. While i do like this format i think it would not work well for us since our explanations are pretty beefy and our module text is kind of slim."*). **The felt problem.** Row 108 made the module the document, but every book it printed had the SAME shape — cover, premise, part plan, parts, one chapter per kind, gallery, ledger — so the renderer's fixed sequence was doing the authoring. The owner's modules do not look like that: the module text is slim and the explanations are beefy, so the hardcover convention (module prose in the flow, explanations in a sidebar) cannot be ported by a procedural rule that does not know what each section IS. He asked for a model for the STRUCTURAL decisions and nothing else. **The ratified boundary (the whole arc is this sentence).** The LLM authors the PLAN; the deterministic renderer authors the PAGES. A re-export must be diffable, and the AI's decisions must be visible and correctable — so the plan is DATA on the module row, never prose and never renderer code. **The plan (what a model may decide, and nothing more).** `moduleDocumentPlanSchema` (zod, STRICT) is an ordered list of sections; each section carries a `title`, ONE role from a CLOSED set, an `audience`, a `source` naming something that EXISTS, and `images` — ids of images the module already holds. The `source` is a union of exactly three shapes: a part of the part plan by its STABLE `planIndex` (`-1` = the premise), an owned/mentioned artifact by id, or an encounter by id. **A plan may only NAME things that exist** (`documentPlanIssues`): a missing part index, a premise on a module with none, an artifact the module neither owns nor mentions, a non-encounter named as an encounter, or an image the document cannot load is a LOUD, NAMED failure (`the plan's section "…" — …`) that refuses the WHOLE plan — never a section silently dropped to make a plan fit. **The role vocabulary is four, and closed**: `explanation` (the body: explanatory prose plus the row's structured data), `read-aloud` (the filled read-aloud box), `gm-note` (the labeled GM box), `aside` (a small, indented, muted parenthetical insert that is NOT a chapter — no page break, no ToC entry). A role changes TREATMENT, never content, and the renderer decides every typographic property of it: the plan cannot smuggle in a font, a colour, a pdfmake node or a raw blob (the schema is strict — an unexpected key is a REFUSAL, and there is no field a renderer would have to ignore). **GM and player both exist from the start, and every element declares its audience.** `audience` is `all \| gm \| player`; the plan's declared audience decides a section's PLACEMENT, so the kind defaults (`note`/`plotarc` GM-only, the rest shared) are the DEFAULTS it may override — visibly, in the plan the owner inspects — not a second authority. FIELD-level GM material is still keyed on the DOCUMENT audience (faction `methods`, encounter `tactics`/`treasure`/`terrain`, PC `notes`, the part plan, the treasure ledger), because that is the leak boundary: a section declared `all` prints for players WITHOUT its GM fields. Maps stay in both documents. **Loud fallback, in two places, with the whole plan refused.** A stored plan that cannot be applied (schema-invalid, or a stale reference after the owner deleted a row) prints the PROCEDURAL document, pushes a problem at `the document plan` into the export's `problems` list, and states it IN the document on its own page — "This document was laid out from the procedural outline: the module's document plan could not be applied — … Regenerate it from the module's "Document plan" surface". The export still lands; NEVER a half-applied plan. An ABSENT plan is not a fallback and not an error: it is the normal pre-plan state and renders the procedural outline silently. **Determinism (measured, not asserted).** The RENDERER NEVER CALLS A MODEL — export applies the persisted plan only, so the same `(module, plan)` renders the same book: two renders of the same definition are byte-identical (fixture definition 6359 characters) and two full PDF builds with a pinned `compiledAt` are byte-identical (51271 bytes, first differing byte `-1`). That required pinning the compile date, because pdfkit derives the trailer `/ID` from `info.creationDate` (`md5(CreationDate.getTime() + info)`), so two unpinned builds differ in the trailer and nowhere else. **Where the decision is made (and what it is told).** `src/llm/modulePlan.ts` is the ONE planner seam: it claims the module through the shared canvas-busy registry, refuses a module with no part plan, sends the scoped artifact inventory plus the image inventory (ids, kinds, names, one-line summaries — NOT full prose), parses the reply through `parseJsonReply` + zod AT THE BOUNDARY (an invalid JSON, a wrong shape or an invented id fails LOUD and the caller writes nothing), and stamps provenance from the reply's own `modelUsed` into the plan. **Where it lives.** `src/domain/documentPlan.ts` (schema, role vocabulary, reference check, `readStoredDocumentPlan`), `src/llm/modulePlan.ts` (the seam + prompt), `src/lib/modulePdf.ts` (`resolveDocumentPlan` + the section renderers), `src/domain/module.ts` (`documentPlan`), `src/features/modules/module-plan-dialog.tsx` (the surface), mounted next to `ModulePdfButton` in the canvas header; spec in `docs/07-MILESTONE-3.md §M3-D`, seam in `docs/18-ARCHITECTURE.md §2.3/§4`. **Persistence.** `modules.documentPlan` is an ADDITIVE OPTIONAL field read parse-on-read as `z.unknown()` (the mapMode/canvas precedent) — NO Dexie version bump and no index change (`modules` is index-only), ONE write site (the planner seam's caller) and ONE reader (`readStoredDocumentPlan`), and it rides backup/export because it is a module row field (`tests/features/module-plan-dialog.test.tsx` round-trips it through `buildCampaignExport`/`importExport`). Stored UNVALIDATED on purpose: a corrupt plan must never brick the module row — it reads as `invalid` and the export falls back loudly. **The surface is an INSPECTOR, not a builder**: it lists the sections in order with their title, role, audience, source (named in the owner's terms) and image-anchor count, shows the model that decided them (`WriterModelId`), and offers Regenerate (absent → "Generate plan", invalid → "Replace it"). There is NO drag-and-drop, no tree, no add/remove/reorder, and no per-node styling. The ONE per-section control is the AUDIENCE, because that is the decision that changes what a player may read. A failed regeneration toasts by name and leaves the previous plan EXACTLY as it was; nothing is ever part-written. **Rationale.** A fixed sequence cannot know that one of these modules needs its explanation before its read-aloud and another needs the opposite; a model can, and the owner can SEE and re-roll that judgement. Keeping the plan as validated data (instead of letting a model write the document) is what preserves the two properties he asked for: re-export stays diffable, and a bad decision is a visible, correctable plan rather than an unreproducible page. **UNPROVEN (explicit).** (a) The planner is NEVER run against a live provider by a test and no live run was observed: every test mocks `chat` at the protocol boundary, so whether a real model plans WELL from the names/kinds/summaries inventory is unmeasured — and the inventory deliberately carries no full prose, which may be the first thing to revisit if the owner finds the plans repetitive. (b) The AESTHETIC quality of a plan is unjudged (no human evaluation, no fixture golden plan from a live model). (c) A plan declaring a GM-tagged row `all` puts it in the PLAYER document by design (the override is visible in the plan and the field-level leak boundary still holds), but nothing verifies that the prose a model chooses to share is appropriate to share. (d) The plan can neither SUPPRESS back matter nor reorder the back matter (the gallery and the ledger are renderer-owned and always follow the body), and it cannot ask for a page break or a layout of its own. (e) Byte-determinism holds per `(module, plan, compiledAt)` — the compile DAY is printed on the cover, so a rebuild tomorrow differs on purpose, and no cross-machine byte comparison was done. (f) The image-decode limits of row 108 are unchanged (jsdom has no canvas). | `src/domain/documentPlan.ts`; `src/llm/modulePlan.ts`; `src/lib/modulePdf.ts` (`resolveDocumentPlan`, `plannedSectionContent`, the four role renderers); `src/domain/module.ts` (`documentPlan`); `src/features/modules/module-plan-dialog.tsx`; `src/features/modules/canvas/CanvasPage.tsx`; `tests/domain/documentPlan.test.ts` (20); `tests/llm/modulePlan.test.ts` (11); `tests/lib/modulePdfPlan.test.ts` (18); `tests/features/module-plan-dialog.test.tsx` (10); docs/07 §M3-D; docs/18 §2.3/§4; docs/08 §The document plan | A model is good at deciding what a book needs and bad at deciding what a page looks like deterministically; the split gives the owner the first without giving up the second. Storing the decision as validated data is what makes it inspectable, correctable, diffable across exports and cheap to re-roll — and the loud fallback keeps a bad plan from ever printing a half-made book. | active |
| 110 | **A module row's `status: 'generating'` is a LEASE and the stream watchdog measures LIVENESS — a healthy stream is no longer killed by a backgrounded tab, and a page that dies mid-generation is reconciled LOUDLY instead of spinning forever** (owner report, verbatim: *"When i use the app and create a module, then switch to another browser app, i get the feeling that it gets stalled easily. I heard that background tabs can get that treatment. Since generating a module is taking pretty long, this is pretty normal user behaviour. Can we do something to make the browser still give the app the needed resources?"*). **What was measured first, because the report mixed two different problems.** (1) A merely HIDDEN tab keeps generating: every rAF in this app is a display-only coalescer and the run/part loops are promise-driven, so switching apps stops nothing. (2) What a hidden\|frozen page DOES break is the 1 Hz WALL-CLOCK watchdog in `llm/openrouter.readStream`: it compared `Date.now()` deltas, so the suspended gap counted as silence and it called `reader.cancel()` on a healthy stream — and after a resume the post-loop diagnosis (:637-659) re-derived the same failure from the same clocks EVEN AFTER A CLEAN `done` close (`if (done) break` at :607 falls straight through into it), so a provider that ends the body instead of sending `[DONE]` had its COMPLETE answer thrown away as `content-stall`\|`max-duration`. (3) The WORST case was a discarded\|reloaded tab: `runRepo.failRunningRuns` reconciles RUN rows at boot, but NOTHING reconciled a MODULE row, so a module mid-generation stayed `'generating'` FOREVER — a permanent spinner, a Stop button that was a silent no-op (`cancelModuleGen` on a module with no live controller is `controllers.get(id)?.abort()`), every retry affordance gated behind `!busy`, and "Stop all generations" counting that dead row as stopped and toasting that it had stopped it. **The status is a LEASE, and the fix names the guard.** `llm/moduleGenReconcile` is the ONE place that decides whether a `'generating'` row is owned: `moduleGen.hasLiveModuleGen(id)` (THIS page's controller registry) OR a held Web Lock named `campaigner:generation:module-gen:<id>` (the cross-tab lease, `lib/generationLocks`). App START reconciles every row that says `'generating'` and nobody owns: the row lands `'failed'` with a NAMED sentence (`INTERRUPTED_MODULE_GEN_MESSAGE`: the page writing it is gone, finished parts are untouched, unfinished ones are back to pending, press "Resume module generation"), every part slot still at `'generating'` rewinds to `'pending'`, and the batch entry point toasts the count. Nothing is reset silently, nothing already written is touched, and the guard is RE-EVALUATED INSIDE the write transaction (`moduleRepo.failInterruptedModuleGen` takes the predicate), so a pass that registers between the read and the write cannot be failed. Because the rewind lands the module in exactly the state `generateMissingParts` already recovers from, the recovery path needed no new machinery (pinned end to end: the rewound part is written by `generateMissingParts` and the finished part is not re-run) and the reader's own `!busy` gate opens with its failed banner + **Resume module generation** + **Generate missing parts** enabled. **Start is the load-bearing moment, and that was a second defect.** The three boot calls (`failRunningRuns`, `seedBuiltInPersonas`, `ensurePersistentStorage`) sat in `AppShell`'s RENDER BODY, so EVERY re-render — a theme toggle, the help dialog, any store change — re-ran them, and `failRunningRuns` fails every `'running'` row: an unrelated UI change could fail a live run. They are a mount effect now, and the module reconciliation runs there too (a discarded tab RELOADS, so it gets no `visibilitychange` at all). The visibility path is the second chance, not the main one: on `visibilitychange`\|`freeze`\|`resume`\|`pagehide`\|`pageshow` the page records the SUSPENDED GAP (`lib/pageLiveness`) and reconciles module rows again — deliberately NOT runs, because a hidden tab keeps running its engine and failing those rows would invent the very defect this row removes. **Every Stop control tells the truth, and "Stop all" counts only work it did.** `moduleGenReconcile.stopModuleGeneration` is the ONE behaviour behind the reader's Stop and the board's Stop: a live controller in this page → `cancelModuleGen` (a real abort, unchanged); another tab holds the generation lock → nothing is stopped and nothing is failed, and it SAYS SO ("Another tab is generating this module — press Stop there."); nobody owns the row → it is reconciled (real work: the loud failed state + rewind); the row already settled → it says that too. Silence is the bug in all four cases. `features/progress/stop-all-generations` keeps its exact count of DISTINCT stopped units but now asks the same guard: a live forge is cancelled and counted, an unclaimed row is reconciled and counted SEPARATELY (`{ stopped, reconciled }`, the reconciled rows toasted as the loud interrupted report), and when nothing was stopped but a dead row was reconciled it no longer claims "Nothing was running" (pinned: the row is failed with its recovery sentence and `cancelModuleGen` is never called). **The watchdog now measures liveness, and reports only a limit it ARMED.** `readStream` computes its three deltas through `lib/pageLiveness.activeElapsedMs` (wall time MINUS the gaps the page reported), and the post-loop diagnosis fires only for the limit the watchdog actually tripped (`trippedLimit`), so a clean close after a long silence returns the accumulated text. Both directions are pinned, because the cheap wrong fix here is a looser limit: a gap far beyond the content-stall limit does NOT kill a healthy stream (which then completes normally), a clean close after a ten-minute suspension returns the COMPLETE answer, and a stream that really died STILL fails — after the resume, on the same 2s\|3s limits, as `stalled after 2s of silence` — plus the keep-alive-only case that must still trip `content-stall`. Nothing about the limits themselves moved. **Two cheap, documented freeze mitigations.** (1) Every generation PASS (spine, parts, post-generation sweep) runs inside `withGenerationLock(moduleGenLockName(id), …)`: Chromium's published freeze criteria list "the page holds a Web Lock" as an opt-out, and the same lock is the cross-tab lease above. It is ADVISORY, NEVER A DEPENDENCY: with no Web Locks API the work runs directly, an unavailable lock still runs the work (a second tab generating the same module was already possible and must not become a new failure mode), and nothing branches on whether it was granted. (2) `lib/backgroundTitle` writes `document.title` while the tab is HIDDEN only — `Working: <label> — Campaigner`, `✓ Finished: …`, `⚠ Failed: …` (failed outranks finished outranks running, extras counted as `(+N more)`) — because the tab strip is the only surface a backgrounded page has, and a page that keeps producing observable output is not the page the freeze heuristics pick. A USER STOP reaches no verdict: the entry is cleared, so the title can never invent "finished". **The wait for a run rides the ROW, not a timer.** `waitForRunStatus` slept in chained 250 ms `setTimeout` calls; Chromium throttles a hidden page's timers to about one wake-up a MINUTE, and a chain or batch step boundary is exactly where this is awaited — the shape of the owner's report. It now awaits `runRepo.waitForRunRowChange` (a Dexie `liveQuery` over the run row, resolving on any change or on the row disappearing), with the abort semantics and the "row disappeared" contract preserved to the character, and it is pinned structurally: NO 250 ms timer is scheduled while the wait is pending. **A completed run keeps no stale interruption message.** The step-outcome write now clears `errorMessage`\|`failureKind` along with restoring `'running'`, so a run the old render-body `failRunningRuns()` had wrongly marked failed is LIVE and truthful again at its next step — and the completion write, the in-loop cancel and `cancel(runId)` clear it too. The residue was a `'completed'` row still reading "Interrupted by reload". MEASURED, and reported rather than rounded up: the step write's clearing is injection-proven (removing it fails the pin), while the completion write's is REDUNDANT today — removing it leaves the suite green, because every path to completion passes a step write — and is kept, with a comment saying so, so a future path that completes without a step write cannot resurrect the defect. **NOT built, deliberately (the brief's own exclusions, upheld).** No partial-stream journaling and no Web Worker orchestration: the owner's ask is that the browser keep giving the app resources, and a journal would be a new storage seam with its own reconciliation problem, while a Worker would move the same timers into another thread that is throttled and frozen identically. The module PDF\|export\|plan seam and the creature\|cast arcs are untouched. **Honest limits.** (1) NO test in this repo can FREEZE a page or throttle its timers — jsdom has no freeze, no discard and no timer throttling — so the freeze MITIGATION is documented (Chromium's published opt-out criteria) and the gap crediting is pinned by driving the very events the browser fires (`visibilitychange`\|`freeze`\|`resume`), NOT by observing a real freeze: that half of the owner's original question stays UNPROVEN in tests by construction, and docs/08-TESTING.md says so in the same words. (2) The watchdog timer itself is unchanged (1 Hz), so on a frozen page it wakes up late — now harmless, because the gap is credited and the stream survives — which means a dead stream is still detected, just later than it would be on a visible page. (3) The cross-tab lease is Web Locks, so in a browser WITHOUT that API a second tab cannot see another tab's forge: start-time reconciliation there can fail a row a live other tab is writing. It is self-healing (that pass rewrites the status on its next part boundary and completes normally), but a transient spurious failure is possible and is stated here rather than hidden. (4) The tiny race where a resumed pass registers its controller just after the reconcile's read is self-healing for the same reason. (5) The 250 ms poll is gone from `waitForRunStatus`; the timeouts layered ON it (a chain step's own bound) are a different seam and were not touched. | `src/lib/pageLiveness.ts` (NEW: `notePageSuspended`\|`notePageResumed`\|`activeElapsedMs`\|`suspendedMsWithin`\|`onPageResumed`\|`resetPageLiveness`\|`installPageLiveness`, auto-installed on the four page events); `src/lib/generationLocks.ts` (NEW); `src/lib/backgroundTitle.ts` (NEW); `src/llm/moduleGenReconcile.ts` (NEW: `INTERRUPTED_MODULE_GEN_MESSAGE`, `isModuleGenClaimed`, `reconcileInterruptedModuleGen`/`s`, `formatInterruptedModuleGenReport`, `stopModuleGeneration`); `src/llm/openrouter.ts` (active-time deltas + `trippedLimit` + the diagnosis); `src/llm/moduleGen.ts` (additive `hasLiveModuleGen`, the pass wrappers holding the lock + the title entry, `errorMessage: ''` on the step write); `src/llm/runEngine.ts` (`waitForRunStatus` on the row observable, the background entry per run, the cleared messages on every settle path); `src/db/runRepo.ts` (`waitForRunRowChange` + `runRowSignature`); `src/db/moduleRepo.ts` (`listGeneratingModules`, `failInterruptedModuleGen`); `src/features/progress/stop-all-generations.ts` (`{ stopped, reconciled }`); `src/features/modules/ModuleReaderPage.tsx` + `src/features/modules/board/BoardPage.tsx` (ONE stop behaviour); `src/features/modules/post-generation.ts` (the lock around the sweep); `src/app/layout/AppShell.tsx` (mount effect + visibility resume); tests `tests/llm/openrouter-watchdog-gap.test.ts` (5), `tests/llm/moduleGenReconcile.test.ts` (6), `tests/llm/runWait.test.ts` (8), `tests/lib/backgroundTitle.test.tsx` (5), `tests/lib/generationLocks.test.tsx` (6), `tests/features/app-shell-boot-reconcile.test.tsx` (4), plus updated `tests/features/stop-all-generations.test.ts` (3), `tests/features/module-reader-stream-churn.test.tsx`, `tests/features/blocked-reasons.test.tsx`, `tests/features/module-board-rewrite.test.tsx` and `tests/llm/runEngine.test.ts`; docs/18 §2.2/§2.3 + §4, docs/08-TESTING.md §Runs and generations in the background, docs/04 acceptance criteria, docs/05 §Progress dock | The owner's sentence describes ONE felt problem with THREE causes, and the cheap half of the fix is a lie unless the hard half is done: crediting a gap to the watchdog without reconciling dead rows would leave the module spinning forever with no explanation; reconciling rows without a liveness guard would kill a real generation the moment the owner opened a second tab; and moving the boot calls off the render body without a mount effect would drop the reload reconciliation the app already promised. So the arc is ONE rule applied everywhere — the `'generating'` status is a LEASE and every consumer asks the guard — plus the honest measurement of what a background tab really does to this app (hidden is not frozen, and frozen is not discarded). Two choices deliberately refuse the literal ask: the watchdog does NOT get a longer limit (a dead stream must still fail on the same numbers) and the page does NOT fail runs on visibility (a hidden tab is still working; failing those rows would invent the defect being fixed). The freeze mitigation is the cheapest documented one that costs nothing when absent, and the second mitigation — the tab title — answers the half of the owner's report that is a QUESTION: how does he know it finished while he is in another app. | active |

| 68 | **A stopped orchestration must not start its next unit — and a cancel is not a failure** (owner report, verbatim: "Stop all should stop all generations, but it only stops the current type loop"). Intent: one press of Stop all ends the WORK, not merely the units that happen to be running at that instant. Cancelling units was already done; the loops around them kept going, and one of them read a cancelled unit as a finished one. TWO rules close it. **(1) The stop epoch** (`lib/stopEpoch` — ONE app-level counter, bumped by the sweep BEFORE it cancels anything): a loop captures `getStopEpoch()` at entry and asks `stoppedSince(captured)` between units. Consulted at the post-generation kind loop AND before each of its three enqueue blocks; the entity-batch pool's worker entry (remaining targets are never launched); the three parts-pass automation gates; the parts loop's post-pass normalization boundary; and `post-run-extras`' enqueue path (a run that finished after the stop enqueues nothing — an enqueue would restart a cancelled queue's pump). Deliberately an EPOCH and not a sticky flag: a pass that starts after the stop holds the fresh epoch and runs normally, so one Stop all never silently disables the next user-visible generation; direct user actions are not gated at all. The parts tail needed a second signal besides the epoch, because the persisted row cannot express "cancelled": a cancelled parts pass keeps `'ready'` with parts present so Retry stays available — byte-identical to a completed pass, which is exactly how the post-generation sweep started ~1s after a stop. `runParts` therefore returns `aborted` alongside the module and the gates refuse it (the old comment claiming a cancel "leaves the module short of ready" was false against its own code). **(2) A cancelled unit is WITHDRAWN, never a failure**: `runEntityBatch` records a `cancelled` run outcome as withdrawn — no `failed` entry, no red toast, dock says 'Stopped by the user' — mirroring `jobQueue`'s silent `'cancelled'` `JobOutcome`; the pre-fix behaviour ("3 of 5 npcs failed to generate", `"Kael" — run ended cancelled`) was both a lie and console-noise the user never asked for. A stopped forge normalization pass likewise propagates the cancel instead of recording `entityNamesNormalized: false`, and the canvas surfaces skip their error toast when their own controller is aborted. Coverage added in the same landing: the fourth job queue (covers) and the canvas AI turns (the `canvasBusy` abort registry, whose sweep abort reaches BOTH the turn's model signal and the caller's controller — that second leg is what keeps "the user stopped this" rendering as an aborted partial reply instead of a "Chat failed" toast), plus a signal on the forge's entity-normalization call (the last chat call that could outlive a stop). Still NOT covered, by design: PDF builds and backup jobs (no cancel seam), the cross-campaign shared mob-portrait cache worker, queue FAILED retry lists, and paused runs. | `src/lib/stopEpoch.ts` (new); `src/features/progress/stop-all-generations.ts`; `src/features/modules/post-generation.ts`; `src/features/modules/entity-batch.ts`; `src/llm/moduleGen.ts` (`PartsPassResult.aborted`, `throwIfStopped`, signal threaded through `normalizationCall`); `src/features/campaign/post-run-extras.ts`; `src/llm/canvasBusy.ts` (`registerCanvasAbort`, `cancelCanvasGenerations`); `src/llm/canvasChat.ts` / `src/llm/canvasRefine.ts` (`turn` controller); `src/features/modules/canvas/ChatSidebar.tsx` / `CanvasPage.tsx` (abort-aware catch branches); tests/features/stop-orchestration.test.ts + stop-canvas-normalization.test.ts + stop-all-generations.test.ts (count now covers all four queues and the canvas relay) + moduleGen-auto-spine + post-run-extras; docs/18 §2.3 (three seam rows), docs/05 §Progress dock; 65a16fb, 5ae4739, 6ab85f9 | Cancelling units is not stopping work: the loops that drive them are the real orchestration, and a cancelled unit that either returns normally or lands on a failure list is invisible to every guard written against "did it finish?". | 50 | **Canvas CHAT is an XML edit-command protocol with an aider-lineage tolerant ladder; the context ALWAYS pins the CURRENT doc** (chat co-editor arc, owner-directed "a real chat where the LLM can make targeted edits" + "XML for commands"): the assistant replies with prose plus zero-or-more `<edit all="…"><search>…</search><replace>…</replace></edit>` blocks — XML over JSON because nested multi-line prose bodies need no escape dance (JSON escapes are exactly where long replacements err; aider/Cline lineage, Anthropic XML guidance) — and this is the deliberate DEVIATION from canvasRefine's strict-JSON-schema reply (prose+XML is not a JSON shape; no `responseFormat`, validation is the strict extractor + zod boundary). Failures are first-class: every failed command/reply carries a Report-to-LLM button that composes error + failed command + current-text excerpt as a user turn (aider's "N SEARCH/REPLACE blocks failed to match! / Did you mean…" retry loop). Every request re-reads the doc from the CM6 view at send time with an explicit doc-is-current system note (Open Canvas split: chat steers, document is the truth); history is tail-capped to 12 messages with older docs stripped + an omission note (v1 trim policy — a note, not an LLM summary; AMENDED by row 57, owner-overturned 2026-09-09: the cap is deleted — the FULL history rides every request with stale doc blocks stripped and no omission note). | 63 | **Simple undo: durable whole-document versions, snapshotted BEFORE every AI change** (owner-directed, verbatim: "There is no undo in chat right now. I would like to have a simple one. Before each AI change, simply save the whole content in a version. Make an option under versions to clear all previous versions."): a new additive table (`moduleVersions`, Dexie v19, no upgrade fn) holds one row per AI change = the WHOLE parts document BYTE-EXACT as it stood immediately before that change, in the ONE `assembleModulePartsDocument`/`splitPartsDocument` format (spine premise excluded; a restore re-splits against the CURRENT plan, never a second format). ONE shared seam `db/moduleVersionRepo.snapshotModuleVersion(moduleId, source, label)` runs immediately BEFORE the write (never after) on EVERY AI mutation path: the canvas AI saves through `saveWholeModuleDocument` origin 'ai' (editor chat batch, preview-snapshot chat batch, accepted Refine/Rewrite proposal, restore — the `version` arg is REQUIRED there and its absence THROWS, writing nothing), `runParts` at entry (generation, missing-part fill, single-part rewrite/regenerate — the reader's Rewrite and the board's staged rewrite both ride it — plus in-pass floor repairs), every `normalizeModuleEntityNames` pass, and the entity panel's consented apply of stored normalization rewrites. NOT snapshotted: manual typing and hand saves (CM6 history owns hand edits), and the board's Apply/Discard of a staged rewrite (the engine write was already captured at pass entry). DURABLE by decision (survives reload) and explicitly SEPARATE from the session-only per-part ledger, whose "do not persist" contract is unchanged — the opposite of a fix to it; the menu labels both groups so they can never be confused. Labels name what the change was about to do ("Chat: …", "Generate parts", "Rewrite part 2 — <title>: <instruction>", "Normalize entity names", "Restore from <time>"). Bounded at `MODULE_VERSION_CAP` = 25 per module, oldest pruned in the insert transaction, retention STATED in the menu (never a silent cap); one parts pass may legitimately add up to 3 rows (pass + up to two normalization passes). **Restore** validates the snapshot against the current part plan (mismatch → loud refusal, no proposal) then rides the existing whole-document proposal → accept → split-save path — no side-door row write, restored text byte-identical to the snapshot, and the restore snapshots its own pre-restore state so a wrong restore is recoverable; it needs the mounted editor, so in preview it toasts loudly instead of no-oping. **Clear all previous versions** is an AlertDialog-confirmed item in the same menu: clears THAT module's durable version rows only (another module's survive), takes NO snapshot first, leaves the document text, the chat thread and the session ledger untouched, and toasts the count removed. **Clear chat is unchanged and does NOT clear the durable versions** (undo history is not chat state); **every module-delete path now sweeps the module’s durable versions in its own transaction** — `deleteModule` plus the three bulk module deletes (`deleteCampaign`, `removeAllGeneratedContent`, `deleteCampaignWorkspace`) all ride the ONE `moduleVersionRepo.deleteModuleVersionsForModules(moduleIds)` seam with the module ids re-listed in that transaction immediately before the rows go, and the campaign wipes also collect rows whose module row is already gone (`pruneOrphanedModuleVersions` — residue from a build that predates the seam, which no module-keyed sweep can reach). | 69 | **The Party is invisible to module creation** (owner rule, verbatim: "The creator is referring to players in the party. The party should not be visible to module creation."). What the owner saw: generated modules addressed the players' characters by name, because `campaignCastContext` built its "Shared campaign cast (used across modules — reuse these exact names, do not duplicate them)" block from EVERY campaign-scoped row — and a PC is campaign-scoped by definition (`kind: 'pc'`) — which rode the spine and parts prompts next to an "Existing campaign entities" index built from the same unfiltered list, and the very same lists were the candidate sets the name-classification pass resolved against. The fix is ONE domain constant read by every consumer, mirroring row 66's `BULK_REMOVE_EXCLUDED_KINDS` precedent: `MODULE_CREATION_EXCLUDED_KINDS` (`['pc']`) with the predicate/helper `visibleToModuleCreation` / `moduleCreationPool` (`src/domain/artifact.ts`), never a scattered `kind !== 'pc'`. Every MODULE-CREATION artifact list is now the module-creation pool: the cast block, the spine's and parts' "Existing campaign entities" indexes, the spine-time and post-parts normalization artifact indexes, the incremental classification's resolved set, the post-generation batch/image target filters, the entity panel's resolution (its unresolved rows ARE the batch work queue) and the stub popover's hand-typed-name classification. Why the Party is special: a PC is AUTHORED BY THE PLAYERS — it is not campaign setting content the generator may reuse. **Collision rule** (a generated name that happens to equal a PC's): the name maps to itself and becomes a NEW module-owned entity record, batch-generated as the module's OWN artifact (module-tier resolution then wins inside that module's text); the player's row is never touched (no alias, no revision, no scope change) and module creation never quietly binds itself to a party member. The attempt to bind anyway is LOUD through the EXISTING channel, never a new one: a verdict whose canonical is a PC's name fails `validateNormalizationReply` (the canonical must be a listed name, a recorded name or an existing artifact), is retried once with the violation stated, and is then recorded on the module row (`entityNamesNormalized: false` + `entityNormalizationError`) with the panel's Retry owning recovery. Deliberately linking a PC stays available in the stub popover's explicit "Use existing entity…" picker (a user act, not a silent verdict). **Boundary (do not "fix" it):** this is ARTIFACT visibility to generation, not censoring the owner — the campaign premise/description and the module premise are the owner's own prose and stay byte-identical even when they name a party member, the reading surfaces (reader chips, wiki-graph, module chat grounding) still resolve against the FULL pool (`resolveWikiLink` is unchanged), the party-derived LEVEL context (`partyLevelLine`, `PARTY_SIZE`, encounter difficulty is about levels, not players) and the npc-only fixed cast stay exactly as they are, and the battle/play seeding of PCs is untouched. TWO changes, one seam each. **(a) Persisted draft.** Every value the dialog holds — concept included — is saved to the settings row as ONE field, `newModuleDraft`, TAGGED with the campaign it was written in; reopening in that campaign prefills it, a draft written in another campaign is neither prefilled nor overwritten, the save is debounced (500 ms) and FLUSHED when the run starts, when the dialog closes and on unmount, and a footer **Reset to defaults** overwrites it (a prefill with no way out is its own trap). One tagged entry rather than a per-campaign map: a map would add a second record shape that EVERY delete path (`deleteCampaign`, `removeAllGeneratedContent`, `deleteCampaignWorkspace`) would have to sweep — exactly the orphan class this repo has closed twice. The two campaign WIPES therefore KEEP the draft on purpose (it is authored input and retry-after-reset is the entire feature), while `deleteCampaign` clears it because the campaign it is tagged for is gone. A stored draft that no longer validates fails the settings read LOUDLY — never a silent half-prefill. **(b) Editable encounter floor.** The floor is editable as TWO NUMBERS behind the dialog's **Advanced — encounter guardrails** disclosure: an on/off switch and encounters-per-level (integers, default 1). Editing PROSE was explicitly rejected by the owner, and the schema enforces the range (min 0, and an ENABLED floor needs ≥ 1 — only a disabled floor may carry 0). ONE source of truth (`domain/module.encounterFloorGuardrailSchema` + `encounterFloorGuardrailFor`): the same number renders the spine prompt clause, the spine repair retry, the parts-pass per-part share and the floor repair instruction, AND sets the floor gate's thresholds and failure copy — a value can never disagree with the gate it configures. | 74 | **Two DERIVED repair controls on the canvas: "Fix module problems" and "Resume automatic module creation"** (owner requests, verbatim: **"Fix module problems"** — *"only when problems exist"* — and **"Resume automatic module creation"**; on the SCOPE of the first, verbatim: *"This is about the module text, not entities. Neither control is a gate: no runtime check, prompt clause or run state depends on them (docs/18 §2.2/§2.3). **"Fix module problems" = the module TEXT.** Its problem set comes from detectors that ALREADY exist — the module's encounter floor per level band (`countModuleEncounters` + the module's own `encounterFloorGuardrailFor`, the same numbers the generation gate and the in-pass repair judge by) and the module reader's own unresolved-link test (`resolveWikiLink` over the premise and every part; the dashed *"not detailed yet"* chip IS that verdict). **Only the floor is repairable by rewriting text, and the owner's boundary is why:** an unresolved `[[Name]]` is the reader's way of saying an ENTITY is not detailed yet, so it is DETECTED and REPORTED — listed in the same confirmation under "Also detected, not fixed here (entity work)" with its remedy (the entity panel, or the resume control) — and never rewritten; fuzzy/heuristic name matching for a phantom link is forbidden here (a variant of an existing name is the normalization pass's job, judged by the model). Consequently the control APPEARS only when a rewrite can fix something (`hasRewritableProblems`): turning it on because entities are missing would be entity work under a text label, and it would open a dialog with nothing to rewrite. The rewrite rides the EXISTING repair seam — `generatePart` with the floor repair's instruction and the escalated repair model — scoped to the failing check, **ONE attempt per part per invocation**, no retry loop, no candidate slate, no "improve the prose" behaviour; a `snapshotModuleVersion` row is written BEFORE the attempt ("allow rewriting with snapshot": the snapshot precedes the ATTEMPT, so a failed attempt leaves a restorable, unchanged version rather than no version — no rewrite may ever run without a prior recorded version), the name-normalization pass re-runs so the counted number can actually move, and afterwards the module is `ready` (one success toast) or `failed` with the floor's own message and a loud refusal to fake success; a part whose call threw has its pre-repair text restored BYTE-IDENTICALLY and is reported, never silently patched. **"Resume automatic module creation" = the entities/images/maps/portraits the owner asked creation to automate.** The target state is RECORDED (`automationIntent`, row 71); the DEVIATION is derived, and every target list is the sweep's OWN (`batchTargets`, `imageTargets`, `encountersNeedingMaps`, `encountersNeedingMobPortraits`) over the sweep's OWN pool (the module-creation pool — the Party is invisible to module creation, row 69), plus `unclassifiedModuleNames` for names the text picked up with no recorded type and the closed `entityNamesNormalized` gate, which silently blocks every entity batch. That is deliberate: the confirmation then names exactly the work the sweep would do — it can neither promise work the sweep would skip nor hide work it would run. **The resume is ADDITIVE by construction**: the work is the EXISTING post-generation sweep, whose every step targets only what is missing, so nothing that already exists is re-generated, re-detailed or overwritten and the prose is never touched; it may run two EXISTING passes first, each for a concrete reason (incremental classification; name normalization when the gate is closed), and if that gate cannot be reopened it refuses LOUDLY and runs NOTHING rather than half-running (a sweep with the gate closed would generate nothing silently). It captures the stop epoch at entry and asks `stoppedSince` before each unit, so **"Stop all" during a resume ends it where it is** ("a stopped orchestration must not start its next unit", row 68), and the sweep keeps its own capture for a stop landing mid-sweep. A module created before the intent existed has nothing to resume (legacy rows stay INERT — intent is never inferred from what the engine did); a row whose automation fields have drifted from the recorded intent is refused loudly rather than guessed at; a resume with nothing missing is a no-op with no call, no write, no enqueue and no toast. **Deliberately NOT built:** any new runtime gate or check over prose (pacing, fairness, "is the story conflicted", ratios/cardinality — the row 72/73 boundary holds), any stored deviation flag, entity/image work under "Fix module problems", autonomous or background execution of either control, a second repair path or a second automation pipeline, and any change to the encounter floor (schema, resolver, message and goldens untouched). The two controls answer two different questions and are documented as such, including the one honest nuance: only the FLOOR is a machine-checkable problem in this repo, so "Fix module problems" is precisely the floor-shortfall repair until a future pure check in the floor's family is PROPOSED and ratified. **Reported to the owner as a brief-level critique (accepted into this implementation):** the brief listed unresolved wiki-links as a *minimum detector* for the fix control while the owner's own sentence puts entities outside it — resolved by DETECTING and REPORTING them there while keeping them off both the rewrite list and the visibility rule, with the entity half on its own control. | 79 | **A streaming tick re-renders its own card, not the document** (owner report, verbatim: "1 thing in the module reader: Scrolling this is slow and jumpy, as if its getting rendered fresh and complete for each tick."). MEASURED, in a real browser (headless Chrome over CDP against the dev server, 12 parts × ~4 KB), because the symptom has two very different possible causes and only one of them was real: BEFORE, every token tick re-parsed every part body plus the premise — 200 ticks cost 13,578 ms of main-thread task time (one 50–115 ms long task per tick), scrolling WHILE generating ran 51.1 ms median frames with 32,134 DOM mutations; AFTER the fix, 200 ticks cost 520 ms with ZERO long tasks and 134 mutations, and scroll-while-streaming is 16.7 ms median = idle parity. REFUTED as the cause: idle scrolling was already 16.8 ms median with no long tasks, so there was no unvirtualized-DOM/CSS/image cost — and with idle already at frame rate, virtualization would have bought nothing while breaking `ReaderSearch`'s walk of the rendered text (which is why it was rejected). The fix is structural, not a tuning pass: `features/modules/streamTails.ts` is the ONE external store for reader tails (`moduleGenEvents` → value-diffed frozen snapshots, the board store's slice pattern) read through `useStreamTail`/`useSyncExternalStore` by the streaming card alone, with ONE `ModuleGenTailsBridge` subscribing for the screen; `PartBody`/`IntroBlock`/`StreamingTail`/`WikiMarkdown` are memoized on real inputs and the page passes stable identities. A SECOND, independently found churn source is pinned: an unmemoized `WikiMarkdown` rebuilt every markdown tree on ANY page re-render (e.g. hiding the contents sidebar). The tails' `done` event is a deliberate no-op — the generator emits it in a `finally` AFTER the row writes, so clearing there repainted the empty placeholder for one frame. TEST HONESTY: the committed pins prove RENDER CHURN only (that a part's markdown tree is not rebuilt) and say so in their header — jsdom has no layout, paint or scrolling, so the smoothness claim rests on the browser measurement, not on the suite; the scroll numbers are not covered by CI. | `src/features/modules/streamTails.ts` (new); `src/features/modules/ModuleReaderPage.tsx`; `src/features/campaign/components/wiki-markdown.tsx`; `tests/features/module-reader-stream-churn.test.tsx` (new; revert-proof — reverting the three source files fails both churn pins); docs/18 §2.3 + §3 | The emitter must never also be the subscription: `moduleGenEvents` is per-token and in-memory, so any page-level listener turns every token into a document-wide re-render. | 102 | **Both AI actions work in the RENDERED preview: a DOM selection maps back to SOURCE byte-exactly or refuses by name — and the wash that re-parsed markdown slices was silently deleting whitespace** (owner report, verbatim: *"In the module chat, 'refine selection' and 'rewrite part' does not seem to work at all. I guess it was part of the raw text version that we abandoned. I would like to make those work again, in the nicely rendered version."*). **The diagnosis, verified before anything was built.** Both actions were implemented, pinned and correct — `tests/features/module-canvas.test.tsx` drives them end to end — but their gate was `disabled={aiBlocked || previewOpen}` and the canvas OPENS in Preview (`previewStore` `openByModule[moduleId] ?? true`), so in the view the owner actually works in both were dead; row 98 gave that state a perceivable reason, and this row REMOVES the reason by removing the gate (the honest rungs — generating / a refine in flight / an open proposal — stay, single-sourced through the one `busyReason` helper, and `PREVIEW_AI_ACTIONS_REASON` is deleted with its last consumer, pinned by a source scan). A whole-part proposal cannot exist while the preview is open (the preview toggle is disabled while `suggestions.length > 0`), so the un-gating needed no proposal path in preview. **What a rendered selection needed, and why geometry was refused.** "Refine selection" must name an EXACT source span; in the preview that span comes from a DOM selection over rendered HTML. `getBoundingClientRect`/`caretRangeFromPoint` were rejected — jsdom implements neither, so the mapping would be unpinnable, and they are wrong across the highlight and lazy layout anyway. The mechanism is an OPT-IN source map: `remarkSourceSpans` wraps every mdast text node outside links in a `<span data-md-from data-md-to>` carrying that run's byte range, and `resolveSelectionRange(partText, start, end)` is its inverse — the ONE place a rendered selection becomes source offsets. It runs BEFORE `remarkWikiLinks` because that plugin REPLACES any text node holding a `[[token]]` (a marker on the text node would vanish exactly where the map matters most, next to a chip; the wrapper survives and its children ARE the split pieces). The proof of exactness is byte-exactness: the resolver rebuilds the run's pieces (its text nodes, and each chip's byte-exact `data-wiki-raw` `[[…]]` token) and REQUIRES them to concatenate to exactly the source slice the run claims — a run that no longer reproduces its source (an escape, an entity, a trimmed line) REFUSES rather than being mapped through a transform. Refusals are named, never guessed (no clamping, no rounding, never the chip's display label): a point strictly inside a wiki chip says a chip is replaced as its whole token, a cross-part selection says one span inside one part, an unmapped span (inline code, an image, the unwritten-part placeholder) says so, an empty selection says select first, and a capture measured against an OLDER document says select again. **Where the capture lives, and why.** `previewStore.selectionByModule` (per module, session-only like the toggle): a click on the header button COLLAPSES the browser selection, so it cannot be read at confirm time, and the dialog must show the same text it captured — so `CanvasPreview` captures on `selectionchange` plus the pane's mouse/key release (collapsed selections report nothing, so the click that consumes the capture cannot erase it), and `CanvasPage` re-resolves it against the LIVE document when the dialog opens AND when the action runs. The dialog SHOWS the exact SOURCE bytes it will replace (a scrollable `<pre>`: for a rewrite, the picked part's whole text), which is the safety property that makes a rendered selection usable at all — a mis-mapped or stale range is visible BEFORE it can apply — and confirm is held while there is no range, with the refusal sentence in its place. **Preview applies through the seam the preview chat already uses, never a second one.** The preview has no editor and the proposal machinery is CM6 state, so `applyPreviewInstruction` splices the zod-validated reply over exactly `[from, to)`, validates the resulting parts document at that boundary (a reply that would break the scaffolding is refused loudly with nothing written and no durable snapshot of a change that never landed), then lands it through `saveWholeModuleDocument` (`origin: 'ai'`, durable pre-change version `refine`/`rewrite` first — which is the undo the row-58 "no undo in preview" caveat lacked for this path) and advances the page's mirror state exactly as a settled preview chat turn does, wash included. The wait is visible and stoppable ("Rewriting the part / Refining — the change lands in the preview when the reply settles", and the header's own **Stop proposal** aborts it, pinned as a write-nothing), the dialog's copy and its confirm label are mode-dependent ("Apply" in preview, "Propose" in Edit), and the toasts are honest per path ("Refinement applied"/"Rewrite applied" in preview, "Proposal applied" after an Edit-mode accept). Grounding is unchanged and stated: the model gets markdown SOURCE (the `[[…]]` token, never the chip label — pinned off the mocked request) and its reply lands verbatim; nothing protects, restores or reconciles tokens. **A SECOND DEFECT, FOUND WHILE PINNING THE FIRST, MEASURED, AND FIXED HERE.** The preview's last-replacement wash rendered the part in three separately parsed markdown SLICES, and MEASURED in jsdom, `one two three` with the wash over `[4,7)` rendered **`onetwothree`** — the whitespace was gone from the DOM, not merely unstyled — because CommonMark strips the initial and final whitespace of a paragraph's content and each slice was parsed as its own document (the same slicing re-chunks any token or emphasis run that straddles a boundary, which is WHY the resolver's mismatch refusal exists). Pre-existing at HEAD for every preview chat apply (the owner asked for the rendered view to be the honest one), and my own apply path sets the same wash, so it is fixed here rather than reported: the wash is now applied INSIDE one parse of one string, by the same source-span plugin — a text node that only partly overlaps the range is split into up to three nodes with recomputed positions, the middle one washed inline and carrying its own exact source range — so the rendered text is the source text character for character and a washed run stays mappable. The three pins that described the old block-level wrapper were updated to the new shape AND strengthened (the whole rendered text is asserted byte-exact against the source, which the old mechanism could not satisfy), and the general lesson is in docs/18 \u00a74. **The reader is untouched, and that is the point of the opt-in:** no prop \u21d2 no plugin, no attribute, no wrapper \u2014 output byte-identical, pinned against a hardcoded HTML string; the peek modal, board cards, artifact bodies and part previews pass nothing. **Honest limits.** jsdom has no layout, no hit-testing and no real input, so the pins prove the DOM contract (the recorded run ranges, the resolved source slice, the exact sentence per refusal, the dialog's displayed bytes, the apply landing in both the rows and the preview, and the reader's byte-identical output) and NOT that a real mouse drag produces those Ranges, that a selection PAINTS, or that the inline wash is visible \u2014 the tests build the Ranges a drag produces and drive the pane's own handlers, so the code path is the production one. One further limit, stated rather than hidden: the confirm-time re-resolution is defence in depth and currently UNREACHABLE through the UI, because the dialog re-renders reactively and shows the refusal with a held confirm the moment the document moves under it. | `src/features/campaign/components/wiki-markdown.tsx` (`sourceOffsets` + `remarkSourceSpans`/`resolveSelectionRange`/`SOURCE_MAP_REFUSALS`, the one-parse wash, the slicing path deleted); `src/features/modules/canvas/CanvasPreview.tsx` (the capture, `CROSS_PART_SELECTION_REASON`); `src/features/modules/canvas/previewStore.ts` (`selectionByModule`); `src/features/modules/canvas/CanvasPage.tsx` (`resolveRefineRange`, `captureRefineTarget`, `applyPreviewInstruction`, the dialog's source block + refusals, the gate); `src/llm/canvasRefine.ts` (doc comment only); `tests/features/wiki-source-map.test.tsx` (new, 14), `tests/features/canvas-preview-ai-actions.test.tsx` (new, 9), `tests/features/blocked-reasons.test.tsx` (the two preview-gate pins replaced by the inverse pins), `tests/features/canvas-preview-default.test.tsx` (the wash pins updated + the one-parse pins); docs/05 \u00a7Module canvas + \u00a7AI actions, docs/08 \u00a7AI actions + \u00a7Preview/highlight, docs/18 \u00a72.3 + \u00a74, this row | The owner's diagnosis was half right and the half that mattered: the actions were not abandoned with the raw-text version, they were GATED on the view that had since become the default, so the fix is to make them work where he reads rather than to teach him to switch views. A rendered selection is only usable if a wrong span is IMPOSSIBLE or VISIBLE, and both halves were built: the mapping is byte-exact or refuses by name (never a clamp), and the dialog shows the exact bytes before anything runs \u2014 which is also what makes a direct apply acceptable in a view with no editor. Everything else reuses what exists (`canvasRefine`, the split-save, the durable version, the preview chat's application path, the blocked-control device): a second apply path or a second save seam would have been the real cost of this feature. The wash defect is the residue of taking "the rendered view" seriously: a decoration that re-parses part of the text is a silent text-loss machine, and it was measured, fixed and turned into a general gotcha rather than left for the owner to notice as "the refine broke my spacing". Rejected: geometry-based mapping; clamping an unmappable selection to a nearby boundary; mapping a chip to its display text; making the reader carry the map (output must stay byte-identical); a second save/apply path for preview; keeping the block-level wash (it cannot preserve the text); and keeping the preview in the gate. | active |

| 111 | **The plan surface reaches the campaign tree ("Yes, both places."), three src comments stopped lying about the deleted deliverables subsystem, and the debounced writers now land their work when the PAGE goes away** (owner's decision, verbatim — asked whether the plan surface should be reachable from the campaign tree's module-group header AS WELL AS from the module canvas header: **"Yes, both places."**). **(1) The plan surface, both places, ONE component.** The tree's module-group header mounted ONE action (`ModulePdfGroupAction` → the shared `ModulePdfButton`); it now mounts a `ModuleGroupActions` that resolves the module row ONCE and renders the SAME two shared components the canvas header renders — `ModulePdfButton` and `ModulePlanButton` — with the SAME artifact pool (the campaign's rows plus the shared library, in that order) and the canvas's own defaults, keeping the existing "a module row that vanished between render and click renders NOTHING, never a button that would print a ghost" rule for both. The brief's own replacement text ("the thing that runs there today is the module AUTOMATION") was NOT used, because the floor bands it dismissed are still live and still keyed on `planIndex` — measured in `moduleGen.ts`, not assumed. `app/layout/TopBar.tsx` and `app/layout/CampaignBar.tsx` both advertised "Deliverables" as a live campaign tab while `app/layout/nav.campaignTabs` has returned Modules / Workspace / Graph since row 108. Comment-only, no behaviour change, NO test (stated as such rather than faked). **(3) The page-hide flush seam.** The writers that debounce a row write flushed on UNMOUNT only — which covers a route change and nothing else: a tab that is BACKGROUNDED and then frozen or discarded never unmounts, so a settled chat turn or a typed draft sitting inside its window was gone with no surface saying so. `lib/pageFlush.ts` is the ONE seam: `registerPageFlush(flush)` keeps a registration list and installs exactly ONE `pagehide` listener and ONE `visibilitychange` listener while at least one flush is registered, firing only when the document reports `hidden`. The contract every registered flush keeps is what makes it safe rather than a second write path — pending-gated (a writer with nothing queued writes NOTHING, which is what stops a tab switch from becoming a write loop), idempotent (the pending work leaves the writer's own queue BEFORE the write, so `hidden` followed by `pagehide` performs ONE write), and non-throwing (each writer already toasts its own failures; anything that escapes a lifecycle listener is toasted by the seam, AGENTS 2). TWO writers are on it: `chatPersist` registers at module scope (the queue and the debounce are the writer's own, so the writer owns its flush) and `new-module-dialog` registers `flushPendingDraft` — a PENDING-gated wrapper around the existing `flush`, deliberately NOT the unmount flush itself, which writes a touched draft even after its timer fired and would therefore have re-written the settings row on every tab switch. Nothing new was built: no Dexie version, no second write path, no new serialization, and no cancellation API for a queue that a flush can drain. **(4) TWO ITEMS LEFT OPEN, BY INSTRUCTION.** (a) **The cast count still does not reach the toast** (row 107's gap stands): a sweep whose ONLY work was casting a library creature composes an EMPTY summary — every part of the module-automation toast is `> 0` gated and `EntityBatchResult.cast` is deliberately outside `generated` — so it toasts nothing at all and the owner cannot tell that work happened. A working fix (count `result.cast.length` per kind, add ONE part to the same toast, worded as the ACT rather than a creation because `castCreatureAsNpc` is idempotent per (campaign, module, name, identity)) was written, tested and injected on this branch, then DROPPED whole because `features/modules/post-generation.ts` was occupied by another writer's uncommitted work — the item is queued for a later slice, and nothing of it survives here. (b) **The module board's layout write is NOT on the seam** (AMENDED IN PLACE by row 118, which WIRED IT — the sentence below describes row 111's own tree, not HEAD: `features/modules/board/BoardPage.tsx` now registers a pending-gated `flushPendingLayout` through `lib/pageFlush`, and so does `features/campaign/components/artifact-editor.tsx`): `BoardPage`'s 600 ms layout debounce still flushes on unmount only and has exactly the exposure this row fixes for the other two writers; it was excluded by the same instruction, and the seam takes it with a pending-gated flush plus one `registerPageFlush` call. **What the brief had wrong, reported not silently absorbed:** the item-3 file is `features/modules/post-generation.ts` (not `src/llm/post-generation.ts`), and the item-2 characterization of `planIndex`'s dependents did not match the code (above). **UNPROVEN.** (a) The flush hands each writer's promise to the page without awaiting it (a lifecycle handler cannot hold the page open), and jsdom has no tab teardown at all — so "the IndexedDB transaction COMMITS before the browser actually destroys the tab" is unmeasured; what IS measured is that the write is taken out of the debounce window and issued on the browser's own going-away signals. (b) `pagehide` and `visibilitychange` are the browser's contract, not this repo's: no real mobile Safari / Chrome tab-freezing run was performed.
