| 112 | **A cited creature row is never ASKED for a stat block, and the refused pair is never CONSTRUCTED** (owner report, verbatim: *"One other thing that currently fails often is NPC generation. My first theory was that it always fails on npc that need a stat block, but i found 1 that successfully has one. Still, there seems to be a connection though its not 100%. Unfortunately i do not see the failures as failed runs. There is a warning message shown briefly with tons of text, but its only shown briefly and looked like lots of json (not sure though). This worked before the refactor."*). THE OWNER'S CORRELATION IS EXACT and the "not 100%" is the tell: `npcDataSchema` refuses a row that carries BOTH an authored `statBlock` and a `creatureRef` citation (the refine's own words: "an npc carries either an authored stat block or a library creatureRef to derive one from, never both — clear the stat block or drop the creature reference"), and the only rows that can carry a citation are the ones the cast tier births (`db/creatureRepo` writes `statBlock: null` + `creatureRef` in ONE literal — the sole creator, docs/17 row 106). Generation "fails on npc that need a stat block" accordingly means **a CITED npc whose draft answered `needsStatBlock: true`** — a goblin warrior, the zombie Aunt Agatha — while the ONE that "successfully has one" is a cited npc whose draft answered `needsStatBlock: false` (a contact, a merchant), because then the statblock step never ran and the pair was never built. Nothing about generation was 100% broken; the trigger was the MODEL's answer to a question it had no business answering on a cited row. REPRODUCED, measured on the pre-fix tree at `0688c94` (scratch log kept for the writer's report, and now pinned as a test that is RED without the fix): a refill of a cited npc with a `needsStatBlock: true` draft spends TWO chat calls (draft + statblock), then `mergeRefillData` builds `{…, statBlock, creatureRef}`, `updateArtifact`'s `anyArtifactSchema.parse` throws, and the run row is left `failed` / `failureKind: 'invalid-output'` / `errorMessage` = the zod issue dump, artifact byte-untouched. FIX — the Aunt Agatha rule, both halves. (1) NEVER ASK: `runEngine.runStatblock` resolves the refill target (`getAnyArtifact(input.targetArtifactId)`) and, when `isCastCreatureNpc` holds, finishes the step `'skipped'` with a reason naming the citation and the library creature — decided BEFORE the model call, so no call is spent and no block is left in the run row for finalize to merge. The step-off is scoped to the REFILL TARGET, not to the kind: a NEW npc (no target) is unaffected, and a non-cited refill still produces its block. (2) NEVER CONSTRUCT: `mergeRefillData`'s npc branch throws a NAMED refusal when a block arrives anyway (a run persisted before the rule: resumed, or with a hand-edited statblock step), and keeps the citation on the row it returns — never dropping the citation (that severs the identity stating where the numbers come from), never dropping the block (that discards what a user-edited step produced), never "preferring one" silently. Measured: this was the ONLY pair-builder in `src/` (grep `creatureRef:` finds the cast writer's `statBlock: null` literal, the schema field, and this merge) — the same class of bug was hunted at every other `creatureRef`+authored-stat-block site and none can express it (readers: `roomBudget.fixedCastStatsFor` prefers the authored block and otherwise DERIVES from the citation; `canvasChat`'s two renderers are read-only and one narrows to `statBlock === null`; the entity/roster batches cannot reach a cited row at all — `entity-batch` starts a run only when `target.artifactId === undefined`, and `change-artifact` refuses cast rows outright — docs/18 §4). VISIBILITY — the owner's "i do not see the failures as failed runs" is the SECOND defect, and it is not a missing run row: the failure was ALWAYS written as a failed run (`runEngine.fail` writes `status: 'failed'` + `failureKind` + `errorMessage`, listed by `listRunsByCampaign` and rendered inline in the persona panel's Runs tab), and what hid it is that the toast's HEADLINE was the raw zod dump — `fail` passed `errorMessage(error)` (i.e. `ZodError.message`, the `[{code,path,message}…]` JSON) as the toast TITLE, and `lib/toast` deliberately humanizes only the DESCRIPTION, so the owner read megabytes-shaped JSON in a ~4 s sonner toast (no duration override anywhere) and went looking for a run row that was never on screen. Fixed at the same seam: `composedFailureMessage(error)` composes `Refused by a data check: <field>: <why>. Nothing was written.` for a zod failure (digging through `union` branches — a failed union is ONE issue whose own path is `[]` and whose message is the literal "Invalid input", measured; composing from the top level produced a sentence that named nothing) and passes every other error's message through VERBATIM, so the named refusals this engine throws stay exactly as written; the same string is the toast headline and the run row's `errorMessage`, and the raw error stays the logged cause (`debugLog` + `lib/toast`'s `console.error`). What the owner sees now: a cited npc's refill SUCCEEDS, its statblock step reads `skipped` with the reason in the run row, its prose is refilled, the citation is byte-identical and its `statBlock` stays null; if a run persisted before this rule ever carries a block to finalize, he gets one readable sentence naming the npc and saying nothing was written — never a JSON dump. | `src/llm/runEngine.ts` (`runStatblock`'s cited-target force-off, `mergeRefillData`'s npc refusal + citation-preserving return, `composedFailureMessage`+`readableZodIssues`, `fail`); `tests/llm/refill-creature-stats.test.ts` (6 pins, each with a recorded revert); docs/11 §A cited row's REFILL; docs/18 §2.2 + §4; docs/08 §The creature tier; docs/05 §Error surfaces | The refused pair is refused BY NAME at the schema, so the fix must make the pair unreachable rather than teach a caller to tolerate the throw: forcing the step off is what stops the wasted call and the misleading "the model produced unusable output" verdict, and the merge refusal is what keeps the rule true for runs that already carry a block. Delegating the decision to the draft was the actual defect — `needsStatBlock` is a MODEL's answer about whether stats matter, and on a cited row the numbers are the LIBRARY's, so the question must not be asked at all. UNPROVEN, stated rather than implied: the owner's own failing runs were not available to inspect (no run rows or transcripts), so the reproduction drives the same code path from a crafted cited row instead of replaying his run; the "not 100%" split is INFERRED from the code (the draft's `needsStatBlock` is the branch) and not measured against his history; whether every npc-generation failure he saw was this path is NOT established — a draft parse failure or a refused model reply fails an npc run by other routes and does write a failed run row; and the "worked before the refactor" reading rests on the citation itself arriving with the cast tier (`234df17`, the one commit that introduced `creatureRef`), so before that tier existed there was no cited row to refill. | active |

| 113 | **A consent prompt may only claim authorship the ROW can support: the one part-text save seam records the ORIGIN of the text, the normalization pass holds only text a human authored, and the generated premise stops asking about itself** (owner report, verbatim: *"Module creation after creating parts when normalizing now ALWAYS brings up this: 'Normalization wants to update hand-edited text ? review the proposed rewrites.' There is actually nothing hand written."*). **(1) The diagnosis, MEASURED in the code before anything was built, and it was TWO causes feeding one banner.** (a) The PREMISE took the proposal path UNCONDITIONALLY: `applyNormalizationVerdict` pushed `{ planIndex: -1, replacements }` for the premise on every run, whether the model had just written that premise or the owner had, so a module the owner never touched raised the banner whenever its premise named a variant. (b) `edited` was the ONLY thing the pass could ask, and the ONE part-text seam stamped it on EVERY write: `db/moduleRepo.patchModulePartText` set `edited: true` for a hand edit AND for model text the canvas auto-accepted, so "written outside the generator" was read as "the owner wrote this". The owner's second answer pins which of those the report was about, verbatim: *"Usually parts are auto accepted via checkbox. That was my scenario."* **(2) The fix is a RECORDED origin, not a guess.** `domain/module.textOriginSchema` (`'human' | 'model'`) is a nullable, additive, parse-on-read field on the module spine AND on each part (`origin`), written at the ONE seam that writes part text: `patchModulePartText` stamps `writerModel === undefined ? 'human' : 'model'`, because supplying a writer model IS the machine-write signature — every canvas/chat apply path supplies it (`canvas/saveDoc.ts`, `CanvasPage`'s apply, `canvas/chatController.ts`, `canvas/snapshotChat.ts`) while a hand save deliberately omits it, so the two are distinguishable at the seam and nowhere else. `domain/provenance.textOriginIsMachineWritten` is the ONE authorship decision point the pass consults (`origin === 'model'` only), so nothing else can re-derive authorship from `edited` — or from `writerModel`, which is NOT a proxy: a hand edit deliberately CARRIES the previous model id forward (row 93), which is exactly why the stored id can never answer "who wrote the text now". **(3) What each writer stamps.** The generator writes `origin: 'model'` on the spine premise it emits (`normalizeAndSave`, with `spineReplySchema` extended `.omit({ writerModel: true, origin: true })` so the model cannot answer a question about its own authorship) and on every part slot its parts pass writes; the checkpoint's **Generate parts** (`approveSpineAndRun`) reads the STORED row and stamps `origin: 'human'` only when the approved premise TEXT differs from what is stored (`editedByHand`), otherwise it carries the stored origin forward — so merely clicking through the checkpoint claims nothing, while rewriting the premise there is recorded as the owner's own. A model-written premise is rewritten in place by the pass (applied, `entityRewriteProposals: null`), a human/legacy one is still HELD with its banner and dialog. **(4) The board's two gestures state the authorship they do NOT change.** Found while auditing every writer of part text, not by report: the board's **Apply** re-lands the text the engine just wrote (which the generator stamps `origin: 'model'`) and **Discard** puts the PREVIOUS text back, and both called the save seam with no writer model — so the default would have recorded `'human'`, i.e. it would have relabelled a model's rewrite as the owner's and made the next normalization pass ask him about text he never wrote (the owner's defect, re-created through the board). `stagedRewrites` now captures the replaced part's `origin` + `writerModel` at `stageProposal` (the last moment they are readable — the engine's write overwrites the row), Apply passes `authorship: 'model'` for the engine's own text, Discard passes the captured pair back, and `patchModulePartText`/`saveModulePartText` take that explicit optional `authorship` ONLY for writes that do not change the answer (`undefined` keeps the writer-model rule as the single default; nothing else stores or derives an origin). **(5) Legacy rows keep asking, deliberately, and the row says why.** An `origin: null` row (written before the field) reads as HUMAN-authored in `textOriginIsMachineWritten`, so it keeps its consent prompt — the conservative direction, because the origin is genuinely NOT recoverable from stored data (see the carried id above). **(6) The banner, the dialog and every label stopped asserting authorship they cannot support.** `entity-panel`'s banner is now derived once (`heldRewriteSummary`/`heldRewritesBanner`) and reads *"Normalization is holding N link rewrite(s) for review — the premise and part 3. M documents are waiting on your decision; nothing has been changed."*; the dialog lists `Premise — you: …` / `Part 1 — model-id: …` through `modulePartWriterLabel`/`moduleTextWriterLabel` ("You wrote this part." / "The model `id` wrote this part." / "This part was written by hand (or before the app recorded authorship)."), and the reader's and the board's rewrite alerts use the same labels. The floor-repair clause that read "hand-edited" is renamed `writtenOutsideGenerator`. `edited` itself is UNCHANGED in meaning ("written outside the generator") because its other readers must not move: the durable-version restore guard (`live.edited && !snapshot.edited`) and the floor repair target filter (`part?.edited !== true`) both still read it, and both are about whether the text left the generator — never about who wrote it. **(7) Tests.** NEW `tests/llm/module-edit-origin.test.ts` (13): the seam's two branches, the generated premise applying with `origin` intact, the human premise holding, a canvas-applied MODEL part (`edited: true`) applying while a human part beside it holds, the generator's own stamps, the legacy `null` default on both documents, the accessor table (`null`/`undefined`/`'human'` false, `'model'` true), the checkpoint's unchanged-vs-rewritten premise, and the pre-field row parsing to `origin: null` with no Dexie version bump. `tests/features/entity-classify-new.test.tsx` gains the pair that pins the two branches through a REAL chat apply vs a REAL hand save; `tests/features/module-problems.test.ts`, `tests/features/module-reader.test.tsx` and `tests/features/entity-panel.test.tsx` assert the new sentences and the absence of any "hand-edited"/"your text" claim; `tests/llm/moduleGen.test.ts`'s part seeder now names the origin it means; the board's two gestures are pinned in `tests/features/module-board-rewrite.test.tsx` (Apply keeps the engine's `'model'` and its id, Discard restores the captured pair) and in `tests/features/staged-rewrites.test.ts` (the capture survives, and a re-proposal replaces text AND authorship together). **What I could NOT prove.** (a) `origin: null` is a GUESS by policy, and a legacy row whose text a model wrote still asks — the field cannot see into rows written before it, and no heuristic was built (a heuristic over `writerModel` would be provably wrong for a hand-edited model part). (b) The AUTO-ACCEPT reading itself is the dispatcher's interpretation of the owner's sentence, not something a test can observe: what is pinned is that a write supplying a writer model is recorded as machine-written and no longer holds. (c) No live-provider run was performed; every pin mocks `chat` at the protocol boundary. (e) The board's Discard restores the CAPTURED authorship, and the capture lives in session-only memory: a reload between the rewrite and the Discard loses it (the staging dies with it, so that write never happens and the row keeps the engine's text, as before). (f) The premise's `origin` is written by exactly two production paths (the generator and the checkpoint); any FUTURE premise write must stamp it or the row falls back to the conservative "human" — a convention this row records rather than a constraint the type system enforces — and the same holds for any part-text writer other than the one seam (the board's gestures now state it explicitly, so the compiler asks any new caller of that argument for an answer). | `src/domain/module.ts` (`textOriginSchema`, `TextOrigin`, `ModuleSpine.origin`, `ModulePart.origin`, the reworded `edited` doc); `src/features/modules/module-problems.ts` (`writtenOutsideGenerator`, `heldRewriteSummary`, `heldRewritesBanner`, `modulePartWriterLabel`, `moduleTextWriterLabel`); `src/features/modules/entity-panel.tsx` (banner + dialog); docs/05 §The entity panel; docs/05 §Fix module problems | The owner's report is not "the banner is wrong" but "the app is telling me I wrote something I did not write", and no prompt can be honest while authorship is INFERRED from a flag that means something else. So the arc records the origin where the text is written (one seam), decides authorship in one function, and makes every surface say what the row can support: "you", the model's id, or the honest "by hand — or before the app recorded authorship". The generated premise stops asking about itself because the generator wrote it; genuinely hand-authored text keeps every bit of its protection; and legacy rows keep asking because the alternative — guessing — is the defect this row removes. Rejected: inferring origin from the stored `writerModel`; a per-part origin editor; a second review surface or any plan-style settings UI; two sources of truth for authorship; a Dexie table or version bump for a field that parses on read; and repurposing `edited` (its restore-guard and floor-filter readers depend on its current meaning). |
| 114 | **The module creator is SHOWN the library it may name: the bestiary slot carries the actual creature list, built from the pool the cast resolves against, and a name that still misses is refused WITH the nearest creatures** (owner report, verbatim, with the terminal’s umlaut damage ignored: *"«Mara Distelrauch» asks to borrow the stats of «Zombie-Schlurferin der Stufe 1 beziehungsweise passend angepasster Zombie-Schlurfererchorchener Zombie-Schergeerschergeerschläger»"* — and three siblings for `Zombie-Schläger` / `Zombie-Schlurfer`, each ending *"but this workspace's library holds no creature of that name — import the book it comes from, or name a creature the library has (never a guess)"*. His diagnosis of the cause, verbatim: *"Ok, here is what i remember how that worked earlier (maybe check docs if its still in them) — There was a list of 300 mobs from these sources build by giving the first 300 mobs, ordered by level difference from the module level. That was given to the creator LLM. I guess this was somehow discarded during refactor."*). **His memory is right about the mechanism; the wire to the creator was never run.** The window is real and ratified (docs/12 §7, `src/llm/encounterRoster.ts`, `ROSTER_LIMIT = 300`, ordered by level distance to a target level by owner amendment) — but it was consumed ONLY by the encounter/smith prompts (`runEngine.ts:2661` and `:3552`). The module creator never called it: the pre-refactor spine prompt had no roster clause either and `moduleGen.ts` never called the formatter, so the refactor DELETED nothing — the creator was given the bestiary slot (row 107, the Aunt Agatha case) with AN EXAMPLE and no vocabulary, and the only thing reaching the prompt from the library was a BOOLEAN (`(await listLibraryCreatures()).length > 0`). A model asked to name a creature from a list it was never shown invents example-shaped names — which is why a German module produced `Zombie-Schläger`/`Zombie-Schlurfer`, and why the degenerate name exists at all: nothing said a cast borrows the creature's numbers AS THEY ARE, so it tried to encode a level-adapted variant inside the name field. The cast resolution (`features/modules/entity-batch.ts`, exact name match, trimmed and case-insensitive, with the book disambiguator) is CORRECT and its loud refusal is the required behaviour; the defect is that we made the refusal unavoidable. **What landed.** (1) *One source of truth* — `src/llm/creatorRoster.ts` builds the window from `db/creatureRepo.listLibraryCreatures()` (every stat-block chunk of ANY book origin — `pdf` or `pack`, any status), NOT the encounter roster's pack-only filter. This was a trap: a pack-only window would be EMPTY for a library built from an ordinary rulebook import while the slot stayed on offer, which is the same defect with a different trigger. (2) *The same order and cap as §7* — level distance to the module's `levelMin`/`levelMax` band midpoint, through a comparator SHARED with the encounter roster (`encounterRoster.libraryLevelOrder`, extracted together with `levelDistanceTo` so the two windows cannot drift), ties by `levelSort` then locale name, `"—"`-level creatures last, `CREATOR_ROSTER_LIMIT = 300` lines with the `(roster truncated; N more)` note, recomputed per run and deterministic. The level is DERIVED with the SAME parser (`encounterRoster.parseLevelSort`) over the chunk's stat block, which is why `listLibraryCreatures` now carries the `statBlock` its own rows already held — reading the chunks a second time would have been a second source. `encounterRoster.ts`'s own output is unchanged (its 29 pins stay green). (3) *The prompt states the rule AND shows the list* — `promptStyles.spineEntityKindsClause` appends, inside the entity-kind bullet (no new placeholder, no template change): name ONLY a creature from the list, copied exactly as written; a cast borrows the numbers exactly as the library holds them, never a level-adapted, renamed or decorated variant and never a translation or a remembered name; and if the creature is not on the list, leave the slot OFF and write the mob into the scene. (4) *Byte-identity where there is nothing to list* — an empty library, and now an empty WINDOW, compose the pre-change prompt byte for byte and the slot is NOT offered (a slot with no vocabulary is uncastable by construction). (5) *The refusal is actionable* — when the name resolves to nothing, the message appends up to three nearest creatures with their books, computed by `domain/creatureName.creatureNameSimilarity` over a normalization that forgives case, whitespace, umlauts/diacritics (NFKD plus hand-folded æ/ø/ß/đ/ð/þ/ł), hyphen-vs-space and a trailing `(…)` qualifier. The RESOLUTION IS NOT WIDENED: `sameName` is untouched, nothing is auto-substituted, and a query with nothing close keeps the pre-114 sentence byte-identically (a wrong "did you mean" is a second wrong answer). **MEASURED.** A 300-line window of realistic creature names costs 5,805 bytes (302 lines) of the spine prompt; the rule plus header alone is 4,386 bytes (~1,100 tokens); a 400-creature library is capped at 300 lines with `(roster truncated; 100 more)`, and 1,000 creatures produce the identical block. The entity-kind clause grows 566 → 7,585 bytes (≈2,100 tokens in total) — the same order as the encounter roster's own window and small against the module text the spine prompt already carries, so the cap was kept at §7's 300 rather than a new smaller bound. **UNPROVEN (explicit).** (a) No live-provider run observed a model naming a creature from the list: every pin mocks the transport at the protocol boundary, so "the model now copies a listed name" is the INTENT, not a measurement. (b) The suggestion floor (`0.4`) is a judgement: it keeps `Bog Shambler` quiet against a zombie-only library and names `Zombie Schlägerin` → `Zombie-Schläger`, but a compound-language library could produce a near miss the threshold does not reach — the suggestion is bounded and SILENT by design, and no real library was swept to calibrate it. (c) The window's ORDERING cannot be observed in a real module; only the composed prompt is pinned. (d) Whether 300 lines is the right cap for a CREATOR prompt (as opposed to the encounter one) is inherited from §7, not re-derived for this consumer. | `src/llm/creatorRoster.ts` (new: `collectCreatorRoster`, `buildCreatorRoster`, `creatorRosterEntries`, `nearestLibraryCreatures`, `CREATOR_ROSTER_LIMIT = 300`); `src/domain/creatureName.ts` (new: `normalizeCreatureName`, `sameCreatureName`, `creatureNameTokens`, `creatureNameSimilarity`); `src/domain/index.ts`; `src/db/creatureRepo.ts` (`LibraryCreature.statBlock`); `src/llm/encounterRoster.ts` (`levelDistanceTo` + `libraryLevelOrder` extracted, `buildPackRoster` reads them); `src/llm/promptStyles.ts` (`SPINE_BESTIARY_RULES`, `SPINE_BESTIARY_HEADER`, `BestiaryVocabulary`, `bestiaryVocabularyBlock`, the widened `spineEntityKindsClause`/`spineContractValues`); `src/llm/moduleGen.ts` (`spineMessages` builds the window from the module's band midpoint); `src/features/modules/entity-batch.ts` (the suggestion half of the no-such-creature refusal); `src/features/settings/prompt-style-preview.ts` (renders the clause WITH a sample vocabulary); `tests/llm/creatorRoster.test.ts` (new, 14); `tests/domain/creatureName.test.ts` (new, 13); `tests/llm/moduleGen-cast.test.ts` (7 new pins, 3 amended in place); `vite.config.ts` (the two new files run in the node project); docs/12 §7; docs/18 §2.2/§4; docs/08 §The creator's bestiary window | The refusal was never the bug — asking a model to name a creature from a list it cannot see is. The window is built from the population the LOOKUP uses, so the vocabulary a prompt shows and the lookup that judges the reply cannot disagree; it stays capped and level-ordered because a 1,000-creature bestiary must still fit a prompt; and the actionable refusal turns the owner's next near miss into a name he can fix instead of a guess that cannot resolve. | active |
| 115 | **A stop is an INTENT, not a moment: the cancel outlives the call that records it, so the step still holding an `await` when the owner stops a run can neither resurrect the stopped run nor report the stop as a failure** (flake, three sightings, all the same test and the same two lines: `tests/features/encounter-map-queue.test.ts` → `cancelAll withdraws queued jobs, cancels the in-flight run and settles silently (stop-all seam)`, intermittent at roughly 1 in 3–5 full runs, `AssertionError: expected "vi.fn()" to not be called at all, but actually been called 1 times` with `toastError`'s first argument `Encounter step "brief" failed: PersonaRun not found: 030db5ab-…` and the `NotFoundError` itself as the second — the oldest sighting after `0688c94`, NOT bisected, so no commit is accused here). **The shape, established by FORCING the ordering instead of repeating suites (the `89e5d71` method, docs/08), with every hop named.** `tests/features/encounter-map-queue.test.ts:371-395` (the no-double-work guards test) enqueues a REAL map job — `chatMock.mockReturnValue(new Promise(() => undefined))` at `:385`, `enqueue` at `:386`, then `waitFor(() => expect(isEncounterMapPending(null, encounter.id)).toBe(true))` at `:389-391` — and RETURNS after `useEncounterMapQueue.getState().reset()` at `:393` with the job body still inside its opening `Promise.all` reads (`src/features/modules/encounter-map-queue.ts:85`), which never observe `ctx.signal`: **MEASURED with a probe in `clearDatabase` that the guards test leaves NO run row behind (`runs=0`), i.e. its `runEngine.startRun` lands in the NEXT test's window.** `reset()` aborts the job's controller (`src/lib/jobQueue.ts:318-324`) while the pipeline it started is fire-and-forget (`src/llm/runEngine.ts:1651`), so two things can straddle a `beforeEach`: the run row can be created before a `clearDatabase()` (`tests/db/helpers.ts`, `db.runs.clear()`) and the OPENING write of that still-live pipeline can land after it. The stop in play is the queue's own abort reaction: `waitForRunStatus(runId, { signal: ctx.signal })` (`:123`) rejects on the abort and `await runEngine.cancel(runId)` (`:131`) records the stop. **At HEAD that stop was a MOMENT, not an intent**: `cancel()` added `cancelRequested` (`src/llm/runEngine.ts:1952`) and then DELETED it four statements later (`:1961`), before the step that was already awaiting a reply could possibly observe the abort — so the pipeline stopped tracking its own cancellation and did exactly what an uncancelled one does: it wrote on (`:2048` step-start, `:2080` post-step, restoring `'running'` OVER the `'cancelled'` row), and where the row had been deleted in the meantime the write threw `NotFoundError` out of `runRepo.updateRun` (`src/db/runRepo.ts:50`), which the pipeline's catch wrapped (`src/llm/runEngine.ts:2171`) and `fail` surfaced through `toastError` (`:5963`) — a failure toast, and a failed run row, for a stop the owner asked for, against `docs/05-UI.md` ("a cancelled run is never reported as a failure"). **Why the ordering usually hides it:** in the common interleaving the leaked job's row is created and cancelled entirely inside ONE later window (probe: `runs=1[cancelled] pipelines=0 intent=0` at the following boundary) and its writes meet a row that still exists, so seven runs in eight are green — the toast needs the row to be created just before a clear and written just after, which is why the flake never reproduced on demand. **Pre-existing in KIND, and that is the load-bearing evidence for not blaming `0688c94`:** the delete at `:1961` predates every sighting, and the one thing that commit changed is REACHABILITY — its event-backed `waitForRunRowChange` replaced a 250 ms poll, so the queue's abort reaction became immediate and the stop now overlaps the in-flight write often enough to be seen; MEASURED by reading the commit's own diff, and by the fact that restoring the delete by hand (injection I1) brings the three pins straight back, while no commit authored the defect. Ledger row 97 (the same class, and the previous arc) explicitly deferred exactly this cure — *"that cure belongs in `src/llm/runEngine.ts`, owned by another arc"* — so this row pays it. **The fix recognises the late write as belonging to an ABORTED run instead of suppressing it by error kind** (the brief's preference, and the only version that cannot be fooled by a genuine failure wearing the same boots): `RunEngine.cancelRequested` is documented as the durable per-run cancel INTENT; `cancel()` no longer clears it; `executeFrom` now has a late-arrival check after each step returns (`:2118`) and at the pipeline's tail (`:2201`) that DISCARD a result landing after the stop before any write happens (the stop's own verdict is already on the row, and the row may be gone); the pipeline's own cancel writes route through a new `recordCancelled` (`:5950`) that tolerates a row the owner has since deleted (a vanished row cannot be "reported as a failure" — this is cancel-path-only tolerance, NOT a general one: a step that dies with no stop in play still wraps, still toasts and still writes its failed row through `fail`, pinned); and the intent is consumed where the pipeline actually ENDS — `executeFrom`'s `finally` (`:2240`), its early return for a run that is already cancelled/failed/gone (`:2041`) — and dropped by each deliberate restart, because the newest owner action wins (`startRun`, `retryStep` `:1827`, `resumeRun` `:1898`, `regenerateEncounterLayout` `:1928`, `regenerateEncounterCandidates` `:1975`). What the owner sees differently when he stops a run: the row stays `cancelled`, the background line stops claiming the run is working, no failure toast, no failed row, and the row keeps its Retry (which now works — a stop no longer strands the pipeline's state behind it). Pinned by four new tests in the map-queue file (the two late-write seams, the vanished-row spurious-toast pin, the genuine-failure contrast) and two in `tests/llm/runEngine.test.ts` (the stopped run is not resurrected by the in-flight step; the stopped run still offers its Retry), all RED before the fix with the sightings' own message; the pre-existing `cancelAll` pin is byte-unchanged. **UNPROVEN, stated rather than implied:** (1) which sibling test loses the race in any GIVEN sighting — the guards test (`:371`) and the dequeue test (`:416`) both return with real orchestration in flight, and the forced pins prove the SHAPE, not the victim; (2) no live-provider or real-app sighting was reproduced (every pin mocks the transport at the protocol boundary); (3) two of the fix's guard lines are not reached by any pin (the tail check at `:2201`; the intent clears in `executeFrom`'s `finally`/early return) — they are kept as consistency, and docs/08 says so instead of implying coverage; (4) a cancel that arrives AFTER a pipeline has already ended still leaves the intent set (`pipelines=0 intent=1`, measured) — it is consumed by the row's next deliberate restart, and `approve`/`editStep` never reach a `cancelled` row (`persona-panel.tsx:1329` renders them only for `awaiting_user`/`needs_review`), but no pin covers that lifetime; (5) `fail()`'s own write is still loud when a run row is deleted with NO stop in play (the Runs list deleting a running run, ledger 97's real-app analogue) — deliberately untouched, and pinned as the honest path. | active |

| 116 | **Deleting a run IS a stop: every path that deletes run ROWS stops a still-generating run FIRST, so the owner's own delete can no longer come back at him as `PersonaRun not found`** (the defect ledger 97 named and row 115 explicitly LEFT ALONE and pinned as the honest path — its UNPROVEN item (5), verbatim: *"`fail()`'s own write is still loud when a run row is deleted with NO stop in play (the Runs list deleting a running run, ledger 97's real-app analogue) — deliberately untouched, and pinned as the honest path."*; row 97 had already deferred the cure to this file: *"the product-side cure for the analogous REAL-APP case (the Runs list lets the owner delete a RUNNING run…) belongs in `src/llm/runEngine.ts`, which a concurrent arc owns"*). **The defect, with its evidence.** The Runs tab renders its delete button for EVERY row whatever the status — `src/features/campaign/components/persona-panel.tsx:1903` (`aria-label={\`Delete run ${stamp}\`}`) → `:1906` `void handleDeleteRun(run.id)` — and `handleDeleteRun` (`:1776`) called `deleteRun` with no status check at all. Deleting a run whose step is in flight therefore removes the row the pipeline is still writing: the step's next write throws `NotFoundError` out of `runRepo.updateRun` (`src/db/runRepo.ts:50`), the encounter pipeline's catch wraps it as `Encounter step "brief" failed: PersonaRun not found: <uuid>` (`src/llm/runEngine.ts:2270`), and `fail` toasts that sentence and tries to write a `'failed'` row that no longer exists (`:6095` → its own `updateRun` rejects with nothing awaiting it, the row-97 unhandled-rejection shape) — a failure report, and a failed-run write, for a run the owner deliberately removed, against docs/05 ("a cancelled run is never reported as a failure"). REPRODUCED FIRST, by FORCING the ordering rather than repeating suites (docs/08 §own the promise, not the clock): `tests/features/run-delete-running.test.tsx` → the forcing pin parks the encounter brief's reply on a promise the test releases BY HAND, clicks the Runs tab's own delete button while the row is `running`, and only then releases the reply. RED at `561c5b2` with the sightings' own sentence, `expected [ Array(1) ] to deeply equal []` / `"Encounter step \"brief\" failed: PersonaRun not found: <uuid>"`, plus the unhandled `NotFoundError` from `fail`'s write; GREEN with the fix. **EVERY path that deletes run rows, and which of them can act on a RUNNING row (all four CAN — none refused it, each is stated with its line rather than assumed).** (1) `db/runRepo.deleteRun` (`src/db/runRepo.ts:57`) — its only production caller is the Runs list (`persona-panel.tsx:1776`), whose button is unguarded (`:1903`); (2) `campaignRepo.deleteCampaign` (`src/db/campaignRepo.ts:118`, `db.runs.where('campaignId').equals(id).delete()`) — reachable from the campaign picker (`CampaignPickerPage.tsx:333`) while a campaign's unattended runs are generating; (3) `campaignRepo.removeAllGeneratedContent` (`:330`) — reachable from the edit-campaign dialog; (4) `maintenance.deleteCampaignWorkspace` (`src/db/maintenance.ts:158`) — the one that ALREADY aborts the work it would strand, but only the MODULE half (`:95-98`, `cancelModuleGen` per module): the runs half was simply missing, so a run in flight during "Clear workspace" produced the same toast. NOT delete paths, checked rather than assumed: `lib/exportImport` only READS runs (`:173`) and ADDS them on import (`:880`) — it deletes no run row; `runRepo.failRunningRuns` (`:150`) writes verdicts, never deletes; the fifth row-deleter is test-only (`tests/db/helpers.ts:17`, `db.runs.clear()`). **The fix is the owner's cancel-then-delete, through the machinery row 115 just landed — no second mechanism for the same idea.** `RunEngine.stopRunsBeforeDelete(ids)` (`src/llm/runEngine.ts:2053`) stops every id that is still GENERATING through the existing `cancel()` — i.e. the SAME durable intent the Stop button records (`cancelRequested`, `:1594`), which the pipeline already honours at its write boundaries (`:2118`, `:2201`), whose late results are discarded before any write, and whose verdict goes through `recordCancelled` for a row that is already gone. It asks `isGenerating` (`:2070`): the engine's own controller registry FIRST (a pipeline this page is driving), then the ROW (`status === 'running'` — a stale row after a reload, or one another tab drives). A row that is NOT generating is left completely alone, so the delete of a finished/failed run is byte-for-byte unchanged. It lives in the engine, not the UI, because the engine is the only thing that knows a run is GENERATING as opposed to merely saying so in a row. Wire-ups: `persona-panel.tsx:1785` (`await runEngine.stopRunsBeforeDelete([id])` before `deleteRun(id)`) and the campaign-level half `stopGeneratingRunsForCampaign(campaignId)` (`runEngine.ts:426`) at `campaignRepo.ts:93-94` and `:265-266` and `maintenance.ts:105-106` — all three awaited BEFORE the caller opens its Dexie transaction, because `cancel()` writes the row through its own transaction and joining an open scope would commit it early (the `cancelModuleGen` precedent in the same functions, and the reason the import is dynamic: `runEngine` already imports `campaignRepo`, so a static one is a cycle). What the owner sees differently when he deletes a running run from the Runs tab: the run is stopped first (its row goes `cancelled`, the background line stops claiming it is working, the abort reaches the provider), the row is actually gone when the gesture completes, NO failure toast, and no `'failed'` row — while a run that dies on its own with no stop and no delete in play still toasts and still writes its failed row (the contrast pin, green before and after). **Tests, 8 new pins in `tests/features/run-delete-running.test.tsx`, 4 RED at `561c5b2` and 4 green throughout** (the file did not exist before this row, so its RED is the pre-fix behaviour it drives): the forcing pin above; the three campaign-level wipes (`deleteCampaign`, `removeAllGeneratedContent`, `deleteCampaignWorkspace`, one `it.each`) each parked-then-wiped-then-released, all RED; "a row that SAYS running — no pipeline in this page — is still stopped on its way out" (the ROW half of `isGenerating`); "deleting a FINISHED run is unchanged: no stop, no failure toast, the row goes" (asserts `runEngine.cancel` was NEVER called); and the contrast pin ("a step that dies on its own — no stop and no delete in play — STILL toasts and STILL writes its failed row"). Probe, three variants, one process, `CAMPAIGNER_TEST_WORKERS=2`: (A) delete the run row while the job watches → TWO toasts, the pipeline's `Encounter step "brief" failed: PersonaRun not found: <uuid>` AND the queue's `Could not generate a map for "<encounter>"` (`Run <id> disappeared while waiting for it to finish`), `failed:1`; (B) cancel-then-delete (this row's gesture) → the pipeline's toast is GONE, the queue's remains (`… disappeared while waiting for it to finish`), `failed:1`; (C) `cancel()` ONLY, nothing deleted → the queue STILL toasts (`… run ended cancelled`), `failed:1`. So the queue's classification of a run the owner stopped is PRE-EXISTING and NOT delete-specific (`src/features/modules/encounter-map-queue.ts:135` throws `run ended ${status}` for anything but `completed` → `src/lib/jobQueue.ts:202` toasts and the job lands on the retryable `failed` list), and it contradicts docs/05's own rule for a cancelled run. It is deliberately NOT touched here: its pins live in `tests/features/encounter-map-queue.test.ts`, which this brief freezes ("re-run them, do not edit them"), and curing it means deciding what a queue job does when the run it drives is withdrawn by the owner — a decision that touches the shared `jobQueue` contract, not this one. | 117 | **A job whose RUN was withdrawn by an owner action settles SILENTLY, while a run that failed on its own stays loud: the unattended encounter-map queue no longer hands the owner his own stop back as a failure** (the RESIDUE row 116 measured and deliberately left, verbatim: *"the map queue's own failure verdict for a stopped run is a real surface, measured, older than this row, and out of this slice's frozen test files"*). **The defect, with its three-variant evidence.** The unattended Cartographer queue waits for its run through `waitForRunStatus` (`src/features/modules/encounter-map-queue.ts:124`) and then threw for ANY terminal state other than `completed` (`:135`: `throw new Error(run.errorMessage \|\| \`run ended ${run.status}\`)`), which the shared factory turns into a failure toast plus a retryable `failed` entry (`src/lib/jobQueue.ts:202` toast, `:224` failed list). Withdrawn means the run was cancelled by the owner or its row deliberately deleted; silent means no error toast, no retryable failed entry, no failed-run row. **The fix, CENTRALIZED rather than re-derived (AGENTS rule 4, added mid-slice and binding).** The fact "this run was WITHDRAWN by an owner action" gets ONE name, owned by the engine: `isRunWithdrawn(run)` (`src/llm/runEngine.ts:372`, beside `isTerminalRunStatus`) — true when the row is `'cancelled'` (the owner's Stop, Stop all, every cancel seam) or when the row is GONE (every run-row delete stops the run FIRST, row 116, so a vanished row is that same gesture seen one step later); false for a run that died on its own (`'failed'` with its `errorMessage`, INCLUDING `failRunningRuns`' reload reconcile, whose `failureKind: 'cancelled'` names the KIND of failure and not a withdrawal). The queue reads that ONE predicate — never an error's kind, never its message — and the verdict crosses to the factory through an explicit, additive seam: `ctx.withdraw()` (`src/lib/jobQueue.ts:63`), which is the SAME settlement as `dequeue`'s (abort the job's signal, no toast, no `failed` entry, dock counter decremented rather than ticked done), because a withdrawal the queue did not initiate must look exactly like the one it did. ONE withdrawal path serves both doors (`withdrawJob`, `src/lib/jobQueue.ts:203`): idempotent per key, and the per-key counter debt is cleared when that key is enqueued again (a withdrawal is spent per JOB, not per key). `processJob` additionally guarantees that an ABORTED job can never settle as `'done'`/`'skipped'`/`'failed'`, whatever the body returns. In the queue body the verdict is read from the ROW in both shapes: after a normal wait (`if (isRunWithdrawn(run))` → `ctx.withdraw()`), and after a THROWN wait, where the row is re-read (`getRun`, `src/features/modules/encounter-map-queue.ts:140`) precisely so "the row disappeared" is classified by the fact and not by the error. Loudness is untouched and pinned: the `run.status !== 'completed'` throw stays for a genuine failure, and the cheap wrong fix (swallowing every non-completed status) REDs three pins, two of them pre-existing. **Callers FOLDED onto the predicate (rule 4), each semantics-identical for a row that exists** (`isRunWithdrawn(defined row)` ≡ `row.status === 'cancelled'`): `src/features/modules/entity-batch.ts:501` (was `outcome.status === 'cancelled'`; the pool stops on a user stop), `src/llm/chainRunner.ts:201` (was `outcome.status === 'cancelled' ? 'cancelled' : 'failed'`) and `:318` (was `if (outcome.status === 'cancelled')` — the chain ends `'cancelled'`, never `'failed'`). The unmount guarantee is unchanged in substance (a navigation still flushes) and is now the SAME function as the page-hide one, so the two cannot drift; the fire-and-forget contract is unchanged (a lifecycle handler cannot hold the page open, and `persistLayout` still toasts by name on failure); nothing new was added to `lib/pageFlush.ts` (byte-identical: `git hash-object` `1d1eef7424e22680db5e0d6b591adca90771ef78` before and after) — no second listener, no Dexie version, no new write path. **THE SWEEP (the point of rule 4), with verdicts.** Every `setTimeout`/`requestIdleCallback`/`setInterval` in `src/**` was read, plus every `useEffect` cleanup that writes: (1) `features/modules/canvas/chatPersist.ts` — already on the seam; (2) `features/modules/new-module-dialog.tsx` — already on the seam; (3) `features/modules/board/BoardPage.tsx` — FOLDED (this row); (4) **`features/campaign/components/artifact-editor.tsx` — FOUND AND FOLDED**: its autosave debounces 800 ms (`AUTOSAVE_DELAY_MS`, `:83`; the timer itself at `:261`, whose effect is at `:262-270` before this change) and flushed only on unmount (a one-effect cleanup, `:276-281` before this change), so a tab closed with an edit inside the window lost it AND left no revision to restore it from — the identical hole, one file over; it now registers the SAME pending-gated `saveDraft` (`:283-315`), which is already gated by its `deepEqual(effective, lastSavedRef.current)` early return and idempotent because a successful write moves `lastSavedRef` onto what it wrote, so a tab switch writes nothing and fires no revision; (5) `features/rules/search-browser.tsx:54` — a debounced SEARCH FILTER over in-memory state, no row write (verdict: not this class); (6) `features/dice/useDiceEngine.ts:120`, `features/images/zoomable-image.tsx:259`, `features/campaign/components/persona-panel.tsx:1555/1807` — animation/UI timers, no row write; (7) `llm/openrouter.ts`, `ingest/packFetch.ts:679`, `lib/exportImport.ts:980`, `services` — in-flight network/export timers whose work is owned by a visible run row, an explicit Stop, or a download the owner just started (verdict: out of this class — a pagehide flush cannot land a network call, and a QUEUE that survives reloads is not a debounce); (8) `features/onboarding/SetupWizardDialog.tsx:65` — writes are event-driven and awaited with an idempotence set, no debounce (verdict: not this class); (9) the module-version/draft stores and `boardStore`/`stagedRewrites` are SESSION-only by design (nothing to flush). | 121 | **"Add this name to an artifact's alias pool" was hand-rolled SIX times and had drifted into THREE comparison rules — on the one field that decides whether `[[wiki links]]` resolve — and is now ONE comparator plus ONE merge rule in `domain/artifactAlias.ts` with ONE write path in `artifactRepo`** (audit finding, every `file:line` re-verified at the base commit `276f41f7beac394234fabdbfb9aa9aeb7b2844de` before anything changed). **Why this field and not a cosmetic copy.** `AnyArtifact.aliases` is the pool `lib/wikilinks.resolveWikiLink` answers a link from (`:175-177`, name first then aliases, both trimmed), so a MISSING alias is a dead link and a DUPLICATED alias is a second row in the alias editor for one name. **What the audit found.** The six copies, as they stood at the base SHA: `src/llm/runEngine.ts:5757-5761` (the prose-only redesign: guards `nextName` OR `target.name`, appends `target.name`), `src/llm/runEngine.ts:5786-5800` (the encounter refill: filter-out-then-append when renamed, guard-and-append otherwise — two shapes 15 lines apart), `src/llm/runEngine.ts:6001-6005` (the generate-persona refill), `src/llm/moduleGen.ts:2232-2240` (`applyNormalizationVerdict`: a map of artifactId→variants, `filter` against the pool, one `updateArtifact` per artifact), `src/features/modules/stub-popover.tsx:175-181` (`needsAlias` — the ONLY copy that also skipped a name equal to the artifact's own name), `src/features/modules/ModuleReaderPage.tsx:281-284` (the ONLY copy that compared UNTRIMMED). Three comparison rules, and the live consequence the audit named: a stored alias `"Kael "` made the reader append a duplicate `"Kael"` that `entity-batch.alignEntityName` skipped. **WHAT LANDED.** (1) `src/domain/artifactAlias.ts` (NEW, pure — no Dexie, no React): `sameAliasName(left, right)` is the ONE comparison and `mergeAliasNames(existing, names, artifactName)` the ONE merge rule. The rule it owns, stated in its doc comment: compare TRIMMED and case-insensitively; a name equal to the artifact's OWN name (same comparison) is NOT an alias (`resolveWikiLink` matches the name first, so such an alias could never resolve — `alias-editor.tsx`'s own header says the same at the form); never store a duplicate, neither against the pool nor against an earlier name of the same batch; and return the caller's list UNCHANGED — the SAME reference — when nothing is added, which is how a caller knows to skip its write. **STORED SPELLING, decided here:** the accepted name is stored EXACTLY as the caller passed it, never re-trimmed and never re-cased — the comparison trims, the ROW does not. Three of the six call sites hand the rule a row's own name and one hands it a hand-typed link name; re-trimming would have changed stored bytes at those sites for no resolution gain (the resolver trims on read), and it is what keeps five of the six folds byte-identical. (2) `src/db/artifactRepo.addArtifactAliases(id, names, meta)` is the ONE WRITE PATH: it reads the row INSIDE its own `rw` transaction over artifacts+revisions (the neighbouring `stampModuleOwnership` shape, so a caller's stale snapshot cannot clobber a concurrent alias), applies `mergeAliasNames`, and writes ONE revisioned save — or returns `null` with **nothing written** when the pool already answers (a missing row is a loud `NotFoundError`, never a `null`, because `null` means success-with-no-write to every caller). (3) **All six sites folded.** `moduleGen`, `stub-popover` and `ModuleReaderPage` now call the write path; the three `runEngine` sites and `entity-batch.alignEntityName` call the shared RULE directly (`mergeAliasNames`/`sameAliasName`) and put the result in their OWN patch, because their alias rides a combined content write (summary/body/provenance/data in one revision, and a rename for entity-batch) — a second `updateArtifact` there would split one save into two revisions and leave the alias on the row when the content write fails. That is a deliberate RULE-only fold, not an oversight. (4) `entity-batch.alignEntityName`'s comparison is folded (both the early self-name return and the "does this name already answer?" dedupe) but the REST of `alignEntityName` is not: deciding WHICH artifact a name BELONGS to (`libraryCitationForEntity`, the creature lookup, `moduleCreationPool`) is a different question. **DELIBERATELY NOT FOLDED, named as boundaries rather than oversights (docs/18 §2.1):** `src/lib/wikilinks.ts:177` (the RESOLVER — it answers a link against the pool; folding it would tie resolution precedence to the merge rule) and `src/features/campaign/components/alias-editor.tsx:48-50` (the FORM — it REJECTS a keystroke a person just typed, which is UI feedback, not a row merge). **FINDINGS RATHER THAN SMOOTH LANDINGS (all four measured at the base SHA).** (a) **The intended behaviour change "moduleGen stops writing an alias equal to the artifact's own name" DOES NOT EXIST at HEAD and cannot be pinned behaviourally**: the alias pass only records a variant when `canonicalKey !== nameKey` (`moduleGen.ts:2221`, `if (canonicalKey === nameKey) continue;`) while the artifact it attaches to is found BY that canonical key (`:2224`), so the variant is structurally guaranteed ≠ the artifact's own name under this very comparison — the fold there is byte-identical and the rule is defence, pinned at the seam and the write path plus the scan, with nothing behavioural to write. (b) **The reader's untrimmed comparison was reachable only through the popover's editable Name field**: the resolver TRIMS (so a stored `"Kael "` always resolves `[[Kael]]`) and the reader's resolution pool (all campaign rows + the library, `ModuleReaderPage.tsx:124`) is a SUPERSET of the picker's (`useScopedArtifacts('moduleView')`), so an unresolved chip can never reach `linkExisting` with a name the picked artifact already answers; the pin therefore drives the real route (edit the Name field, then "Use existing entity…") and is RED before the fix. (c) **A SEVENTH copy the audit did not count — `src/features/campaign/components/campaign-tree.tsx:313-317`** (rename-keep-alias, UNTRIMMED comparison, hand-appended pool). It is left UNFOLDED on purpose: folding it is a THIRD behaviour change (whitespace-differing dedupe + no self-alias) and this slice was told to report rather than smuggle; it is recorded in docs/18 §5 known debt with the exact four-line patch, and the scan names it as a documented carve-out so folding it later cannot turn the pin red. (d) **Two rule consequences that are fixes, not questions of taste, and are pinned:** `runEngine`'s prose-only branch used to guard the pool against the NEW name and then append the OLD one unconditionally, so a row whose pool already spelled its own name got that name written TWICE (`encounterRepopulate.test.ts`, the new "without writing the old name TWICE" pin, RED at base); and `moduleGen` compared a verdict's variants against the POOL only, so one verdict naming a variant twice wrote two aliases that both resolve (`domain/artifactAlias` rule table). **TESTS.** New: `tests/domain/artifactAlias.test.ts` (7 — the rule table: trim, case, self-name, within-batch dedupe, same-reference no-op, verbatim storage, and the `"Kael "` agreement), `tests/db/artifactRepo-alias.test.ts` (6 — the write path's row contract: one revision per real add, NOTHING on a no-op, idempotence, concurrent calls merging, loud NotFound), `tests/features/alias-merge-seam.test.ts` (7 — the SOURCE SCAN, labelled as a scan: the two hand-rolled shapes exist in exactly the three documented carve-outs, and each folded file routes its write through the seam with a COUNTED number of calls), plus one behavioural pin per reachable fold: the reader UI pin in `module-reader.test.tsx` and the prose-only duplicate pin in `encounterRepopulate.test.ts`. | 122 | **A fully green suite came back RED: the page-hide flush's own retry settled after the jsdom environment was torn down, `setSaveState` threw `ReferenceError: window is not defined` into an unhandled rejection, and the root cause is TEST-SIDE — every production path on the seam is correct** (found by the dispatcher, not by any test: the gate exited 1 while reporting **288 files / 3324 tests green** plus an `Unhandled Errors` block, `This error originated in "tests/features/editor-page-flush.test.tsx"`). **REPRODUCED FIRST, DETERMINISTICALLY, rather than by repeating suites.** Instrumenting `globalThis.window` with an accessor that records the reader reproduced the dispatcher's stack **verbatim** on the pre-fix tree, in ONE isolated run of `tests/features/editor-page-flush.test.tsx`: `getCurrentEventPriority (react-dom.development.js:10993) ← requestUpdateLane (:25495) ← dispatchSetState (:16648) ← src/features/campaign/components/artifact-editor.tsx:251:7` — the **SUCCESS** line `setSaveState('saved')`, never the `setSaveState('error')` the brief suspected — followed by `Unhandled Rejection: ReferenceError: window is not defined`. A second probe (a write deferred by one macrotask with the test ending before it settled) produced the same unhandled rejection, which is the same mechanism in its simplest form. **WHICH OF (a)/(b)/(c): (c), the environment tearing down mid-write, with (b) — a deferred promise chain settling after the test finished — as its condition. NOT (a): the write does not fail here at all.** **THE RACE, STEP BY STEP, each step measured.** (1) The page-hide flush issues `saveDraft()`, which fails against the pin's `refuseRevisionWrites` stub, so `lastSavedRef.current` does **not** move — `setSaveState('error')` and the `Autosave failed` toast are the outcome the test asserts, and they arrive inside the test. (2) That leaves `saveDraft`'s pending gate **OPEN**, and the editor's registration effect cleanup is `unregister(); flushPendingEdits();` — so the UNMOUNT flush re-issues the very same draft. (3) The unmount is `cleanup()` in `tests/setup.ts`'s `afterEach`, and vitest runs a FILE's own `afterEach`s BEFORE the config/setup ones, so by then the pin's `finally { restore() }` has **already** put the real `revisions.put` back. (4) The retry therefore runs against the REAL Dexie/fake-indexeddb path, which takes longer to settle than the environment's teardown takes to start; the flush is fire-and-forget by design (a lifecycle handler cannot hold the page open), so when the promise settles there is no environment left, its continuation calls `setSaveState`, React dispatches, `window` is gone, and the `ReferenceError` reaches nobody. MEASURED where it fires: a probe logging the seam, the repository and both Dexie tables showed the unmount flush's `updateArtifact` arriving exactly ONCE, from `cleanup()`, **after** the file's own `afterEach` had finished and after every write the test body awaited — and the same probe showed that test body's own awaited write settling INSIDE the test. **WHY IT WAS FLAKY RATHER THAN RED.** The defect reproduced in isolation on demand, yet the pre-fix tree also ran a FULL suite green (`288/288`, `3324/3324`, exit 0 on this box, twice; one earlier baseline carried a failure in `tests/rules-page.test.tsx`, a separate pre-existing flake). It needs the teardown to land inside one write's settlement window — a race against the worker's teardown, not a logic error — which is exactly why the dispatcher saw it and no test did. **NOT A PRODUCT DEFECT, AND THE SEAM'S CONTRACT IS WHY.** `src/lib/pageFlush.ts`'s BEHAVIOUR is unchanged by this row — its only edit is the contract's new item 4 in the doc comment (no statement of executable code differs; the base tree's hash was `c8e0db5ff9cf6ea2c54c25a44b5986d79686496e` and the file is otherwise byte-identical) — no writer changed, no new catch anywhere: the flush's synchronous `try`/`catch` + seam `toastError` already owns every throw the seam can see, and all FOUR registrations declare `(): void` and were MEASURED to return `undefined` (an instrumented seam printed the return value of every `flush()` call across the editor's suite and produced nothing — no promise ever reaches the seam to be caught). **THE RULE, IN ONE PLACE.** A page-hide flush cannot reject unhandled because it RETURNS NOTHING: `PageFlush` is `() => void`, each writer discards its own async work at the call site (`void saveDraft()`, `void persistLayout()`, `void flushChatPersist()`), and the seam's synchronous `try`/`catch` therefore has nothing asynchronous to miss. That contract is stated in the seam's own doc comment and in docs/18 §2.3/§4 — NOT re-implemented as a defensive `.catch` per caller, which would have hidden a future writer's real unhandled rejection (the class this row exists to keep visible) and would have double-reported every failure the writers already toast by name (AGENTS rules 1–2). **What the TYPE does and does not do, MEASURED because the first draft of this row got it wrong:** `() => void` is NOT enforced by the compiler against an `async` flush — an `async () => {}` argument typechecks clean against `PageFlush` (verified with `tsc --noEmit` on a probe file, exit 0), so the void-returning item is a CONVENTION held by the four writers and verified by measurement, not by the type system. The seam's doc comment says so rather than implying a guarantee that does not exist. **WHAT LANDED.** `tests/features/editor-page-flush.test.tsx`'s failing-write pin, and nothing else: after the refusal is lifted the pin now settles the retry IT started — through the same seam (`flushPageHide()`), waiting for the revision row to reach 2 within the file's existing `WRITE_LANDED` bound (`AUTOSAVE_DELAY_MS + 1000`) and asserting through the file's existing `countWrites` instrument that the write was ISSUED, so the 800 ms debounce cannot be what landed it. Once `lastSavedRef` holds the written draft the gate closes, so the unmount flush `cleanup()` runs is the gate's NO-OP — no write, no state update, nothing left to settle after teardown. No test deleted, skipped or weakened: the file's other five pins are byte-unchanged, the file still reports **6 passed**, and no test file was added or removed. **The retry's `countWrites` instrument and the retry's own dispatch both came back GREEN under injection, and that is reported rather than implied** — with the component still mounted the 800 ms debounce also lands the write inside the pin's wait bound, so what the pin ENFORCES is that nothing is in flight at teardown (the wait), while the dispatch and the counter are the direct trigger and the wrong-reason guard. The pre-fix tree had neither a wait nor a dispatch: `cleanup()`'s unmount retry re-issued the write after the file's `afterEach` had restored the real writer, which is the measurement in docs/08. **PIN.** The retry block IS the pin: reverting it is reverting the fix (the file then leaves the unmount retry in flight at teardown again — the probe's measurement). **UNPROVEN.** (1) A FUTURE writer could register a `Promise`-returning flush and leak a rejection the seam cannot see — and this is NOT merely a cast: MEASURED, an `async () => {}` argument is accepted by `PageFlush` with no diagnostic at all (probe: `tsc --noEmit`, exit 0), so the compiler does not stand in the way. No runtime guard exists and none was built — a detector would be a second production mechanism serving a test-only race — so the only defence is the contract, the measurement, and the seam's doc comment naming the gap. (2) NO FREQUENCY IS CLAIMED: the race is timing-dependent by construction, seen by the dispatcher, reproduced twice on demand by probe, and NOT reproduced by two full pre-fix suite runs here, so how often it fires is unknown. (3) The other three writers were audited by READING their gates against this mechanism — the board nulls `persistTimer` before writing and the draft writer's page-hide half returns early unless `timerRef.current !== null`, so neither can re-issue on unmount — but that audit is a source reading, not an injection; only the editor's flush was driven red. | `tests/features/editor-page-flush.test.tsx` (the failing-write pin: the lifted-refusal retry, awaited and counted); `docs/18-ARCHITECTURE.md` §2.3 (the seam row's contract) + §4 (the new gotcha); `docs/08-TESTING.md` §The page-hide flush's retry; this row. **NOT touched, byte-identical:** `src/features/campaign/components/artifact-editor.tsx` (`a5ab04cf5b762d56336be7b678b6e7dccbd9441c`), the other three registered writers, `tests/setup.ts`, `vite.config.ts`, and the file's other five pins | The brief's own rule decided the design: *"If the honest root cause is test-side (the test finishes with a pending flush it never awaited), then fix the TEST to settle what it starts — and say plainly that the product path was correct, rather than adding defensive code to production."* The measurement says exactly that, so that is what landed. A seam-level `.catch(() => {})` would have silenced the rejection instead of containing it — hiding a real unhandled rejection from a future writer, the class this row exists to keep visible — and a seam-level toast would have reported every failure twice, since the writers already toast their own by name. The rule is therefore stated where it binds (the seam's type, the seam's contract, §4) rather than re-implemented at each of the four callers (AGENTS rule 4). |

| 124 | **A negative DOM assertion on TRANSIENT UI is a flaky pin, not a broken assertion and not an app defect: the held-reason pin asserted the absence of a Base UI tooltip popup at an instant the APP could open it, and the cure is ONE seam that AWAITS the framework's own transition instead of sleeping or loosening** (found by the dispatcher: a full gate came back `exit 1` with **3345 of 3346 passing**, `FAIL \|jsdom\| tests/rules-page.test.tsx > rules screen > states why the import/embed controls are held: the page-wide import, the per-book embed, the delete icon and both menu items`, `Error: expect(element).not.toBeInTheDocument()`, `found <div class="z-50 …" data-slot="tooltip-content" data-instant="delay" data-open="" data-side="bottom">`; a second identical run was green 3346/3346). **WHAT WAS MEASURED, not guessed: which popup, and who opened it.** The element was the reason popup of **the very control under assertion** (`…-blocked-reason`, `data-open` — OPEN, not closing) and `document.activeElement` was that control's own wrapper (`SPAN[retry-book-<id>-blocked]`, `SPAN[embed-book-<id>-blocked]`). So it was not a leftover from an earlier test (`cleanup()` unmounts between tests), not the test's own pointer (the helper's `hover` had not run yet at that assertion) and not a permanently rendered node: **the APP opened it by putting FOCUS on the held control** — a Base UI menu places focus on the held item's wrapper as it opens (`useFocusableWhenDisabled`; the `aria-disabled` item is not natively focusable, the wrapper's `tabIndex=0` is the tab stop) and `BlockedControl` opens the reason on focus **by design** (docs/05 §Why a control cannot act, docs/18 §2.3). An in-test event trace (`pointerover`/`focusin`/`pointerdown` in capture phase + a `MutationObserver` on `body`) pinned the blocking window: the `focusin` landed on the wrapper ~2ms AFTER the assertion in a green run, and BEFORE it in a red one. **THE PIN'S MEANING IS CORRECT, SO THE APP WAS NOT "FIXED".** The reason really does travel through `BlockedControl` — the wrapper is the tooltip trigger, focusable while blocked, `aria-describedby`-associated with the hidden sentence — and never through a `title` on a natively disabled control that Chrome will not show (docs/18 §4). This is a **test-side race**, and the brief's own rule was followed: the test settles what it starts. **BOTH EDGES OF THE POPUP ARE THE FRAMEWORK'S TRANSITIONS.** Base UI removes it when the exit animation finishes — `internals/useAnimationsFinished` waits one `requestAnimationFrame` + a microtask, then `flushSync(forceUnmount)`; jsdom always takes that path because `tests/setup.ts` stubs `Element.getAnimations` to `[]`. Measured consequence beyond the red sighting: a popup also lingers present-but-CLOSED (`…-blocked-reason:closed` observed in the document at the next control's assertion) while its exit frame is pending. **REPRODUCED DETERMINISTICALLY BY DELAYING THE CAUSE (`89e5d71` method), never by loading the box** (one file, `CAMPAIGNER_TEST_WORKERS=2`, one run at a time): the pre-fix tree as-is was **GREEN 10 runs of 10** in isolation (repetition never finds this — the dispatcher's full-gate timing did); with **ONE macrotask injected at the exact site** (the helper's step immediately before the absence assertion) it was **RED 3/3**, the popup of the control under assertion open with `activeElement` = its own `…-blocked` wrapper; with the delay expressed in-tree as `settleAppFocus()` and the **fix reverted** it was **RED 2/2** — the dispatcher's exact signature; and with the fix AND the probe removed it was **GREEN 3/3**, which is the measurement that makes the delayed cause the only honest proof here. **THE FIX, ONE SEAM (AGENTS rule 4):** `tests/helpers/blocked-reason.ts → dismissOpenPopup` — (1) `flushAsyncUpdates()` (the suite's own drain seam) so whatever the app already scheduled has landed, (2) hand back the two triggers the app can have used (`user.unhover(trigger)`, `document.activeElement.blur()`), (3) `await waitFor(() => expect(popup).not.toBeInTheDocument())`. Step 3 is an await of a REAL transition — not a sleep, not a retry — and it is also the non-vacuity half: a popup that cannot leave fails LOUDLY on the timeout instead of the pin passing on a wrapper that opens nothing. All nine files that pin a reason now go through that one seam; the in-tree `settleAppFocus()` stays at the two menu-item sites as the falsifier, and `tests/rules-page.test.tsx`'s other assertions are untouched. **Nothing was widened to make it quiet:** no `setTimeout` sleep, no assertion loosened, no `--retry`, no test deleted or skipped, no `ALLOWED_NOISE` entry, and `src/components/blocked-control.tsx` is byte-identical. **SEQUENTIAL REPETITION OF THE SINGLE AFFECTED FILE** (`CAMPAIGNER_TEST_WORKERS=2 pnpm exec vitest run tests/rules-page.test.tsx`, foreground, one after another): **green 8/8** with the fix in place, against the pre-fix tree's own 10/10 in isolation (the same measurement that says repetition is not evidence). **INJECTIONS, every one applied, printed with `grep -n`, `git diff --stat` checked, and restored byte-identically (`git hash-object` identical before and after), INCLUDING the green one:** the pre-fix synchronous assertion restored at `blocked-reason.ts:91` → **RED 2/2**; the `blur()` removed → **RED**; the awaited `waitFor` replaced by a synchronous assertion → **RED**; an always-rendered `data-testid="…-blocked-reason"` node injected into `src/components/blocked-control.tsx:93` → **RED naming that node** (the guard still has teeth); and **GREEN: removing the helper's internal drain leaves every current pin green** (`rules-page` 7/7 + 9 helper-consumer files, 69/69), because the only two call sites where the app's focus is actually pending pre-drain in-tree (`settleAppFocus`) — the line is KEPT as step 1 of the seam because without it steps 2–3 race the app's pending focus at every other call site and every caller would have to remember to drain first. **UNPROVEN.** (1) No real-browser measurement — jsdom has no layout or hit-testing, so that the popup paints over the control remains a DOM contract only (row 98's honest limit). (2) Base UI's exact focus scheduler was not traced into the framework (measured only as "one macrotask is enough"). (3) Which of the four menu items Base UI picks was not determined — only that the HELD item's wrapper receives the focus, in both menus that have one. (4) The internal drain has no failing pin of its own (the green injection above). | `tests/helpers/blocked-reason.ts` (`dismissOpenPopup` + the rewritten `assertReason` step); `tests/rules-page.test.tsx` (`settleAppFocus` + its two call sites); `docs/18-ARCHITECTURE.md` §4; `docs/08-TESTING.md` §A negative DOM assertion on transient UI; this row. **NOT touched, byte-identical:** `src/components/blocked-control.tsx` (`d66883a57bcd163c2ce36597e296d4a0f2bbddc0`), `src/components/ui/tooltip.tsx`, `src/features/rules/RulesPage.tsx`, `tests/setup.ts`, `vite.config.ts`, and the other seven assertions in `rules-page.test.tsx` | A gate that fails intermittently teaches everyone to re-run until green, and that habit hides real failures — the same class of harm as the unhandled rejection row 122 fixed, one file over. The two cheap cures were both refused on measurement: a `setTimeout` sleep guesses a duration the framework owns (the exit is an rAF plus a microtask, not a fixed delay), and loosening the assertion would stop pinning the only property that makes the popup pin meaningful — that the reason appears BECAUSE the control was interacted with rather than being always present. Awaiting the framework's own removal keeps the guard's teeth (an always-rendered popup cannot be dismissed and fails on the timeout, proved by injection) and makes the pin deterministic at the same time. The cure went into the SHARED helper rather than into this file because nine files pin the same contract, and a per-file fix would have left the eighth caller to re-derive it. | active |

'<what the action does>'}` on `disabled={derivedBlocked !== null \|\| fixRunning\/resumeRunning}`, with the wrapper's `reason={… : derivedBlocked}`: here only HALF the title was the reason, and the other half was real copy. `entity-panel.tsx:907` — `title={batchGateReason}` where the wrapper's `reason` at `:896-901` opens with that very expression. `entity-panel.tsx:936-961` — a two-branch title whose first branch is byte-identical to the wrapper's `reason` at `:924`, 17 lines apart. '…' : '…'}`, under a comment at `:723-724` that blesses the restatement verbatim — *"its own sentence, already in the `title`"*). Seven, not five, and the shape is a PATTERN rather than five accidents — which is why the fix is a rule plus a scan, not five deletions. **THE BRIEF'S OWN CLAIM, CORRECTED RATHER THAN ABSORBED.** The brief said the classify control's title carried copy the wrapper's `reason` does NOT contain (*"…its own normalization pass records the names when the parts land"*). MEASURED: that sentence IS the wrapper's `reason` at `:924`, verbatim — the title's first branch was a copy of the REASON, not new copy. The branch NOT in the reason is the SECOND one, the enabled-state description (`'Classify the names this text picked up since the last pass (one model call, the same pass as at creation) — their batch buttons then appear.'`), which is the one worth keeping. **Per-site decisions, so nothing was lost silently.** canvas-save `title` REMOVED outright (a total restatement; a comment in the source now says why). `batch-${kind}` `title` REMOVED outright (same, and `batchGateReason` is left with ONE reader — the wrapper's `reason`). `canvas-fix-problems`, `canvas-resume-automation` and `entity-classify-new` KEEP their enabled-state DESCRIPTION, rewritten as `title={blocked ? undefined : '…'}` so it can only ever appear on a control that can act: `'Rewrite the parts whose text falls short of the encounter floor'`, `'Generate only what creation was asked to automate and the module does not have yet'` and the classify sentence all survive BYTE-IDENTICAL, and each is now pinned on the LIVE control as well as proven absent while held. NOTHING was reworded, moved or dropped. **What landed, per surface.** `CanvasPage.tsx`: the save child has no title; `fixBlocked` and `resumeBlocked` are ONE expression each (`derivedBlocked !== null \|\| fixRunning` and `\|\| resumeRunning`), read by the child's `disabled` and by the description's gate, so the gate cannot disagree with itself. `entity-panel.tsx`: the batch child has no title; the classify control's reason is lifted to a body `classifyBlockedReason` (arms byte-identical) and its gate to `classifyBlocked`, so that surface has ONE reason expression and ONE gate expression and the sentence is written once, in one file. The two are deliberately NOT merged into the reason's null-ness: `classifying` and `normalizing` are held with `reason === null` ON PURPOSE, because their labels read "Classifying…" and the block is self-evident (docs/18 §2.3). **PINS, AND THE TWO PRE-EXISTING PINS THAT ASSERTED THE DEFECT.** Two existing pins named the invisible copy and could not stay byte-identical once it was gone: `tests/features/module-canvas.test.tsx:303` (its own title was *"a blocked Save states its reason in title"*, asserting `toHaveAttribute('title', …)`) and `tests/features/canvas-module-actions.test.tsx:397` (the Fix control's `title` while held). Both now assert the reason through the shared device AND `not.toHaveAttribute('title')` — and the Fix control's other pin asserts the description while it is LIVE. NEW: `tests/features/blocked-control-title-scan.test.ts` (2, a labelled SOURCE scan: the violation list by EQUALITY against a two-entry known list, plus a coverage pin that the scanner reaches all six named wrappers and that exactly five wrappers in `src/` carry ANY title); +2 behavioural pins in `entity-classify-new.test.tsx` (held-while-generating states the reason and carries no title; the description present while live) and +1 in `canvas-module-actions.test.tsx` for the same pair on Resume; the batch-gate pin in `entity-classify-new.test.tsx` gains the no-title half. The pins the brief named as must-stay-green all pass untouched: `blocked-control.test.tsx`, `blocked-reasons.test.tsx`, `blocked-reasons-entity-sweep.test.tsx`, `generate-everything.test.tsx:648,657` and `spine-checkpoint.test.tsx` (5 files, 40 tests, green). **INJECTIONS — every one applied, printed back with `grep -n`, `git diff --stat` checked BEFORE the run, and restored byte-identically (`git hash-object` identical before and after, all six files).** RED: I1 the canvas-save title restored (`CanvasPage.tsx:1710`) → 3 failed (both scan pins + the module-canvas pin); I2 the batch `title={batchGateReason}` restored (`entity-panel.tsx:929`) → 3 failed (both scan pins + the batch-gate pin); I3 the classify title's reason branch restored (`:953`) → 2 failed (the scan's `branch` rule + the classify pin); I4 the Fix control's `title` back to `derivedBlocked ?? '…'` (`CanvasPage.tsx:1546`) → 2 failed (scan + its held pin); I5 the same for Resume (`:1570`) → 2 failed (scan + its held pin); I6 the reason sentence's VALUE changed → 1 failed (the classify pin); I7 `module.status === 'generating'` dropped from the gate → 1 failed (the pin's `toBeDisabled()` half); I8 the description made UNGATED → 1 failed (the held-state no-title half); I9 a known-list entry disturbed → 1 failed (the equality assertion); I10 the classify DESCRIPTION deleted → 2 failed (the scan's population pin + the live-description pin); I12 the Save reason's sentence changed → 1 failed (`module-canvas.test`); I13 the batch gate reason's sentence changed → 2 failed (`entity-classify-new.test` + `entity-panel.test`). **GREEN, named rather than dressed as coverage.** (a) The FIRST run of I3 left the scan GREEN: the `branch` rule compares literals against the `reason={…}` expression, and a reason collapsed into a body `const` puts its sentence OUTSIDE the span, so a title restating a COLLAPSED reason read as clean. The scan now resolves a bare-identifier `reason` ONE level to its `const` declaration (re-run of I3: the RED 2 above), and the measurement is recorded in docs/18 §4 — the line the pins did not reach was the scan's own resolution. (b) I11 un-collapsing the classify reason (inline ternary, byte-identical arms, the `const` deleted) → 3 files, 40/40 GREEN: a fold of this kind has no behavioural detector by construction (row 123's shape); what the pins reach is the TITLE duplication, not the collapse. (c) Under I2, `entity-panel.test.tsx` (28 tests) stayed GREEN — no pin there reads the batch child's title; only the scan and the classify file's batch-gate pin do. (d) Under I4 the Fix control's LIVE-description pin stayed GREEN, because with the control live `derivedBlocked ?? '…'` and `fixBlocked ? undefined : '…'` produce the SAME string — only the held-state pin and the scan reach the gating. (e) Under I6 and I8 the scan stayed GREEN: a changed VALUE and an ungated description are not restatements, and that boundary is held by behaviour, not by the scan. **WHAT THIS SLICE DID NOT FOLD, named with its licence rather than left silent.** The two sites re-verification found are held as KNOWN entries in the scan's equality list: `entity-panel.tsx`'s `generate-everything` (its reason is restated in the title, and it is pinned AS a title by `tests/features/generate-everything.test.tsx:646` and `:676` — pins this slice was told to keep passing and did not rewrite) and `artifact-editor.tsx`'s `encounter-repopulate`. (2) The scan is textual: a sentence COMPOSED at runtime is invisible to it, and a `reason` identifier is resolved only ONE level (a reason named through a second alias is invisible to the `branch` rule). The cure is stated where the seam index can hold it and pinned where a scan can: the wrapper's `reason` is the only home of a reason, and a `title` inside a wrapper is at most a DESCRIPTION, gated on the control being able to act.
