| 131 | **The batch-failure report was a four-second one-liner that called three different outcomes "failed to generate"** — the owner ran module generation and got `4 of 10 npcs failed to generate`, *"the error was a lot longer, vanished quickly though"*, and asked for it to be *"a lot more expressive … and put it in the console"*. MEASURED, not inferred: (a) the copy was composed character-for-character at TWO call sites (`entity-panel`'s batch button and `post-generation`'s unattended sweep) and raised through **`toastError`, which passes no `duration`** — so sonner's `TOAST_LIFETIME` (4000 ms, `node_modules/sonner/dist/index.mjs:470`; the app's `<Toaster>`, `app/layout/AppShell.tsx:229`, sets none) dismissed the only record of the batch before it could be read; (b) the console said **nothing** on this path (the only `console.error` in `lib/toast.ts` is the zod dump and the module-busy branch), and the progress dock drops its job on finish with no failure field, so the toast was the WHOLE record; (c) the `failed` array conflated a by-design cast-creature REFUSAL, a run that did not complete, an interruption by page reload, and a setup throw. **THE CURE (owner's mechanism declined in favour of his intent — "a longer toast" is the wrong carrier for a payload that must be selectable, copyable and still there):** ONE reporting seam, `features/modules/entity-batch-report.reportEntityBatchFailures({ campaign, module, kind, total, failures })`, which the two call sites now share and which raises BOTH surfaces from one call so they cannot drift — a **`[campaigner] <kind> batch: N of M failed`** console entry carrying structured objects (per entity: name, which path, run id, terminal status, the run's own `failureKind` + `errorMessage`, the engine's sentence, the RAW value, and a validation failure's issues as objects), and a **PERSISTENT** toast (`toastErrorPersistent`, `duration: Infinity` — the helper `lib/toast` documents for "a failure nothing else caught", and for a refusal or a setup throw there is no failed run row at all). `EntityBatchFailure` gained `kind` (`refused` / `interrupted` / `run-not-completed` / `setup-error`), `runId`, `status`, `failureKind`, `errorMessage` and `raw` — rule 1 is satisfied by the RECORD, not by asking the reporting layer to re-derive it. **THE ROOT PROBLEM, NAMED BY THE OWNER AFTERWARDS** — *"the root problem is simply not recorded. Thats what i initially wanted to have, when that 4 second error message pops up to have something in the console to post back to you"* — refined two things, both landed in this row: (a) a failure is recorded AT THE MOMENT IT HAPPENS, not only at batch end, because a batch can die mid-flight (his four failures were followed by his own Stop; had the page or the batch gone first, an end-of-batch dump would never have been written and the evidence would be gone) — so every failure goes through ONE funnel inside `runEntityBatch` (`recordFailure`, the only line that appends to the list, pinned by a scan) which writes the record through the seam as it happens; (b) every record ALSO exists as a PASTEABLE single-line JSON string under a stable tag (`[campaigner] entity-batch failure {…}` per failure, `[campaigner] entity-batch summary {…}` per batch), emitted as ONE string argument so that "copy this line" is faithful in any devtools — a row that also carries an object argument renders a devtools-specific (and truncated) preview into whatever gets copied. The live objects are kept beside the lines (`[campaigner] entity-batch detail …`, a DISTINCT tag so the line to copy cannot be confused with the object to expand), and the batch's own kind rides the record as `batchKind` because `kind` means the FAILURE's class there — two facts may not share one key (measured: they silently overwrote each other). A value `JSON.stringify` cannot serialize (a cycle, a `BigInt`) does not swallow the line and does not break the batch — the line says in words that it could not be serialized.

The toast states the classes SEPARATELY (`did not generate — 2 refused as cast-creature collisions (a designed stop, not a generator failure), 1 cancelled or interrupted while they were running (… run the batch again), 1 failed to generate — see the Runs tab`) so the owner's real question — *is my generator broken?* — is answered from the toast alone, while a batch of plain run failures keeps the old sentence **byte for byte**. `interrupted` is deliberately NOT called `cancelled`: a run whose STATUS is `cancelled` is the withdrawn owner-stop this batch keeps silent (row 117), while an interruption is `status: 'failed'` with `failureKind: 'cancelled'` (`db/runRepo.failRunningRuns`) — the same word for two different facts is how the count became a lie in the first place. Nothing about what COUNTS as a success or a failure changed: a designed CAST stays a success (pinned), a withdrawn run stays silent (pinned) | copy + both surfaces: `src/features/modules/entity-batch-report.ts` (NEW — the ONE composer of the count sentence, the console payload and the toast); the records: `src/features/modules/entity-batch.ts` (`EntityBatchFailure`, the three push sites); the callers: `src/features/modules/entity-panel.tsx`, `src/features/modules/post-generation.ts`; tests: `tests/features/entity-batch-failure-report.test.ts` (12 pins incl. the payload shape per path and the `duration: Infinity` measured through the REAL `lib/toast`), `tests/features/entity-batch-failure-report-scan.test.ts` (5 scan pins), `tests/features/entity-batch-fixed-cast.test.ts` (the record per path), `tests/features/entity-panel.test.tsx` (the summary copy, now asserted on `toastErrorPersistent`), `tests/features/creature-row-resolution.test.tsx` (a cast reports NOTHING), `tests/setup.ts` (one documented ALLOWED_NOISE entry, scoped to that file) | a longer transient toast (the owner's literal mechanism): still unselectable, still gone in four seconds, and still unable to carry a stack; a console-only entry (forbidden by rule 2); two sentences at two call sites (the drift this row exists to end); reporting a designed refusal or a reload as a generator failure (row 117's rule, third surface) | active |

| 132 | **A test that failed ONCE in 3417 runs was racing a field its barrier did not cover: the `module:<title>` tag is stamped only after the batch's whole target POOL drains, while the barrier was satisfied by the per-target RENAME — so the tag assertion moved INSIDE the wait, and `src/` was deliberately NOT touched** (read-only probe finding; every `file:line` re-derived at the base commit `0425eb4`). **The mechanism, at the co-ordinates rather than as a story.** In `features/modules/entity-batch.ts` the per-target leg waits for its run and then renames the row to the exact entity name (`:639` calls `alignEntityName`, `:94`, whose `artifactRepo.updateArtifact` is `:111`) — rev 2, at which the row is observable with the right `name`, `moduleId`, no `creatureRef` and the right `summary`. The `module:<title>` compatibility tag is stamped only AFTER `mapConcurrency`/`mapWithConcurrency` has drained the WHOLE pool, in the post-batch loop at `:691-703` (`getArtifact` `:696`, the idempotence test `:697`, `stampModuleOwnership` `:698`) — rev 3. The test's barrier (`tests/features/creature-row-resolution.test.tsx:465-471` at the base commit) asserted only that ONE row of that name exists, so rev 2 satisfied it and the tag assertion that followed (`:477`) raced rev 3. Measured gap rev2→rev3 unloaded: **~2 ms against `waitFor`'s 50 ms poll interval**, which is why it read as ~1 failure in 3417 under load and 0 in the probe's 8 unloaded runs (and 0 in 2 more taken here). **THE DELAY EXPERIMENT, BOTH DIRECTIONS** (the `89e5d71` method — a race is proved by DELAYING ITS CAUSE, never by repetition or by loading the machine; one suite at a time at `CAMPAIGNER_TEST_WORKERS=2`, raw output written to a file and kept). Injected immediately before `const artifact = await artifactRepo.getArtifact(artifactId);` in the post-batch stamp loop, the line `await new Promise((r) => setTimeout(r, 300));`: **UNFIXED + delay → RED 4/4**, all four `AssertionError: expected [ 'undead' ] to include 'module:Ember Crypt'` at `:477:22`; **FIXED + the SAME delay still injected → GREEN 3/3** (7/7), which is the proof the barrier now covers rev 3 rather than merely ending later; **injection REMOVED → GREEN 3/3**, restored by `git checkout --` with `git hash-object` `645bb743d67bc1417feba04e1961e1bffbe0cb02` identical before and after (`git diff` empty for `src/`); **control, unfixed WITHOUT the injection → GREEN 2/2**, i.e. the race is latent, which is why only an injection could see it. The delay is 300 ms and NOT the probe's 3000 ms on purpose: a delay longer than `waitFor`'s 1000 ms default timeout reds the test by TIMEOUT, proving nothing about where the barrier stops. **THE CURE CHOSEN, AND THE ALTERNATIVE DECLINED.** The tag assertion moved INSIDE the barrier (`:482`) — the pattern `tests/features/entity-panel.test.tsx:765-771` already uses on this same batch — because a barrier must cover the FIELD being asserted, not merely the row's existence; it is a direct read of the asserted field and needs no new infrastructure, and the assertion stays at `:490` as the pin. Waiting on the batch's completion signal instead was considered and declined: it would pin the assertion to a UI proxy for "the pool drained" (the panel's own label), a SECOND fact that can drift from the stamp loop. `flushAsyncUpdates` (`:509`) and `actDrained` are not part of the cure — they drain pending React updates, and no draining makes the stamp happen sooner. **THE APP-SIDE STATE IS BENIGN AND MUST NOT BE "FIXED"** (docs/18 §4): the tag's only readers are the batch's own idempotence check (`:697`) and the cast re-stamp (`db/creatureRepo.ts:580`), no UI surface filters on it, and module ownership is set from birth via `placementModuleId` (`:587-589`) — so the row is a normal module-owned row that merely lacks the tag for a few milliseconds. Stamping earlier would buy an extra revision (or a differently-timed write) to satisfy a test; if that ordering ever matters to a product surface it is a design question with its own row. **THE SECOND INSTANCE IN THE SAME FILE WAS CONFIRMED SAFE AND LEFT UNTOUCHED**, measured rather than assumed: `db/creatureRepo.castCreatureAsNpc` writes `tags: [moduleTag]` (`:595`), `moduleId` (`:596`) and `data.creatureRef` (`:605`) in ONE `createArtifact` call — one transaction — so for the cast test (`:571-578`) the row's existence and the `creatureRef` it asserts (`:581`) become observable together, and the cast arm runs before any run is started (`entity-batch.ts:522-543`); that pin stayed GREEN in all four RED runs. **THE FORENSICS LESSON, which cost a separate investigation to recover.** The original sighting's evidence was destroyed by piping the gate through `tail -10`, and the surviving tail MISATTRIBUTED the failure: **a gate tail that ends on a code-frame line N can be an N−2 failure, because vitest prints context lines** — a real `:479` failure prints its caret under 479 and continues to `:481`, while the observed tail ENDED at `:479`, and this run's `:477` failure reproduces exactly that shape (frame ends at `479|`, caret under `477|`, nothing beyond). The one-line rule that comes out of it is now in `AGENTS.md` §Workflow: write the gate's raw output to a file and keep it; never pipe it through `tail`/`head`. **WHAT THIS SLICE DID NOT DO.** It did not touch the other 126 "wait barrier → content assert" sites the probe surveyed (their barrier is the run's terminal status or batch completion, which does cover their content); it did not touch `src/**` at all (the delay above is the only edit that ever existed there and it is gone, hash-verified); and it added no test and no test infrastructure, so the gate count is unchanged. Every pre-existing pin passes unchanged, including the cast pin in the same file. **UNPROVEN.** (1) The ~1/3417 natural incidence is the probe's single observation under concurrent-writer load, not a rate this slice re-measured — this repo no longer creates that condition (AGENTS §Host hygiene), so it is quoted as the probe's measurement and not as mine. (2) The fix is proved against a DELAYED stamp, never against the natural 2 ms window, which no unloaded run can force. (3) Whether the app SHOULD stamp at rev 2 is not answered here — it is explicitly out of scope by owner decision, and the reason the intermediate state is harmless is documented rather than argued away. | `tests/features/creature-row-resolution.test.tsx` (the barrier's comment + the tag assertion INSIDE the wait, `:462-484`, 13 added lines); docs/08-TESTING.md §A barrier must cover the FIELD it asserts; docs/18 §4; this row. **NOT touched, byte-identical:** every file under `src/` (`src/features/modules/entity-batch.ts` `645bb743d67bc1417feba04e1961e1bffbe0cb02`, `src/db/creatureRepo.ts`, `tests/helpers/flush.ts`), and every other test file | A one-in-3417 flake is not noise: it was a test asserting a field its barrier never covered, so it would have failed on whoever's gate happened to be running and been investigated from scratch — as it already was once, and the evidence of that investigation was destroyed by a `tail`. The cure is two lines and covers exactly the field asserted; the product is untouched because the state between the two revisions is harmless by measurement, not by assumption, and "fixing" it would trade a test-visible timing for a real write-behaviour change. The forensics lesson is recorded with the doc because the misattribution, not the failure, is what cost the separate investigation. | active |

Where those paragraphs exist that is right and stays byte-identical; where they do not, the row's whole text was the entity's NAME and the details surface showed a portrait, an empty description and an "Add stat block" button. **WHAT THE OWNER'S ROW ACTUALLY LACKED, PIXEL-MEASURED, not inferred:** an `ArtifactEditor` render of a row born exactly that way and dumped (`tests/features/zz-cast-surface-measure.test.tsx`, scratch, deleted; raw output in the slice's scratch dir) reads `NPC details · Appearance · Personality · Stat block · Add stat block · …` with NO AC, NO HP, NO attack line anywhere in the DOM — so his "no stat block" is CONFIRMED and it is a SECOND defect with its own cause: `db/creatureRepo.resolveDerivedNpcStats` exists so a cast row's numbers are DERIVED at read time, and it has **zero callers in `src/`** (only its own definition at `:176` and `tests/db/creatureRepo.test.ts:135,141`) — the encounter roster derives through `domain/encounterResolve` (`:214`), the row's own details surface never asks. A cast row therefore had no rendered numbers anywhere the owner looked, and rendering them was NOT fixed by THIS slice (recorded there as a gotcha, docs/18 §4) — **AMENDED by row 134**, which renders them read-only and labelled through ONE component; that sentence is the record of what row 133 left open and nothing else in this row changes. **THE RULE CHOSEN FOR "does the text describe her?", and why.** ONE seam, `describesEntity(text, name)` (`src/lib/wikilinks.ts:347`, floor `ENTITY_DESCRIPTION_FLOOR = 40` at `:323`): take the entity's own name out of the passage (case-insensitively, literally, everywhere it occurs) and count what is LEFT in NON-WHITESPACE characters. It answers "does this text say something about them" rather than scoring prose quality — a scorer over-rejects short true statements and cannot be pinned at a boundary, while 40 characters is roughly one clause that is about something other than the name itself. The order of the two asks is the DESIGN: module paragraphs FIRST (`entity-batch.ts:642` — when the module describes her, her description IS the module's, no run is spent and invented prose never replaces it), the row's own body second (`:654` — a row that already carries a description is never written over, which is the cast's own documented promise and what makes a retry-after-failure safe). **THE WRITE IS NOT A NEW MECHANISM**: the run TARGETS the cast row (`targetArtifactId`, no placement — `:655-665`), so the ratified Aunt Agatha refill does the work end to end — `runStatblock` finishes the statblock step `'skipped'` with its reason BEFORE the model call (docs/11 §A, pinned here by the chat-call count and the step's own status), `mergeRefillData` keeps the citation byte-identical and `statBlock` null, and the refused `creatureRef`+`statBlock` pair stays unconstructible (an injection that disables the step-off builds exactly that pair, and the run dies on the refusal instead). The row stays in `cast`, never in `generated` (the artifact is the cast's; the run only wrote prose into it) — with the ONE new documented overlap: a description run that does not complete appears in `cast` AND `failed` (`:673-689`), because the artifact exists and the prose does not. Stats are never authored, the citation is never dropped, the cast is never skipped: three ratified rules, none of them made reachable. Trash mobs cited only inside an encounter: untouched, no behaviour change. **THE FOLD, and why a shape pin exists.** Two rules that used to be spelled out twice are now ONE: the failure mapping `runFailureRecord` (`:266`, called from the description arm `:681` and the creation arm `:775`; the count of `runNotCompletedReason(` in this file stays 1, which `tests/llm/runNotCompletedReason.test.ts`'s scan already asserts) and the withdrawal `withdrawPool` (`:506`, both arms) — both folds change no behaviour, so the SHAPE is pinned: `tests/features/entity-batch-cast-description.test.ts:484` asserts the floor and the name-stripping exist in `lib/wikilinks.ts` ALONE and that the batch asks `describesEntity(` exactly twice. **INJECTIONS, each applied to the line that executes (printed back) and restored byte-identically (`git hash-object` before/after; note that `git checkout --` restores HEAD, not the pre-injection tree, so the pre-injection content was kept as an out-of-tree copy after that cost one reconstruction):** I1 authoring arm deleted → RED 6 (both thin end-to-end pins, the mocked "aims the run AT THE CAST ROW" and "its OWN sentence" pins, the failure-funnel pin, the shape scan) and GREEN on the described side, the withdrawal pin, and every pre-existing cast pin — the reason the withdrawal pin is green is that no run exists to be stopped; I2 the module-paragraph ask deleted → RED 2 (the shape scan, and "the module's OWN prose decides": a run IS then spent and the module's prose WOULD be replaced), while the created-row pins stayed green because a created row's body IS the module paragraph — the row-body ask shadows it there, and that shadowing is exactly why the reused-row pin was added; | 134 | **A cited npc's BORROWED numbers are now RENDERED — read-only, labelled from the library, drawn by ONE component over the ONE derivation — and a cited row is no longer offered the impossible "Add stat block" button** (owner-reported; this is the STATS half of docs/17 row 133, whose TEXT half landed at `ef8744d`). **The owner's report, verbatim in substance:** *"When the module creates an NPC inside the TEXT … this NPC absolutely needs a description, even if its just a zombie. So, she will have zombie stats but with prose."*, docs/18:736). **THE MECHANISM, MEASURED at base `ef8744d`.** A cast creature npc carries `data.creatureRef` with `statBlock: null`; its numbers are DERIVED at read time and never stored. Two facts made them invisible: (1) `db/creatureRepo.resolveDerivedNpcStats` — the repo-wired read built for exactly that — had **ZERO callers in `src/`** (its only matches were its own definition and `tests/db/creatureRepo.test.ts:135,141`), because the encounter roster derives through `domain/encounterResolve` instead; (2) `NpcForm` (`features/campaign/components/kind-forms.tsx:126-169`) rendered `StatBlockCard` only when `data.statBlock !== null` and otherwise an **"Add stat block"** button — which on a cited row is a DEAD END BY DESIGN: `runStatblock` finishes the step `'skipped'` with a reason naming the citation and the library creature BEFORE any model call (docs/11 §A cited row's REFILL) and `npcDataSchema` refuses the pair by name, so the owner saw no numbers AND an affordance that could never produce any. **THE SEAM FOUND, AND WHY IT WAS THE ONE FOLLOWED (AGENTS rule 4).** A cited creature's numbers were ALREADY rendered on two other surfaces, both through `domain/encounterResolve`: the encounter panel (`MonsterStatblocksPanel` → `resolveMonsterEntries` → `resolveMonsterEntry`'s `npc-ref` arm, `:206-216` at the base) and the battle board (`BattleSurface`'s `tokenCreature`, `db/creatureRepo.ts:363`). The `npc-ref` arm and the zero-caller `resolveDerivedNpcStats` were TWO SPELLINGS OF ONE RULE — both did `resolveCreatureCitation(...)` then wrapped a non-null block in `derivedStatOrigin(npc.name, creature.origin)` — so the rule was FOLDED INTO THE DOMAIN one and the repo read became its thin wrapper: `domain/encounterResolve.resolveDerivedNpcStats(npcName, citation, lookups)` (`:208`) is now called by the encounter `npc-ref` arm (`:255`) and by `db/creatureRepo.resolveDerivedNpcStats` (`:181`, delegating at `:185`), so a row's details panel, an encounter listing that row and a battle token cannot disagree. All four now ASSERT the flag, which is a tightening rather than a loosening: they still pin the duration, the copy and (in the global-errors case) the ABSENCE of a description. The two `expect.objectContaining({ duration: Infinity, description })` pins in the same `globalErrors` file passed untouched and were left alone. The first gate run on this tree is what found them — `Test Files 2 failed \| 300 passed (302)`, `Tests 3 failed \| 3453 passed (3456)`, every failure the same one-key diff — and the raw output is kept in the slice's scratch. `tests/setup.ts` gained a jsdom `Element.prototype.setPointerCapture` no-op, because sonner's swipe handler calls it on pointerdown BEFORE it checks whether the target is a button — so ANY click on a toast (the closer included) threw three unhandled errors and exited non-zero while every assertion passed: a harness trap, not a failure. **INJECTIONS at the lines that execute** (printed back with `grep -n`, `git diff --stat` checked BEFORE each run, one suite at a time at `CAMPAIGNER_TEST_WORKERS=2`, raw output kept in the slice's scratch, each file restored byte-identically from an OUT-OF-TREE copy and re-verified with `git hash-object` — `toast.ts` `dd621acf345a110e23f136d4bf92ddf430417b7a`, `sonner.tsx` `9dd2975fe4d9f5a6195f6abfd84aafd3cc5e2a1c`, both matching after restore): I1 `closeButton: true` → `false` on BOTH branches (`toast.ts:108,110`) → **RED 5** (all three new persistent pins — every one failing with `Unable to find an accessible element with the role "button" and name /^close toast$/i` — plus both option-object pins) while **the FIVE transient pins stayed GREEN**, the measured proof the fix did not travel; I2 **the DECLINED design injected instead** (a global `closeButton` on the Sonner element, `components/ui/sonner.tsx:22`) → **RED 1, exactly the new transient pin** (`a 4-second toastError carries no close control`) with every persistent pin **GREEN** — the global flag fixes the owner's bug too, and this is the pin that says why it is still the wrong design. Results including the greens: docs/08-TESTING.md §A persistent notice carries a real dismiss control. **UNPROVEN.** No real-browser sighting — every pin renders through jsdom, so "the owner now finds the closer" is read off the accessibility tree and sonner's own shipped CSS, not observed; and the double-X judgement (decorative icon beside a real X closer) is a design call from those stylesheet measurements, not a measured absence of confusion — if he mis-clicks the icon again, the next smallest remedy is the labelled "Dismiss" action this row declines today. | `src/lib/toast.ts` (`toastErrorPersistent`'s two branches `:107-111` and its doc comment); `tests/lib/toast-persistent-dismiss.test.tsx` (NEW, 4 pins); `tests/lib/toast.test.ts` (2 option-object pins gain the flag, 1 pre-existing pin named in this row); `tests/setup.ts` (the jsdom pointer-capture no-op); docs/05-UI §Error surfaces; docs/18 §2.3 + §4; docs/08 §A persistent notice carries a real dismiss control; this row | A notice the user cannot remove is not "persistent", it is a wedged screen — and the report names the cause exactly: the only X-shaped thing on screen was an ICON. The smallest change reaching the same intent is sonner's own per-toast flag at the one seam that raises these notices, leaving every transient toast exactly as it was. | active |

## Owner-removed

| # | Decision | Where it lives | Rationale | Status |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 26 | **Player portraits feature removed entirely** (owner): personas work without them — no persona offers a portrait extra (the surface is image / statBlock / mobPortraits / battlemap only), nothing generates portraits for players or PCs, and seedFighter tokens keep the deterministic initials fallback. The image pipeline serves artifact covers and mob portraits only. | `src/llm/personas/extras.ts:24`; docs/11 D5 (initials fallback) | Owner call: mobs needed identity art; players never did. | active |
| 27 | **Persona-panel module select + extras**: one-off module choice, extras remembered — the same machinery as 12 (vetoing 12 covers this row). | `src/features/campaign/components/persona-panel.tsx:66`; bba1c3b | See 12. | active |
| 39 | **The encounter-map verify step is removed entirely** (owner, 2026-09-08): "Nope. Stop the verification altogether. Let the user be the judge with a regenerate option. No need to waste model calls here. Things do not need to be verified in a brittle way. Just have a way to easily regenerate." The Cartographer pipeline is `brief→layout→schematic→stylize→pick→finalize` — no VLM call, no grading, no verdict, no advisory; the human picks the candidate and **"Regenerate candidates"** (re-runs the stylize step only, layout/keys untouched) is the correction path. Deleted with zero remaining consumers: `src/llm/encounterVision.ts` (coarse-grid contract, `arena-verdict`, threshold logic), `runEngine.runEncounterVerify`, `visionRepairModel`, `listVisionChatModels`, the `encounterVerifyModel` setting and its UI row, and the pick UI's drift overlay. The 5a8f8f2/42db-era verify machinery is superseded-then-deleted. Old run rows carrying `verify` steps heal at the run-row parse boundary (`normalizeLegacyRunSteps` drops the step and re-indexes) so legacy runs still render and resume from pick coherently. | docs/11 D14 + §No verify step; `src/llm/runEngine.ts` (`regenerateEncounterCandidates`); `src/domain/run.ts` (`normalizeLegacyRunSteps`) | Verification was spending model calls on brittle self-grading (7/7 single-arena auto runs died at the threshold before 5a8f8f2); the pick pause was always the real judge. | active |

## How to veto

Each item above is independently reversible — this ledger records standing
defaults, not load-bearing foundations. To veto, say the id ("veto 17") and
the behavior changes in the next slice; the row's status is then updated
here. Notes:

- Vetoing is per item; nothing else moves. Where two rows share machinery,
  the row says so (27 → 12).
- A veto that touches stored shapes (e.g. 16's `pdfFiles`, 13's `preset`,
  2's `everLive`) gets its migration cost named in the follow-up — reversal
  is a designed change, never a silent drop of data already on disk.
- This doc is a review surface, not the binding spec: the referenced
  doc/section remains the source of truth. If a row and its reference
  disagree, the reference wins and the row is rot — fix the row.
